Build Seekr from Scratch

A step-by-step guide to building a real search engine for developer docs and engineering blogs: a polite crawler, keyword and semantic search, a true relevance score, a REST API and a clean web UI. Every line of code is here, in order.

How to use this guide. Do the chapters in order. Each step is one action: run a command, or create a file and type the code exactly as shown. Tick the checkbox when a step is done; your progress is saved in this browser. Every chapter ends with a checkpoint so you know it works before moving on.

The map

A search engine has two halves. The offline pipeline collects pages from the web and builds indexes ahead of time. The query path runs every time someone searches: it finds candidates fast, then scores them carefully.

Offline pipeline (runs ahead of time)
seeds.txtch 1
→
Crawlerch 2 · robots.txt, rate limits
→
SQLitech 2 · frontier · pages · chunks
→
Extractorch 3 · clean text, chunks
→
FTS5 BM25 indexch 4 · keywords
Embeddings matrixch 5 · meaning · on the GPU
PageRankch 6 · authority
Query path (runs on every search)
Query"rust ownership"
→
Hybrid retrievalch 7 · BM25 + vectors, RRF
→
Relevance rerankerch 7 · cross-encoder on the GPU, 0–1
→
Final scorech 7 · relevance + PageRank + exact match
→
FastAPI REST APIch 8 · API keys, rate limits
→
Web UIch 9 · search page
External appscurl, your code
built so far

Chapter 0 · Prerequisites ready

Install the tools and create an empty project folder.

Seekr is written in Python because it has excellent libraries for every part of a search engine: HTTP, text extraction, embeddings and web APIs. You need Python 3.11 or newer.

  1. Install Homebrew (the Mac package manager), if you don't have it
    /bin/bash -c "$(curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Homebrew/install/HEAD/install.sh)"
    Check with brew --version. If it prints a version, skip this step.
  2. Install Python and git
    brew install python@3.12 git
  3. Check your Python version
    python3 --version
    You should see Python 3.11 or higher. If it shows something older, use python3.12 instead of python3 in the next steps.
  4. (Optional) Install Docker, only needed in Chapter 10
    brew install --cask docker
  5. Create the project folder and move into it
    mkdir -p ~/Desktop/Search_engine && cd ~/Desktop/Search_engine
  6. Create the empty folders
    mkdir -p seekr tests tools data
    seekr/ holds the engine's code, tests/ the automated tests, tools/ helper scripts, and data/ the database we'll create later.
  7. Create and activate a virtual environment
    python3 -m venv .venv && source .venv/bin/activate
    A virtual environment is a private copy of Python just for this project, so the packages you install don't clash with other projects. After activating, your prompt starts with (.venv). Every time you open a new terminal, run cd ~/Desktop/Search_engine && source .venv/bin/activate again.

Checkpoint

which python && ls

You should see a path ending in Search_engine/.venv/bin/python, and the folders data seekr tests tools.

zsh: command not found: brew
Homebrew installed but isn't on your PATH. Run the two echo ... >> ~/.zprofile lines the installer printed at the end, then open a new terminal.
which python shows /usr/bin/python
The virtual environment isn't active. Run source .venv/bin/activate.

Chapter 1 · Project skeleton ready

A Python package, a settings file, a command-line tool and the first tests.

What we're building: the empty frame every later chapter plugs into. By the end, python -m seekr stats runs and the tests pass. There's no search yet, but every piece has a home.

Why these seed sites? A crawler needs starting points, called seeds. We're building a niche engine for developer docs and engineering blogs, because you can judge result quality yourself, these sites have clean HTML and many internal links, and their robots.txt files allow polite crawlers. We checked all 15: every one allows crawling. eli.thegreenplace.net has no robots.txt at all, which by convention means "everything allowed". A small, high-quality niche beats a big, spammy crawl.

  1. Create the seed list
    seeds.txt
    https://docs.python.org/3/
https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web
https://fastapi.tiangolo.com/
https://www.sqlite.org/docs.html
https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/stable/
https://go.dev/doc/
https://doc.rust-lang.org/book/
https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/
https://jvns.ca/
https://martinfowler.com/
https://simonwillison.net/
https://danluu.com/
https://paulgraham.com/articles.html
https://blog.codinghorror.com/
https://eli.thegreenplace.net/
    One URL per line. The crawler (Chapter 2) starts here and only follows links that stay on these sites. To change the niche later, change this file.
  2. Create the requirements file
    requirements.txt
    pytest==8.3.4
    Lists the Python packages the project needs, pinned to exact versions so everyone gets the same thing. We'll add one line per chapter. For now it's only pytest, the test runner.
  3. Install the requirements
    pip install -r requirements.txt
  4. Tell git which files to ignore
    .gitignore
    .venv/
data/
__pycache__/
.pytest_cache/
    The virtual environment and the database are big, machine-specific and rebuildable, so they don't belong in version control.
  5. Make seekr/ a Python package
    seekr/__init__.py
    """Seekr: a small, open-source search engine."""
    A folder with an __init__.py file is a package: other code can write from seekr import config. The file can be empty; ours just holds a one-line description (a docstring).
  6. Create the settings file
    seekr/config.py
    """All settings in one place. Override any of them with an environment variable."""
import os
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent

DB_PATH = Path(os.environ.get("SEEKR_DB", ROOT / "data" / "seekr.db"))
SEEDS_FILE = Path(os.environ.get("SEEKR_SEEDS", ROOT / "seeds.txt"))
USER_AGENT = os.environ.get("SEEKR_USER_AGENT", "SeekrBot/0.1")
HOST = os.environ.get("SEEKR_HOST", "127.0.0.1")
PORT = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_PORT", "8000"))


def read_seeds(path=None):
    """Return the seed URLs, skipping blank lines and # comments."""
    lines = Path(path or SEEDS_FILE).read_text().splitlines()
    return [line.strip() for line in lines if line.strip() and not line.startswith("#")]
    • Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent: __file__ is this file's path. Going up two levels (seekr/config.py → seekr/ → project root) gives us ROOT, so paths work no matter which folder you run from.
    • os.environ.get("SEEKR_DB", default) reads an environment variable if it's set and otherwise uses the default. You can change any setting without editing code, e.g. SEEKR_PORT=9000 python -m seekr serve.
    • read_seeds uses a list comprehension, [x for x in items if condition], to keep only non-blank lines that aren't # comments.
  7. Create the command-line tool
    seekr/__main__.py
    """Command-line entry point: python -m seekr <command>."""
import argparse

from seekr import config


def cmd_stats(args):
    seeds = config.read_seeds()
    db_state = "exists" if config.DB_PATH.exists() else "not created yet"
    print(f"seeds:    {len(seeds)}")
    print(f"database: {config.DB_PATH} ({db_state})")


def not_built_yet(args):
    print(f"'{args.command}' is not built yet - see guide.html for the next chapter.")


COMMANDS = {
    "crawl": not_built_yet,
    "index": not_built_yet,
    "rank": not_built_yet,
    "serve": not_built_yet,
    "stats": cmd_stats,
}


def main(argv=None):
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog="seekr", description="Seekr search engine")
    sub = parser.add_subparsers(dest="command", required=True)
    for name in COMMANDS:
        sub.add_parser(name)
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
    COMMANDS[args.command](args)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
    • A package's __main__.py runs when you type python -m seekr. That's how we get a CLI without installing anything.
    • argparse (standard library) parses the command line. add_subparsers gives us git-style subcommands: python -m seekr crawl, python -m seekr stats.
    • COMMANDS is a dictionary from a command name to the function that handles it. Python functions are values, so we can store them in a dict and call COMMANDS[args.command](args). Later chapters just swap not_built_yet for the real function.
    • main(argv=None) takes an optional argument list so tests can call main(["stats"]) directly. When it's None, argparse reads the real command line.
    • if __name__ == "__main__": runs main() only when the file is executed, not when it's imported.
  8. Write the first tests
    tests/test_cli.py
    import pytest

from seekr import config
from seekr.__main__ import main


def test_read_seeds_skips_blanks_and_comments(tmp_path):
    seeds = tmp_path / "seeds.txt"
    seeds.write_text("# my seeds\nhttps://a.com/\n\n  https://b.com/  \n")
    assert config.read_seeds(seeds) == ["https://a.com/", "https://b.com/"]


def test_project_has_fifteen_seeds():
    assert len(config.read_seeds()) == 15


def test_stats_prints_seed_count(capsys):
    main(["stats"])
    assert "seeds:    15" in capsys.readouterr().out


def test_unknown_command_fails():
    with pytest.raises(SystemExit):
        main(["fly"])
    • pytest finds files named test_*.py and runs every function named test_*. A plain assert is all you need.
    • tmp_path and capsys are fixtures: pytest sees the parameter name and passes in a ready-made object. tmp_path is a fresh temporary folder; capsys captures what the code prints.
    • pytest.raises(SystemExit) checks that argparse refuses an unknown command (argparse exits the program on bad input).
  9. Run the CLI
    python -m seekr stats
    seeds:    15
database: /Users/you/Desktop/Search_engine/data/seekr.db (not created yet)

Chapter 2 · Crawler ready

A polite, resumable crawler that downloads pages from our seed sites into SQLite.

What we're building: a program that starts at the seed URLs, downloads each page, finds its links and adds them to a to-do list called the frontier. It repeats until it hits a page limit. Every page lands in a SQLite database for the next chapters to work on.

Why "polite" matters: a crawler runs on other people's servers. Ours follows the three rules every good crawler follows:

  • robots.txt: every site can publish /robots.txt listing paths bots must not visit (and sometimes a Crawl-delay). We obey it.
  • Rate limit: at most one request per second to the same host, so we never hammer a server.
  • Identify yourself: we send the User-Agent SeekrBot/0.1, so site owners can see who we are.

How it stays fast anyway: each host is crawled one request at a time, but all 15 hosts are crawled at the same time. That's what asyncio gives us: while we wait for docs.python.org to answer, we can be talking to go.dev.

Why "resumable": the frontier lives in SQLite, not in memory. Press Ctrl+C halfway and run crawl again, and it picks up where it stopped.

  1. Add httpx to the requirements
    requirements.txt
    pytest==8.3.4
httpx==0.28.1
    httpx is a modern HTTP client. Unlike the older requests, it supports async, which lets us download from many sites at once.
  2. Install it
    pip install -r requirements.txt
  3. Add the crawler settings to seekr/config.py (whole updated file)
    seekr/config.py
    """All settings in one place. Override any of them with an environment variable."""
import os
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent

DB_PATH = Path(os.environ.get("SEEKR_DB", ROOT / "data" / "seekr.db"))
SEEDS_FILE = Path(os.environ.get("SEEKR_SEEDS", ROOT / "seeds.txt"))
USER_AGENT = os.environ.get("SEEKR_USER_AGENT", "SeekrBot/0.1")
HOST = os.environ.get("SEEKR_HOST", "127.0.0.1")
PORT = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_PORT", "8000"))

# Crawler
MAX_DEPTH = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_MAX_DEPTH", "3"))
MAX_PAGES_PER_DOMAIN = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_MAX_PAGES_PER_DOMAIN", "300"))
CRAWL_DELAY = float(os.environ.get("SEEKR_CRAWL_DELAY", "1.0"))  # seconds between requests to one host
REQUEST_TIMEOUT = float(os.environ.get("SEEKR_REQUEST_TIMEOUT", "15"))
MAX_PAGE_BYTES = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_MAX_PAGE_BYTES", "2000000"))
RETRIES = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_RETRIES", "2"))


def read_seeds(path=None):
    """Return the seed URLs, skipping blank lines and # comments."""
    lines = Path(path or SEEDS_FILE).read_text().splitlines()
    return [line.strip() for line in lines if line.strip() and not line.startswith("#")]
    Six new settings under # Crawler. int(...) and float(...) are needed because environment variables are always strings. For a quick experiment, try SEEKR_MAX_PAGES_PER_DOMAIN=20 python -m seekr crawl.
  4. Create the database module
    seekr/db.py
    """SQLite storage: one file on disk, a few small tables."""
import json
import sqlite3
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from pathlib import Path

from seekr import config

SCHEMA = """
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS frontier (
    url    TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    host   TEXT NOT NULL,
    depth  INTEGER NOT NULL,
    status TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'queued'  -- queued, done, failed, error or blocked
);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS frontier_next ON frontier (host, status, depth);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS pages (
    url        TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    final_url  TEXT,
    status     INTEGER,
    title      TEXT,
    html       TEXT,
    outlinks   TEXT,  -- JSON list of absolute URLs
    fetched_at TEXT
);
"""


def connect(path=None):
    path = Path(path or config.DB_PATH)
    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    conn = sqlite3.connect(path)
    conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
    conn.executescript(SCHEMA)
    return conn


def enqueue(conn, urls, depth):
    rows = [(url, url.split("/")[2], depth) for url in urls]
    conn.executemany("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO frontier (url, host, depth) VALUES (?, ?, ?)", rows)


def next_url(conn, host):
    return conn.execute(
        "SELECT url, depth FROM frontier WHERE host = ? AND status = 'queued' ORDER BY depth LIMIT 1",
        (host,),
    ).fetchone()


def mark(conn, url, status):
    conn.execute("UPDATE frontier SET status = ? WHERE url = ?", (status, url))


def redirect_targets(conn, urls):
    """Where these URLs ended up after redirects, for the ones that were redirected."""
    marks = ",".join("?" * len(urls))
    sql = f"SELECT final_url FROM pages WHERE url IN ({marks}) AND final_url != url"
    return [row["final_url"] for row in conn.execute(sql, urls)]


def requeue_errors(conn):
    conn.execute("UPDATE frontier SET status = 'queued' WHERE status = 'error'")


def count_done(conn, host):
    sql = "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM frontier WHERE host = ? AND status = 'done'"
    return conn.execute(sql, (host,)).fetchone()[0]


def save_page(conn, url, final_url, status, title, html, outlinks):
    fetched_at = datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
    conn.execute(
        "INSERT OR REPLACE INTO pages VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)",
        (url, final_url, status, title, html, json.dumps(outlinks), fetched_at),
    )


def host_stats(conn):
    return conn.execute(
        """SELECT host,
                  SUM(status = 'done')    AS done,
                  SUM(status = 'queued')  AS queued,
                  SUM(status = 'failed')  AS failed,
                  SUM(status = 'error')   AS error,
                  SUM(status = 'blocked') AS blocked
           FROM frontier GROUP BY host ORDER BY host"""
    ).fetchall()
    • SQLite is a full SQL database that lives in a single file. There's no server to install, and it's built into Python as sqlite3.
    • The frontier table is the crawler's to-do list. url TEXT PRIMARY KEY means a URL can appear only once, which is how we deduplicate. status moves from queued to done, failed or blocked.
    • The pages table stores what we downloaded. We keep the raw html so Chapter 3 can extract text without re-downloading anything.
    • CREATE INDEX makes "find the next queued URL for this host" fast, even with millions of rows.
    • INSERT OR IGNORE silently skips URLs already in the frontier. executemany runs one statement for a whole list of rows.
    • The ? placeholders let SQLite insert values safely. Never build SQL by pasting strings together (that's how SQL injection happens).
    • row_factory = sqlite3.Row lets us read columns by name: row["url"].
    • SUM(status = 'done') works because a comparison in SQLite returns 1 or 0, so summing it counts the matching rows.
    • failed means the server answered with an error (like 404), so there's no point asking again. error means the network broke (Wi-Fi drop, timeout), so requeue_errors puts those URLs back in the queue on the next run.
    • redirect_targets builds SQL with the right number of ? placeholders: ",".join("?" * 3) gives "?,?,?".
  5. Create the URL helpers
    seekr/urls.py
    """URL helpers: clean up links and decide which ones we are allowed to crawl."""
from urllib.parse import parse_qsl, urlencode, urljoin, urlsplit, urlunsplit

TRACKING_PARAMS = {"fbclid", "gclid", "ref", "ref_src"}
SKIP_EXTENSIONS = (
    ".pdf", ".zip", ".gz", ".tar", ".png", ".jpg", ".jpeg", ".gif", ".svg", ".webp",
    ".ico", ".css", ".js", ".json", ".xml", ".txt", ".mp3", ".mp4", ".epub",
)


def normalize_url(href, base=""):
    """Make a link absolute and canonical, or return None if it isn't a web link."""
    parts = urlsplit(urljoin(base, href.strip()))
    if parts.scheme not in ("http", "https") or not parts.netloc:
        return None
    query = [
        (key, value)
        for key, value in parse_qsl(parts.query)
        if not key.startswith("utm_") and key not in TRACKING_PARAMS
    ]
    return urlunsplit((parts.scheme.lower(), parts.netloc.lower(), parts.path or "/", urlencode(query), ""))


def build_scopes(seeds):
    """Each seed allows its host plus the folder it lives in, e.g. docs.python.org + /3/."""
    scopes = []
    for seed in seeds:
        parts = urlsplit(normalize_url(seed))
        folder = parts.path[: parts.path.rfind("/") + 1]
        scopes.append((parts.netloc, folder))
    return scopes


def in_scope(url, scopes):
    parts = urlsplit(url)
    if parts.path.lower().endswith(SKIP_EXTENSIONS):
        return False
    return any(parts.netloc == host and parts.path.startswith(folder) for host, folder in scopes)
    • Normalization turns the many spellings of a URL into one. urljoin makes relative links absolute (asyncio.html on /3/library/ becomes /3/library/asyncio.html). We lowercase the host, drop the #fragment (same page), and strip tracking parameters like utm_source. Without this we'd download the same page many times.
    • Scope keeps the crawl inside our niche. Each seed allows its host plus the folder it lives in: https://docs.python.org/3/ allows /3/... but not the old /2/ docs.
    • SKIP_EXTENSIONS avoids downloading PDFs, images and code files we can't index. str.endswith accepts a tuple, so it checks all of them at once.
  6. Test the URL helpers
    tests/test_urls.py
    from seekr.urls import build_scopes, in_scope, normalize_url


def test_normalize_makes_links_absolute_and_clean():
    base = "https://Docs.Python.org/3/library/"
    assert normalize_url("asyncio.html#top", base) == "https://docs.python.org/3/library/asyncio.html"
    assert normalize_url("https://a.com?utm_source=x&page=2") == "https://a.com/?page=2"


def test_normalize_rejects_non_web_links():
    assert normalize_url("mailto:me@example.com") is None
    assert normalize_url("javascript:void(0)") is None


def test_scopes_keep_the_seed_folder():
    scopes = build_scopes(["https://docs.python.org/3/", "https://paulgraham.com/articles.html"])
    assert scopes == [("docs.python.org", "/3/"), ("paulgraham.com", "/")]
    assert in_scope("https://docs.python.org/3/library/os.html", scopes)
    assert not in_scope("https://docs.python.org/2/library/os.html", scopes)
    assert not in_scope("https://evil.com/3/", scopes)
    assert not in_scope("https://paulgraham.com/essay.pdf", scopes)
    python -m pytest -q tests/test_urls.py
    3 passed
  7. Create the crawler
    seekr/crawler.py
    """A small, polite, resumable web crawler."""
import asyncio
from html.parser import HTMLParser
from urllib.robotparser import RobotFileParser

import httpx

from seekr import config, db
from seekr.urls import build_scopes, in_scope, normalize_url

RETRY_STATUSES = {None, 429, 500, 502, 503, 504}


class LinkParser(HTMLParser):
    """Collects the <title> text, every <a href> and every <iframe src> on a page."""

    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__()
        self.title, self.links, self.in_title = "", [], False

    def handle_starttag(self, tag, attrs):
        attrs = dict(attrs)
        if tag == "a" and attrs.get("href"):
            self.links.append(attrs["href"])
        elif tag == "iframe" and attrs.get("src"):  # some docs load their table of contents this way
            self.links.append(attrs["src"])
        elif tag == "title":
            self.in_title = True

    def handle_endtag(self, tag):
        if tag == "title":
            self.in_title = False

    def handle_data(self, data):
        if self.in_title:
            self.title += data


def parse_page(html, base_url):
    parser = LinkParser()
    parser.feed(html)
    links = {normalize_url(href, base_url) for href in parser.links}
    return parser.title.strip(), sorted(link for link in links if link)


async def load_robots(client, host):
    """Missing robots.txt (4xx) means allow all. Unreachable (5xx, network error) returns None."""
    try:
        resp = await client.get(f"https://{host}/robots.txt")
    except httpx.HTTPError:
        return None
    if resp.status_code >= 500:
        return None
    robots = RobotFileParser()
    if resp.status_code >= 400:
        robots.allow_all = True
    else:
        robots.parse(resp.text.splitlines())
    return robots


async def fetch_once(client, url):
    """Return (status, final_url, html). html is None for non-HTML or oversized pages."""
    async with client.stream("GET", url) as resp:
        final_url = str(resp.url)
        if resp.status_code != 200 or "text/html" not in resp.headers.get("content-type", ""):
            return resp.status_code, final_url, None
        body = b""
        async for chunk in resp.aiter_bytes():
            body += chunk
            if len(body) > config.MAX_PAGE_BYTES:
                return resp.status_code, final_url, None
        return resp.status_code, final_url, body.decode(resp.charset_encoding or "utf-8", "replace")


async def fetch(client, url):
    """fetch_once, retried with exponential backoff (1s, 2s, ...) on network errors, 429 and 5xx."""
    for attempt in range(config.RETRIES + 1):
        try:
            status, final_url, html = await fetch_once(client, url)
        except httpx.HTTPError:
            status, final_url, html = None, url, None
        if status not in RETRY_STATUSES or attempt == config.RETRIES:
            return status, final_url, html
        await asyncio.sleep(2**attempt)


async def crawl_host(client, conn, host, scopes):
    """Crawl one host, one request at a time, waiting between requests."""
    robots = await load_robots(client, host)
    if robots is None:  # can't read the rules, so stay away from this host until the next run
        return
    delay = max(config.CRAWL_DELAY, robots.crawl_delay(config.USER_AGENT) or 0)
    while db.count_done(conn, host) < config.MAX_PAGES_PER_DOMAIN:
        row = db.next_url(conn, host)
        if row is None:
            break
        url, depth = row["url"], row["depth"]
        if not robots.can_fetch(config.USER_AGENT, url):
            db.mark(conn, url, "blocked")
            continue
        status, final_url, html = await fetch(client, url)
        if depth == 0 and final_url != url:  # a seed redirected, e.g. /en/stable/ -> /en/6.1/
            scopes.extend(build_scopes([final_url]))
        if html:
            title, links = parse_page(html, final_url)
            db.save_page(conn, url, final_url, status, title, html, links)
            if depth < config.MAX_DEPTH:
                db.enqueue(conn, [link for link in links if in_scope(link, scopes)], depth + 1)
        if html:
            db.mark(conn, url, "done")
        else:  # no status means a network error: worth retrying on the next run
            db.mark(conn, url, "failed" if status else "error")
        conn.commit()
        await asyncio.sleep(delay)


async def crawl(seeds, conn, transport=None):
    """Crawl all seed hosts at the same time (each host politely, one request at a time)."""
    seed_urls = [normalize_url(seed) for seed in seeds]
    scopes = build_scopes(seed_urls + db.redirect_targets(conn, seed_urls))
    db.enqueue(conn, seed_urls, depth=0)
    db.requeue_errors(conn)
    conn.commit()
    hosts = sorted({host for host, _ in scopes})
    headers = {"User-Agent": config.USER_AGENT}
    async with httpx.AsyncClient(
        headers=headers, timeout=config.REQUEST_TIMEOUT, follow_redirects=True, transport=transport
    ) as client:
        await asyncio.gather(*(crawl_host(client, conn, host, scopes) for host in hosts))
    • async def and await: an async function can pause at every await (for example while waiting for a server to reply) and let other code run meanwhile. asyncio is the "event loop" that switches between these paused functions. One thread can wait on 15 websites at once.
    • asyncio.gather(...) starts one crawl_host per host and waits for all of them to finish. Inside each one, requests happen strictly one after another, with await asyncio.sleep(delay) between them. That's our per-host rate limit.
    • httpx.AsyncClient reuses connections across requests (faster and kinder to servers). follow_redirects=True follows 301/302 redirects, and str(resp.url) gives the final address.
    • client.stream(...) downloads the body in chunks, so we can stop if a page is larger than MAX_PAGE_BYTES or isn't HTML at all, without downloading the whole thing.
    • robots.txt: Python's built-in RobotFileParser reads the rules, then can_fetch(user_agent, url) answers "may I?". If robots.txt is missing (404), everything is allowed. If we can't read it (server error or no network), load_robots returns None and we skip that host for this run only. We stay away, but without marking its pages as blocked forever.
    • Retries with backoff: network hiccups and 429 Too Many Requests / 5xx errors are often temporary, so fetch tries again after 1s, then 2s. Waiting longer each time is called exponential backoff. 2**attempt means 2 to the power of attempt.
    • LinkParser subclasses the standard-library HTMLParser. It calls our methods for every opening tag, closing tag and piece of text, and we collect <a href> links and the <title>. Putting the links in a set removes duplicates.
    • We follow <iframe src> too, because some documentation sites (like the Rust book) load their table of contents in an iframe. Without it, our crawler would only see a handful of pages.
    • Redirected seeds: docs.djangoproject.com/en/stable/ redirects to /en/6.1/. If we only allowed /en/stable/, every link on the real page would be "out of scope". So when a seed redirects, its new folder joins the scopes: right away in crawl_host, and on later runs via db.redirect_targets.
    • depth counts clicks from a seed. We stop following links at MAX_DEPTH, so the crawl doesn't wander off forever.
    • conn.commit() after every page saves progress to disk. That's what makes the crawl resumable.
  8. Wire the crawl command into the CLI (whole updated file)
    seekr/__main__.py
    """Command-line entry point: python -m seekr <command>."""
import argparse
import asyncio

from seekr import config, crawler, db


def print_host_stats(conn):
    print(f"{'host':26} {'done':>5} {'queued':>7} {'failed':>7} {'error':>6} {'blocked':>8}")
    for r in db.host_stats(conn):
        print(f"{r['host']:26} {r['done']:5} {r['queued']:7} {r['failed']:7} {r['error']:6} {r['blocked']:8}")


def cmd_crawl(args):
    conn = db.connect()
    asyncio.run(crawler.crawl(config.read_seeds(), conn))
    print_host_stats(conn)


def cmd_stats(args):
    seeds = config.read_seeds()
    db_state = "exists" if config.DB_PATH.exists() else "not created yet"
    print(f"seeds:    {len(seeds)}")
    print(f"database: {config.DB_PATH} ({db_state})")
    if config.DB_PATH.exists():
        print_host_stats(db.connect())


def not_built_yet(args):
    print(f"'{args.command}' is not built yet - see guide.html for the next chapter.")


COMMANDS = {
    "crawl": cmd_crawl,
    "index": not_built_yet,
    "rank": not_built_yet,
    "serve": not_built_yet,
    "stats": cmd_stats,
}


def main(argv=None):
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog="seekr", description="Seekr search engine")
    sub = parser.add_subparsers(dest="command", required=True)
    for name in COMMANDS:
        sub.add_parser(name)
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
    COMMANDS[args.command](args)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
    asyncio.run(...) is the bridge from normal code to async code: it starts the event loop, runs crawl until it finishes, then closes the loop. stats now also prints a per-host table once the database exists. In f"{row['host']:32}", the :32 pads the text to 32 characters so the columns line up.
  9. Test the crawler against a fake website
    tests/test_crawler.py
    import asyncio

import httpx
import pytest

from seekr import config, crawler, db

# A tiny fake website. Each path maps to (status, content type, body).
SITE = {
    "/robots.txt": (200, "text/plain", "User-agent: *\nDisallow: /private/\n"),
    "/": (200, "text/html", '<title>Home</title><a href="/a#x">A</a> <a href="/private/p">P</a>'
          ' <a href="https://other.com/">O</a> <a href="/file.pdf">PDF</a>'),
    "/a": (200, "text/html", '<title>Page A</title><a href="/?utm_source=x">home</a> <a href="/gone">G</a>'),
    "/gone": (404, "text/html", "not found"),
}


def fake_site(request):
    status, content_type, body = SITE.get(request.url.path, (404, "text/html", ""))
    return httpx.Response(status, headers={"content-type": content_type}, text=body)


@pytest.fixture
def conn(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setattr(config, "CRAWL_DELAY", 0)
    return db.connect(tmp_path / "test.db")


def run_crawl(conn, handler=fake_site):
    asyncio.run(crawler.crawl(["https://example.com/"], conn, transport=httpx.MockTransport(handler)))
    return {row["url"]: row["status"] for row in conn.execute("SELECT url, status FROM frontier")}


def test_crawl_follows_links_and_respects_robots(conn):
    frontier = run_crawl(conn)
    assert frontier == {
        "https://example.com/": "done",
        "https://example.com/a": "done",
        "https://example.com/private/p": "blocked",
        "https://example.com/gone": "failed",
    }
    titles = [row["title"] for row in conn.execute("SELECT title FROM pages ORDER BY url")]
    assert titles == ["Home", "Page A"]


def test_crawl_stops_at_page_limit(conn, monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setattr(config, "MAX_PAGES_PER_DOMAIN", 1)
    assert list(run_crawl(conn).values()).count("done") == 1


def test_crawl_retries_server_errors(conn, monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setattr(config, "RETRIES", 1)
    calls = []

    def flaky(request):
        calls.append(request.url.path)
        if request.url.path == "/" and calls.count("/") == 1:
            return httpx.Response(503)
        return fake_site(request)

    assert run_crawl(conn, flaky)["https://example.com/"] == "done"
    assert calls.count("/") == 2


def test_parse_page_finds_title_and_links():
    title, links = crawler.parse_page('<title> Hi </title><a href="b">b</a><a href="b#2">b</a>', "https://x.com/a/")
    assert title == "Hi"
    assert links == ["https://x.com/a/b"]


def test_network_errors_are_retried_on_the_next_run(conn, monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setattr(config, "RETRIES", 0)

    def offline(request):  # robots.txt loads, then the network drops
        if request.url.path == "/robots.txt":
            return fake_site(request)
        raise httpx.ConnectError("no network")

    assert run_crawl(conn, offline)["https://example.com/"] == "error"
    assert run_crawl(conn)["https://example.com/"] == "done"


def test_redirected_seed_extends_the_scope(conn):
    def redirecting(request):
        if request.url.path == "/docs/stable/":
            return httpx.Response(301, headers={"location": "/docs/6.1/"})
        if request.url.path == "/docs/6.1/":
            return httpx.Response(200, headers={"content-type": "text/html"}, text='<a href="intro/">i</a>')
        return httpx.Response(200, headers={"content-type": "text/html"}, text="<title>Intro</title>")

    transport = httpx.MockTransport(redirecting)
    asyncio.run(crawler.crawl(["https://example.com/docs/stable/"], conn, transport=transport))
    statuses = dict(conn.execute("SELECT url, status FROM frontier").fetchall())
    assert statuses["https://example.com/docs/6.1/intro/"] == "done"


def test_redirected_seed_scope_survives_a_restart(conn):
    conn.execute("INSERT INTO frontier VALUES ('https://example.com/docs/stable/', 'example.com', 0, 'done')")
    conn.execute("INSERT INTO frontier VALUES ('https://example.com/docs/6.1/x', 'example.com', 1, 'queued')")
    conn.execute("INSERT INTO pages (url, final_url) VALUES (?, ?)",
                 ("https://example.com/docs/stable/", "https://example.com/docs/6.1/"))
    page = '<a href="/docs/6.1/y">y</a>'
    site = httpx.MockTransport(lambda r: httpx.Response(200, headers={"content-type": "text/html"}, text=page))
    asyncio.run(crawler.crawl(["https://example.com/docs/stable/"], conn, transport=site))
    statuses = dict(conn.execute("SELECT url, status FROM frontier").fetchall())
    assert statuses["https://example.com/docs/6.1/y"] == "done"


def test_parse_page_follows_iframes():
    _, links = crawler.parse_page('<noscript><iframe src="toc.html"></iframe></noscript>', "https://x.com/book/")
    assert links == ["https://x.com/book/toc.html"]


def test_unreachable_robots_skips_the_host_for_now(conn):
    def down(request):
        return httpx.Response(503)

    assert run_crawl(conn, down)["https://example.com/"] == "queued"
    • httpx.MockTransport replaces the network with a plain function: every request goes to fake_site, which answers from the SITE dictionary. The tests run in milliseconds, work offline, and never bother a real server.
    • monkeypatch.setattr(config, "CRAWL_DELAY", 0) temporarily changes a setting for one test, and pytest restores it afterwards.
    • A fixture you define yourself (conn) works just like tmp_path: any test that takes a conn parameter gets a fresh database.
    • The later tests cover the tricky cases: a network outage (pages become error and succeed on the next run), a redirected seed (with and without a restart), an unreachable robots.txt, and iframe links.
    • The first test checks the whole story at once: links are followed, #x and ?utm_source duplicates collapse, robots.txt blocks /private/, the 404 is marked failed, and off-site links and PDFs are never queued.

    python -m pytest -q
    ................                                                         [100%]
16 passed in 1.20s
    The retry test takes about a second because it really waits 1s before retrying.
  11. Run a real crawl
    python -m seekr crawl
    With 300 pages per site and one request per second per site, this takes about 10 minutes. Leave it running, or stop it with Ctrl+C any time and run the same command later to continue. Want a quick try first? SEEKR_MAX_PAGES_PER_DOMAIN=20 python -m seekr crawl.

Look inside the database: sqlite3 data/seekr.db "SELECT title, url FROM pages LIMIT 5".

sqlite3.OperationalError: database is locked
Two crawls are running at once. Stop one; only one program should write to the database at a time.
Every page for one host shows failed
The site may be down or blocking you. Try the URL in your browser. The other hosts carry on regardless.
A host has 0 done and many blocked
Its robots.txt disallows those paths. That's working as intended; replace the seed with another site.
httpx.ConnectError in the tests
A test is talking to the real network. Check that it passes transport=httpx.MockTransport(...).

Chapter 3 · Extraction & chunks ready

Turn raw HTML into clean text, split it into overlapping passages, and drop duplicates.

What we're building: a web page is mostly not its content. There are menus, sidebars, cookie banners, footers and scripts. If we indexed all of that, every page from the same site would look alike, and a search for "about" would match every footer. This chapter keeps only the main text, plus a little metadata: title, description, date and language.

Why chunks? One page can be 50 words or 180,000 words. We split each page into chunks of about 300 words, for two reasons:

  • Embedding models (Chapter 5) only read about 512 tokens (roughly 350 words) at a time. Anything longer gets cut off.
  • A precise match beats a vague one. If a long page mentions "event loop" in one paragraph, we want to find that paragraph and show it as the snippet.

Neighbouring chunks overlap by 50 words, so a sentence that falls on a boundary still appears whole in at least one chunk.

Why deduplicate? The same text often lives at several URLs (/page/2 and /page/2/, or a blog homepage showing the latest post in full). We fingerprint each page's text with a hash, and if we've seen that fingerprint before, we record the page as a duplicate and don't index it again.

  1. Add trafilatura to the requirements
    requirements.txt
    pytest==8.3.4
httpx==0.28.1
trafilatura==2.3.1
    trafilatura is an open-source library made for exactly this job: it finds the main content of a web page and throws away the boilerplate. It also guesses the title, description and publication date.
  2. Install it
    pip install -r requirements.txt
  3. Add chunk settings to seekr/config.py (whole updated file)
    seekr/config.py
    """All settings in one place. Override any of them with an environment variable."""
import os
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent

DB_PATH = Path(os.environ.get("SEEKR_DB", ROOT / "data" / "seekr.db"))
SEEDS_FILE = Path(os.environ.get("SEEKR_SEEDS", ROOT / "seeds.txt"))
USER_AGENT = os.environ.get("SEEKR_USER_AGENT", "SeekrBot/0.1")
HOST = os.environ.get("SEEKR_HOST", "127.0.0.1")
PORT = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_PORT", "8000"))

# Crawler
MAX_DEPTH = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_MAX_DEPTH", "3"))
MAX_PAGES_PER_DOMAIN = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_MAX_PAGES_PER_DOMAIN", "300"))
CRAWL_DELAY = float(os.environ.get("SEEKR_CRAWL_DELAY", "1.0"))  # seconds between requests to one host
REQUEST_TIMEOUT = float(os.environ.get("SEEKR_REQUEST_TIMEOUT", "15"))
MAX_PAGE_BYTES = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_MAX_PAGE_BYTES", "2000000"))
RETRIES = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_RETRIES", "2"))

# Extraction
CHUNK_WORDS = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_CHUNK_WORDS", "300"))
CHUNK_OVERLAP = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_CHUNK_OVERLAP", "50"))


def read_seeds(path=None):
    """Return the seed URLs, skipping blank lines and # comments."""
    lines = Path(path or SEEDS_FILE).read_text().splitlines()
    return [line.strip() for line in lines if line.strip() and not line.startswith("#")]
    Two new settings under # Extraction: chunk size and overlap, both counted in words.
  4. Add the documents and chunks tables to seekr/db.py (whole updated file)
    seekr/db.py
    """SQLite storage: one file on disk, a few small tables."""
import json
import sqlite3
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from pathlib import Path

from seekr import config

SCHEMA = """
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS frontier (
    url    TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    host   TEXT NOT NULL,
    depth  INTEGER NOT NULL,
    status TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'queued'  -- queued, done, failed, error or blocked
);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS frontier_next ON frontier (host, status, depth);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS pages (
    url        TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    final_url  TEXT,
    status     INTEGER,
    title      TEXT,
    html       TEXT,
    outlinks   TEXT,  -- JSON list of absolute URLs
    fetched_at TEXT
);

-- One row per crawled page. Duplicates and pages without main text get no chunks.
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS documents (
    id           INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
    url          TEXT UNIQUE NOT NULL,
    title        TEXT,
    description  TEXT,
    published    TEXT,
    language     TEXT,
    word_count   INTEGER,
    content_hash TEXT,
    duplicate_of INTEGER
);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS documents_hash ON documents (content_hash);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS chunks (
    id       INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
    doc_id   INTEGER NOT NULL,
    position INTEGER NOT NULL,
    text     TEXT NOT NULL
);
"""


def connect(path=None):
    path = Path(path or config.DB_PATH)
    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    conn = sqlite3.connect(path)
    conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
    conn.executescript(SCHEMA)
    return conn


def enqueue(conn, urls, depth):
    rows = [(url, url.split("/")[2], depth) for url in urls]
    conn.executemany("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO frontier (url, host, depth) VALUES (?, ?, ?)", rows)


def next_url(conn, host):
    return conn.execute(
        "SELECT url, depth FROM frontier WHERE host = ? AND status = 'queued' ORDER BY depth LIMIT 1",
        (host,),
    ).fetchone()


def mark(conn, url, status):
    conn.execute("UPDATE frontier SET status = ? WHERE url = ?", (status, url))


def redirect_targets(conn, urls):
    """Where these URLs ended up after redirects, for the ones that were redirected."""
    marks = ",".join("?" * len(urls))
    sql = f"SELECT final_url FROM pages WHERE url IN ({marks}) AND final_url != url"
    return [row["final_url"] for row in conn.execute(sql, urls)]


def requeue_errors(conn):
    conn.execute("UPDATE frontier SET status = 'queued' WHERE status = 'error'")


def count_done(conn, host):
    sql = "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM frontier WHERE host = ? AND status = 'done'"
    return conn.execute(sql, (host,)).fetchone()[0]


def save_page(conn, url, final_url, status, title, html, outlinks):
    fetched_at = datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
    conn.execute(
        "INSERT OR REPLACE INTO pages VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)",
        (url, final_url, status, title, html, json.dumps(outlinks), fetched_at),
    )


def host_stats(conn):
    return conn.execute(
        """SELECT host,
                  SUM(status = 'done')    AS done,
                  SUM(status = 'queued')  AS queued,
                  SUM(status = 'failed')  AS failed,
                  SUM(status = 'error')   AS error,
                  SUM(status = 'blocked') AS blocked
           FROM frontier GROUP BY host ORDER BY host"""
    ).fetchall()
    • Only SCHEMA changed. CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS means the new tables are simply added to your existing database the next time it's opened. Your crawl data stays.
    • documents has one row per crawled page, including duplicates and empty pages. That way we know a page has been processed, and the next run can skip it.
    • duplicate_of points at the original document's id, or is NULL for originals.
    • id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY is SQLite's auto-numbering column: leave it out of an INSERT, and SQLite picks the next number.
    • chunks.doc_id links each chunk to its document, and position is the chunk's order within the page (0, 1, 2…).
  5. Create the extractor
    seekr/extract.py
    """Turn raw HTML into clean text, split it into overlapping chunks, and store them."""
import hashlib
import json
import re
from concurrent.futures import ProcessPoolExecutor

import trafilatura

from seekr import config

HTML_LANG = re.compile(r"<html[^>]*\blang=[\"']?([a-zA-Z-]+)", re.IGNORECASE)


def extract(html, url, page_title=""):
    """Main text plus metadata, or None if the page has no real content (e.g. a pure link list)."""
    result = trafilatura.extract(html, url=url, output_format="json", with_metadata=True)
    if not result:
        return None
    data = json.loads(result)
    lang = HTML_LANG.search(html[:2000])
    return {
        "title": page_title or data.get("title") or url,
        "description": data.get("excerpt") or "",
        "published": data.get("date") or "",
        "language": lang.group(1).lower() if lang else "",
        "text": data["text"].replace("¶", ""),  # Sphinx docs add ¶ after every heading
    }


def chunk_words(text, size=config.CHUNK_WORDS, overlap=config.CHUNK_OVERLAP):
    """Split text into chunks of `size` words; neighbouring chunks share `overlap` words."""
    words = text.split()
    step = size - overlap
    return [" ".join(words[start : start + size]) for start in range(0, max(len(words) - overlap, 1), step)]


def content_hash(text):
    normalized = " ".join(text.lower().split())
    return hashlib.sha256(normalized.encode()).hexdigest()


def save_document(conn, url, doc):
    """Insert one document and its chunks. Returns 'new', 'duplicate' or 'empty'."""
    if doc is None:
        conn.execute("INSERT INTO documents (url, word_count) VALUES (?, 0)", (url,))
        return "empty"
    digest = content_hash(doc["text"])
    original = conn.execute(
        "SELECT id FROM documents WHERE content_hash = ? AND duplicate_of IS NULL", (digest,)
    ).fetchone()
    cur = conn.execute(
        "INSERT INTO documents (url, title, description, published, language, word_count, content_hash,"
        " duplicate_of) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)",
        (url, doc["title"], doc["description"], doc["published"], doc["language"],
         len(doc["text"].split()), digest, original["id"] if original else None),
    )
    if original:
        return "duplicate"
    chunks = [(cur.lastrowid, i, text) for i, text in enumerate(chunk_words(doc["text"]))]
    conn.executemany("INSERT INTO chunks (doc_id, position, text) VALUES (?, ?, ?)", chunks)
    return "new"


def extract_row(row):
    return extract(row[1], row[2], row[3])


def index_pages(conn, batch_size=200):
    """Extract every page that has no document yet, using all CPU cores. Returns counts."""
    sql = """SELECT p.url, p.html, p.final_url, p.title FROM pages p
             LEFT JOIN documents d ON d.url = p.url WHERE d.url IS NULL LIMIT ?"""
    counts = {"new": 0, "duplicate": 0, "empty": 0}
    with ProcessPoolExecutor() as pool:
        while rows := [tuple(row) for row in conn.execute(sql, (batch_size,))]:
            for row, doc in zip(rows, pool.map(extract_row, rows, chunksize=8)):
                counts[save_document(conn, row[0], doc)] += 1
            conn.commit()
            print(f"  extracted {sum(counts.values())} pages...", flush=True)
    return counts
    • trafilatura.extract(..., output_format="json", with_metadata=True) returns a JSON string with text, title, excerpt, date and more. json.loads turns it into a Python dict. It returns None for pages with no real content, like a pure list of links.
    • We prefer the page's own <title>, saved by the crawler: on the Rust book, trafilatura reports "The Rust Programming Language" for every chapter, while <title> says "What Is Ownership? - The Rust Programming Language".
    • HTML_LANG is a regular expression that finds <html lang="en">. ([a-zA-Z-]+) is a capture group, read with .group(1). We store the language so search can filter later. FastAPI's docs, for example, include German translations.
    • chunk_words moves a window of size words forward by size - overlap each time. With 300 and 50, chunks start at words 0, 250, 500…. words[a:b] is a slice, and max(..., 1) makes sure even a tiny page gets one chunk.
    • content_hash: SHA-256 turns any text into a 64-character fingerprint, and the same text always gives the same fingerprint. We lowercase and collapse whitespace first, so trivial differences don't hide a duplicate.
    • cur.lastrowid is the id SQLite just gave the new document. We need it to link the chunks.
    • ProcessPoolExecutor: extraction is CPU-bound (heavy computing), unlike crawling, which was I/O-bound (waiting on the network). asyncio helps with waiting, not with computing. Because of Python's GIL, one Python process runs Python code on only one core at a time, so we start several worker processes, one per core. pool.map sends rows to the workers and returns results in the same order.
    • extract_row is a separate top-level function because worker processes can only receive functions they can import by name.
    • while rows := ... is the walrus operator: assign and test in one go. "Keep fetching batches of 200 while the batch isn't empty."
    • The LEFT JOIN ... WHERE d.url IS NULL query means "pages that have no document yet". It's what makes index incremental: run it after every crawl, and only new pages get processed.

    from seekr import db, extract

ARTICLE = """<html lang="en-US"><head><title>Ownership - The Book</title></head><body>
<nav><a href="/">Home</a> <a href="/about">About</a></nav>
<article><h1>What is ownership?¶</h1>
<p>Ownership is a set of rules that govern how a program manages memory. Every value has
an owner, and there can only be one owner at a time.</p>
<p>When the owner goes out of scope, the value is dropped and its memory is freed. This lets
the language guarantee memory safety without needing a garbage collector at runtime.</p>
</article><footer>Copyright 2026</footer></body></html>"""


def test_extract_keeps_main_text_and_drops_boilerplate():
    doc = extract.extract(ARTICLE, "https://x.com/own", "Ownership - The Book")
    assert doc["title"] == "Ownership - The Book"
    assert doc["language"] == "en-us"
    assert "only be one owner" in doc["text"]
    assert "¶" not in doc["text"]
    assert "Copyright" not in doc["text"] and "About" not in doc["text"]


def test_chunks_overlap():
    words = " ".join(f"w{i}" for i in range(25))
    chunks = extract.chunk_words(words, size=10, overlap=3)
    assert chunks[0].split()[-3:] == chunks[1].split()[:3]
    assert chunks[-1].endswith("w24")
    assert extract.chunk_words("just a few words", size=10, overlap=3) == ["just a few words"]


def test_content_hash_ignores_case_and_spacing():
    assert extract.content_hash("Hello   World") == extract.content_hash("hello world")


def test_duplicates_and_empty_pages_get_no_chunks(tmp_path):
    conn = db.connect(tmp_path / "t.db")
    doc = extract.extract(ARTICLE, "https://x.com/a")
    assert extract.save_document(conn, "https://x.com/a", doc) == "new"
    assert extract.save_document(conn, "https://x.com/a-copy", doc) == "duplicate"
    assert extract.save_document(conn, "https://x.com/links", None) == "empty"
    assert conn.execute("SELECT COUNT(DISTINCT doc_id) FROM chunks").fetchone()[0] == 1


def test_index_pages_is_incremental(tmp_path):
    conn = db.connect(tmp_path / "t.db")
    db.save_page(conn, "https://x.com/a", "https://x.com/a", 200, "A", ARTICLE, [])
    assert extract.index_pages(conn)["new"] == 1
    assert sum(extract.index_pages(conn).values()) == 0

Chapter 4 · Keyword index (BM25) ready

Find chunks that contain the query's words, ranked with BM25, in a few milliseconds, using SQLite's built-in full-text search.

What we're building: a classic inverted index, the data structure behind every search engine since the 1990s. Instead of storing "page → words", it stores "word → the chunks that contain it". Looking up "flexbox" then means reading one list, not scanning 28,841 chunks. SQLite ships one, called FTS5 (full-text search, version 5), so we don't need any new package.

How BM25 scores a match: for each query word found in a chunk, BM25 adds up three effects:

  • Rare words count more (IDF, inverse document frequency). "flexbox" appears in few chunks, so a match is meaningful. "the" appears everywhere, so it's worth almost nothing.
  • Repetition helps, with diminishing returns. A word appearing 3 times beats 1 time, but 30 times isn't 10× better than 3. That stops keyword stuffing from winning.
  • Shorter chunks win ties. A word in a 50-word chunk says more about it than the same word in a 3,000-word one.

Why keep keywords if we have embeddings? They're good at different things. Keywords are precise on exact terms like ERR_CONNECTION_RESET, fts5 or asyncio.gather, where embeddings can be vague. Embeddings understand meaning when the words don't match. Chapter 7 combines both.

  1. Check that your Python's SQLite has FTS5
    python -c "import sqlite3; c = sqlite3.connect(':memory:'); c.execute('CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE t USING fts5(a)'); print('FTS5 ok, SQLite', sqlite3.sqlite_version)"
    FTS5 ok, SQLite 3.45.3
    Homebrew's Python and the official python.org installers include FTS5. If you get no such module: fts5, see the errors below.
  2. Create the keyword index module
    seekr/index.py
    """Keyword search: a SQLite FTS5 full-text index over chunks, ranked with BM25."""
import re

FTS_SCHEMA = """
CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE IF NOT EXISTS chunks_fts USING fts5(
    title, text,
    tokenize = 'porter unicode61'  -- lowercase, strip accents, and stem: "indexes" matches "index"
);
"""
TITLE_WEIGHT, TEXT_WEIGHT = 5.0, 1.0  # a word in the title counts 5x more than in the body


def build_index(conn):
    """Add every chunk that isn't indexed yet. Chunk ids only grow, so "new" means id > the last one."""
    conn.executescript(FTS_SCHEMA)
    last_id = conn.execute("SELECT IFNULL(MAX(rowid), 0) FROM chunks_fts").fetchone()[0]
    cur = conn.execute(
        """INSERT INTO chunks_fts (rowid, title, text)
           SELECT c.id, d.title, c.text FROM chunks c JOIN documents d ON d.id = c.doc_id
           WHERE c.id > ? ORDER BY c.id""",
        (last_id,),
    )
    conn.commit()
    return cur.rowcount


def to_fts_query(query):
    """Turn free text into a safe FTS5 query: every word quoted, joined with OR."""
    words = re.findall(r"\w+", query.lower())
    return " OR ".join(f'"{word}"' for word in words)


def keyword_search(conn, query, limit=100):
    """Return [(chunk_id, bm25 score)], best first. Higher score = better match."""
    fts_query = to_fts_query(query)
    if not fts_query:
        return []
    rows = conn.execute(
        """SELECT rowid, bm25(chunks_fts, ?, ?) AS score FROM chunks_fts
           WHERE chunks_fts MATCH ? ORDER BY score LIMIT ?""",
        (TITLE_WEIGHT, TEXT_WEIGHT, fts_query, limit),
    )
    return [(row["rowid"], -row["score"]) for row in rows]  # SQLite's bm25() is negative: lower = better
    • CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE ... USING fts5(title, text): a virtual table looks like a normal table to SQL, but a module (here FTS5) decides how the data is stored. Behind the scenes FTS5 builds the inverted index.
    • The tokenizer decides what counts as a "word". unicode61 lowercases and strips accents. porter adds stemming: "indexes", "indexing" and "indexed" all become "index", so the query index finds them all.
    • rowid is every SQLite row's built-in id. We set it to the chunk's id, so a search result maps straight back to its chunk.
    • INSERT ... SELECT copies rows from a query into a table in one statement, without looping in Python. Indexing all 28,841 chunks takes about 4 seconds.
    • Incremental: chunk ids only ever grow, so "new chunks" are exactly those with an id greater than the largest one already indexed. IFNULL(MAX(rowid), 0) gives 0 when the index is empty.
    • to_fts_query: FTS5 has its own query language (AND, NOT, "phrases", col:word…), so a user typing c++ "hello could cause a syntax error. We keep only plain words (\w+), quote each one, and join them with OR. A chunk then needs only some of the words, and BM25 ranks chunks with more of them, and rarer ones, higher.
    • bm25(chunks_fts, 5.0, 1.0) gives one weight per column, so a word in the title counts 5× more than in the body. "SQLite full text search" as a title beats a page that mentions sqlite once in passing.
    • SQLite's bm25() returns negative numbers (more negative = better), so it sorts naturally with ORDER BY score. We flip the sign so callers can think "higher is better".
  3. Add the keyword index to the index command (whole updated file)

  4. Write the keyword search tests
    tests/test_index.py
    from seekr import db, index


def make_conn(tmp_path, pages):
    """pages: {title: text}. Each page becomes one document with one chunk."""
    conn = db.connect(tmp_path / "t.db")
    for doc_id, (title, text) in enumerate(pages.items(), start=1):
        conn.execute("INSERT INTO documents (id, url, title) VALUES (?, ?, ?)", (doc_id, f"https://x.com/{doc_id}", title))
        conn.execute("INSERT INTO chunks (id, doc_id, position, text) VALUES (?, ?, 0, ?)", (doc_id, doc_id, text))
    index.build_index(conn)
    return conn


def test_query_is_escaped():
    assert index.to_fts_query('SQLite "FTS5"; DROP TABLE x--') == '"sqlite" OR "fts5" OR "drop" OR "table" OR "x"'
    assert index.to_fts_query("?!") == ""


def test_title_matches_rank_higher(tmp_path):
    conn = make_conn(tmp_path, {
        "Cooking pasta": "This page mentions sqlite once among many other words about food and sauce.",
        "SQLite full text search": "How to use the FTS5 extension.",
    })
    results = index.keyword_search(conn, "sqlite")
    assert [chunk_id for chunk_id, _ in results] == [2, 1]
    assert results[0][1] > results[1][1] > 0


def test_stemming_matches_word_forms(tmp_path):
    conn = make_conn(tmp_path, {"Postgres": "Creating indexes speeds up queries."})
    assert index.keyword_search(conn, "index query")[0][0] == 1


def test_build_index_is_incremental(tmp_path):
    conn = make_conn(tmp_path, {"A": "alpha"})
    assert index.build_index(conn) == 0
    conn.execute("INSERT INTO chunks (id, doc_id, position, text) VALUES (2, 1, 1, 'beta')")
    assert index.build_index(conn) == 1
    assert index.keyword_search(conn, "beta")[0][0] == 2


def test_no_words_no_results(tmp_path):
    assert index.keyword_search(make_conn(tmp_path, {"A": "alpha"}), "!!!") == []
    make_conn is a small helper that builds a database with one chunk per page, so each test states its data in one line. enumerate(..., start=1) numbers the pages 1, 2, 3…. The escaping test feeds in quotes and SQL-looking text to prove it can't break the query.

sqlite3.OperationalError: no such module: fts5
Your Python was built against a SQLite without FTS5 (rare on Macs). Install Python with brew install python@3.12 and recreate the virtual environment.
sqlite3.OperationalError: fts5: syntax error near ...
A raw user query reached MATCH. Always pass it through to_fts_query first.
no such table: chunks_fts
You searched before building the index. Run python -m seekr index.

Chapter 5 · Embeddings on the GPU ready

Turn every chunk into a vector that captures its meaning, computed on your Mac's GPU, and search them with one matrix multiplication.

What's an embedding? A neural network reads a piece of text and outputs a list of 384 numbers, a vector. The model was trained so that texts with similar meanings get vectors that point in similar directions, even with no words in common: "how do I free memory in Rust" lands near the Rust book's chapter on ownership and drop. Keyword search (Chapter 4) can't do that.

Cosine similarity measures how closely two vectors point the same way: 1 means the same direction, 0 means unrelated. If every vector is scaled to length 1 (normalized), cosine similarity is just the dot product: multiply the numbers pairwise and add them up.

Why the GPU? Searching means comparing one query vector with all 28,841 chunk vectors. Stack the chunk vectors into a 28,841 × 384 matrix, and the whole search becomes one matrix × vector multiplication, exactly what GPUs are built for. Creating the embeddings is heavy maths too: about 512 tokens through 12 neural-network layers, for every chunk. On an Apple M4 Pro we measured:

DevicePrecisionChunks per secondAll 28,841 chunks
CPUfloat3224~20 min
GPU (Metal / MPS)float3244~11 min
GPU (Metal / MPS)float1667~7 min

Half precision (float16) uses 16 bits per number instead of 32. The GPU moves and multiplies half as much data, and the vectors came out the same (cosine 1.0000 against float32).

Which parts can't use the GPU? Crawling waits on the network, extraction is text processing with no GPU support, and keyword search runs inside SQLite. The GPU helps where there's heavy maths: embeddings here, and the relevance reranker in Chapter 7.

  1. Add PyTorch and sentence-transformers to the requirements
    requirements.txt
    pytest==8.3.4
httpx==0.28.1
trafilatura==2.3.1
torch==2.15.0
sentence-transformers==6.3.0
numpy==2.5.4
    PyTorch (torch) is the open-source deep-learning library. It runs on NVIDIA GPUs (CUDA), Apple Silicon GPUs (MPS, Metal Performance Shaders) or the CPU. sentence-transformers sits on top and turns sentences into embeddings in one call. numpy is the standard library for arrays of numbers.
  2. Install them (about 1 GB, so it takes a minute)
    pip install -r requirements.txt
  3. Check that PyTorch can see your GPU
    python -c "import torch; print('mps:', torch.backends.mps.is_available(), '| cuda:', torch.cuda.is_available())"
    mps: True | cuda: False
    On an Apple Silicon Mac (M1–M4) you should see mps: True. On an Intel Mac both are False, and everything still works on the CPU, just slower.
  4. Add the embedding settings to seekr/config.py (whole updated file)
    seekr/config.py
    """All settings in one place. Override any of them with an environment variable."""
import os
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent

DB_PATH = Path(os.environ.get("SEEKR_DB", ROOT / "data" / "seekr.db"))
SEEDS_FILE = Path(os.environ.get("SEEKR_SEEDS", ROOT / "seeds.txt"))
USER_AGENT = os.environ.get("SEEKR_USER_AGENT", "SeekrBot/0.1")
HOST = os.environ.get("SEEKR_HOST", "127.0.0.1")
PORT = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_PORT", "8000"))

# Crawler
MAX_DEPTH = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_MAX_DEPTH", "3"))
MAX_PAGES_PER_DOMAIN = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_MAX_PAGES_PER_DOMAIN", "300"))
CRAWL_DELAY = float(os.environ.get("SEEKR_CRAWL_DELAY", "1.0"))  # seconds between requests to one host
REQUEST_TIMEOUT = float(os.environ.get("SEEKR_REQUEST_TIMEOUT", "15"))
MAX_PAGE_BYTES = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_MAX_PAGE_BYTES", "2000000"))
RETRIES = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_RETRIES", "2"))

# Extraction
CHUNK_WORDS = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_CHUNK_WORDS", "300"))
CHUNK_OVERLAP = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_CHUNK_OVERLAP", "50"))

# Embeddings (runs on the GPU when there is one)
EMBED_MODEL = os.environ.get("SEEKR_EMBED_MODEL", "BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5")
EMBED_BATCH = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_EMBED_BATCH", "64"))
DEVICE = os.environ.get("SEEKR_DEVICE", "")  # "", "cuda", "mps" or "cpu"; "" picks the best one


def read_seeds(path=None):
    """Return the seed URLs, skipping blank lines and # comments."""
    lines = Path(path or SEEDS_FILE).read_text().splitlines()
    return [line.strip() for line in lines if line.strip() and not line.startswith("#")]
    BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5 is a small (33M parameters), fast, open-source embedding model that scores well on retrieval benchmarks. It's downloaded once (about 130 MB) and cached. SEEKR_DEVICE=cpu forces the CPU, which is handy for comparing speeds.
  5. Add the embeddings table to seekr/db.py (whole updated file)
    seekr/db.py
    """SQLite storage: one file on disk, a few small tables."""
import json
import sqlite3
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from pathlib import Path

from seekr import config

SCHEMA = """
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS frontier (
    url    TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    host   TEXT NOT NULL,
    depth  INTEGER NOT NULL,
    status TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'queued'  -- queued, done, failed, error or blocked
);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS frontier_next ON frontier (host, status, depth);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS pages (
    url        TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    final_url  TEXT,
    status     INTEGER,
    title      TEXT,
    html       TEXT,
    outlinks   TEXT,  -- JSON list of absolute URLs
    fetched_at TEXT
);

-- One row per crawled page. Duplicates and pages without main text get no chunks.
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS documents (
    id           INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
    url          TEXT UNIQUE NOT NULL,
    title        TEXT,
    description  TEXT,
    published    TEXT,
    language     TEXT,
    word_count   INTEGER,
    content_hash TEXT,
    duplicate_of INTEGER
);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS documents_hash ON documents (content_hash);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS chunks (
    id       INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
    doc_id   INTEGER NOT NULL,
    position INTEGER NOT NULL,
    text     TEXT NOT NULL
);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS embeddings (
    chunk_id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
    vector   BLOB NOT NULL  -- float32 bytes, length 1.0
);
"""


def connect(path=None):
    path = Path(path or config.DB_PATH)
    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    conn = sqlite3.connect(path)
    conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
    conn.executescript(SCHEMA)
    return conn


def enqueue(conn, urls, depth):
    rows = [(url, url.split("/")[2], depth) for url in urls]
    conn.executemany("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO frontier (url, host, depth) VALUES (?, ?, ?)", rows)


def next_url(conn, host):
    return conn.execute(
        "SELECT url, depth FROM frontier WHERE host = ? AND status = 'queued' ORDER BY depth LIMIT 1",
        (host,),
    ).fetchone()


def mark(conn, url, status):
    conn.execute("UPDATE frontier SET status = ? WHERE url = ?", (status, url))


def redirect_targets(conn, urls):
    """Where these URLs ended up after redirects, for the ones that were redirected."""
    marks = ",".join("?" * len(urls))
    sql = f"SELECT final_url FROM pages WHERE url IN ({marks}) AND final_url != url"
    return [row["final_url"] for row in conn.execute(sql, urls)]


def requeue_errors(conn):
    conn.execute("UPDATE frontier SET status = 'queued' WHERE status = 'error'")


def count_done(conn, host):
    sql = "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM frontier WHERE host = ? AND status = 'done'"
    return conn.execute(sql, (host,)).fetchone()[0]


def save_page(conn, url, final_url, status, title, html, outlinks):
    fetched_at = datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
    conn.execute(
        "INSERT OR REPLACE INTO pages VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)",
        (url, final_url, status, title, html, json.dumps(outlinks), fetched_at),
    )


def host_stats(conn):
    return conn.execute(
        """SELECT host,
                  SUM(status = 'done')    AS done,
                  SUM(status = 'queued')  AS queued,
                  SUM(status = 'failed')  AS failed,
                  SUM(status = 'error')   AS error,
                  SUM(status = 'blocked') AS blocked
           FROM frontier GROUP BY host ORDER BY host"""
    ).fetchall()
    A BLOB column stores raw bytes. Each vector is 384 float32 numbers × 4 bytes = 1,536 bytes. chunk_id is the primary key, so each chunk has at most one vector.
  6. Create the embedding module
    seekr/embed.py
    """Embeddings: turn text into vectors on the GPU, and find the nearest vectors fast."""
import numpy as np
import torch
from sentence_transformers import SentenceTransformer

from seekr import config

# BGE models are trained to expect this prefix on short search queries (not on passages).
QUERY_PREFIX = "Represent this sentence for searching relevant passages: "
_model = None


def best_device():
    if config.DEVICE:
        return config.DEVICE
    if torch.cuda.is_available():
        return "cuda"  # NVIDIA GPU
    if torch.backends.mps.is_available():
        return "mps"  # Apple Silicon GPU (Metal)
    return "cpu"


def get_model():
    """Load the model once, on the best device. Half precision is ~2x faster on a GPU."""
    global _model
    if _model is None:
        _model = SentenceTransformer(config.EMBED_MODEL, device=best_device())
        if best_device() != "cpu":
            _model.half()
    return _model


def embed_texts(texts):
    vectors = get_model().encode(texts, batch_size=config.EMBED_BATCH, normalize_embeddings=True)
    return vectors.astype(np.float32)


def embed_query(query):
    return embed_texts([QUERY_PREFIX + query])[0]


def embed_chunks(conn, batch_size=1024):
    """Embed every chunk that has no vector yet. The page title goes in too, for context."""
    sql = """SELECT c.id, d.title, c.text FROM chunks c JOIN documents d ON d.id = c.doc_id
             LEFT JOIN embeddings e ON e.chunk_id = c.id WHERE e.chunk_id IS NULL LIMIT ?"""
    total = 0
    while rows := conn.execute(sql, (batch_size,)).fetchall():
        vectors = embed_texts([f"{row['title']}\n{row['text']}" for row in rows])
        conn.executemany(
            "INSERT INTO embeddings VALUES (?, ?)",
            [(row["id"], vector.tobytes()) for row, vector in zip(rows, vectors)],
        )
        conn.commit()
        total += len(rows)
        print(f"  embedded {total} chunks on {best_device()}...", flush=True)
    return total


class VectorIndex:
    """All chunk vectors in one matrix on the GPU. Search = one matrix multiplication."""

    def __init__(self, conn):
        rows = conn.execute("SELECT chunk_id, vector FROM embeddings ORDER BY chunk_id").fetchall()
        self.chunk_ids = [row["chunk_id"] for row in rows]
        matrix = np.array([np.frombuffer(row["vector"], dtype=np.float32) for row in rows], dtype=np.float32)
        self.matrix = torch.from_numpy(matrix).to(best_device())

    def search(self, query_vector, k=100):
        """Return [(chunk_id, cosine similarity)], best first."""
        if not self.chunk_ids:
            return []
        query = torch.from_numpy(query_vector).to(self.matrix.device)
        scores = self.matrix @ query  # vectors have length 1, so dot product = cosine similarity
        top = torch.topk(scores, min(k, len(self.chunk_ids)))
        return [(self.chunk_ids[i], score) for score, i in zip(top.values.tolist(), top.indices.tolist())]
    • best_device() picks the fastest hardware: an NVIDIA GPU ("cuda"), the Apple GPU ("mps"), or the CPU. Every tensor and model we .to(device) then lives in that device's memory.
    • get_model() loads the model the first time it's needed and reuses it after that (the global _model line lets the function change the module-level variable). Loading takes a second or two, so we don't want to do it on every search. .half() switches the model to float16 on the GPU.
    • This kind of model is a bi-encoder: it encodes the query and each passage separately into vectors. That's what makes search fast, because passages are encoded once, ahead of time. (Chapter 7 adds a slower but sharper cross-encoder.)
    • normalize_embeddings=True scales every vector to length 1, so a dot product is a cosine similarity.
    • QUERY_PREFIX: BGE models were trained with this instruction in front of short queries, and adding it improves results. Passages go in without it.
    • We embed title + "\n" + text, so a chunk from "asyncio — Asynchronous I/O" knows what page it's on, even if that chunk never says "asyncio".
    • vector.tobytes() turns a numpy array into raw bytes for the BLOB, and np.frombuffer(..., dtype=np.float32) turns them back.
    • VectorIndex loads all vectors into one matrix on the GPU. self.matrix @ query (@ is matrix multiplication) computes all 28,841 similarities at once, and torch.topk picks the k highest without sorting everything. This is brute-force search: exact, and fast enough up to a few million vectors.
    • .tolist() copies the results from GPU memory back into normal Python numbers.
  7. Add embeddings to the index command (whole updated file)
    seekr/__main__.py

import argparse
import asyncio

from seekr import config, crawler, db, embed, extract, index

    print_host_stats(conn)

    print(f"new: {counts['new']}  duplicates: {counts['duplicate']}  no main text: {counts['empty']}")

    if config.DB_PATH.exists():
        conn = db.connect()
        print_host_stats(conn)

    COMMANDS[args.command](args)

  8. Write the embedding tests
    tests/test_embed.py
    import numpy as np

from seekr import db, embed


def test_similar_meanings_get_close_vectors():
    cat, kitten, sql = embed.embed_texts(["a cat sleeping", "a small kitten napping", "SQL database index"])
    assert np.isclose(np.linalg.norm(cat), 1.0, atol=1e-3)
    assert cat @ kitten > cat @ sql


def test_vector_index_finds_nearest_chunk(tmp_path):
    conn = db.connect(tmp_path / "t.db")
    vectors = {1: [1.0, 0.0], 2: [0.0, 1.0], 3: [0.6, 0.8]}
    for chunk_id, vector in vectors.items():
        conn.execute("INSERT INTO embeddings VALUES (?, ?)", (chunk_id, np.array(vector, np.float32).tobytes()))
    index = embed.VectorIndex(conn)
    results = index.search(np.array([0.0, 1.0], np.float32), k=2)
    assert [chunk_id for chunk_id, _ in results] == [2, 3]
    assert abs(results[1][1] - 0.8) < 1e-3


def test_embed_chunks_is_incremental(tmp_path):
    conn = db.connect(tmp_path / "t.db")
    conn.execute("INSERT INTO documents (id, url, title) VALUES (1, 'https://x.com', 'X')")
    conn.execute("INSERT INTO chunks (doc_id, position, text) VALUES (1, 0, 'hello world')")
    assert embed.embed_chunks(conn) == 1
    assert embed.embed_chunks(conn) == 0


def test_empty_index_returns_nothing(tmp_path):
    assert embed.VectorIndex(db.connect(tmp_path / "t.db")).search(np.zeros(384, np.float32)) == []


def test_uses_the_gpu_when_available():
    import torch
    if torch.backends.mps.is_available() or torch.cuda.is_available():
        assert embed.best_device() in ("mps", "cuda")
    The first test uses the real model: "a cat sleeping" must land closer to "a small kitten napping" (no shared words!) than to "SQL database index". The vector-index test uses hand-made 2-D vectors, so we know the right answer exactly: [0.6, 0.8] · [0, 1] = 0.8.

mps: False on an Apple Silicon Mac
Your Python is probably the Intel (x86) build running under Rosetta. Check with python -c "import platform; print(platform.machine())". It should print arm64. If it doesn't, install Python with Homebrew (Chapter 0) and recreate .venv.

The first run needs internet to download the model. After that it's cached in ~/.cache/huggingface, and everything works offline.
