Chapter 6 · PageRank ready

Score every page by how much the rest of the crawl links to it: the idea that made Google.

The problem it solves: BM25 and embeddings only ask "does this page match the query?" They can't tell a well-known, trusted page from an obscure or spammy one with the same words. Links are a vote of confidence: if many pages link to a page, and those pages are linked to a lot, it's probably important.

The random surfer: imagine someone clicking links forever. 85% of the time they click a random link on the current page, and 15% of the time they get bored and jump to a random page anywhere. A page's PageRank is the share of time this surfer spends on it. Pages with many incoming links from important pages get visited more. The 85% is the damping factor, d = 0.85.

Power iteration: we can't simulate forever, but we don't need to. Start every page at 1/n, then repeatedly let every page pass its score along its outgoing links. After a few dozen rounds the numbers stop changing (they converge), and that's the PageRank. In a formula, for every page:

rank(page) = (1 − d) / n  +  d × Σ  rank(q) / outlinks(q)      (for every page q that links to page)

Does PageRank still matter? Yes, but as a tie-breaker, not as the main signal. It knows nothing about your query. So Seekr gives it 15% of the final score in Chapter 7, and relevance gets 75%. In a small focused crawl like ours it mostly rewards each site's hubs (homepages, archives, tables of contents), which is another reason to keep its weight small. CPU or GPU? CPU. Our graph has about 4,000 nodes and 165,000 links, and numpy finishes in well under a second. Moving the data to the GPU would take longer than the maths.

  1. Create the PageRank module
    seekr/rank.py
    """Authority: PageRank over the links between crawled pages."""
import json

import numpy as np

SCHEMA = "CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS pagerank (doc_id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY, score REAL NOT NULL);"
DAMPING = 0.85  # chance the "random surfer" follows a link instead of jumping to a random page


def link_graph(conn):
    """Return (doc_ids, edges): edges are (from, to) positions in doc_ids, one per linked pair of pages."""
    rows = conn.execute(
        """SELECT p.url, p.final_url, p.outlinks, IFNULL(d.duplicate_of, d.id) AS doc_id
           FROM pages p JOIN documents d ON d.url = p.url"""
    ).fetchall()
    doc_of = {}  # any URL we crawled (before or after redirects) -> its document; duplicates -> the original
    for row in rows:
        doc_of[row["url"]] = doc_of[row["final_url"]] = row["doc_id"]
    doc_ids = sorted(set(doc_of.values()))
    position = {doc_id: i for i, doc_id in enumerate(doc_ids)}
    edges = set()
    for row in rows:
        source = position[row["doc_id"]]
        for link in json.loads(row["outlinks"] or "[]"):
            if link in doc_of and position[doc_of[link]] != source:
                edges.add((source, position[doc_of[link]]))
    return doc_ids, sorted(edges)


def pagerank(n, edges, damping=DAMPING, tolerance=1e-10, max_iterations=100):
    """Power iteration. Returns one score per node; the scores add up to 1."""
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0)
    edges = np.array(edges, dtype=np.int64).reshape(-1, 2)
    source, target = edges[:, 0], edges[:, 1]
    out_degree = np.bincount(source, minlength=n)
    rank = np.full(n, 1 / n)
    for _ in range(max_iterations):
        passed_on = np.bincount(target, weights=rank[source] / out_degree[source], minlength=n)
        dangling = rank[out_degree == 0].sum()  # pages with no links share their score with everyone
        new_rank = (1 - damping) / n + damping * (passed_on + dangling / n)
        if np.abs(new_rank - rank).sum() < tolerance:
            return new_rank
        rank = new_rank
    return rank


def compute_pagerank(conn):
    """Rebuild the pagerank table from scratch. Returns (number of pages, number of links)."""
    conn.executescript(SCHEMA)
    doc_ids, edges = link_graph(conn)
    scores = pagerank(len(doc_ids), edges)
    conn.execute("DELETE FROM pagerank")
    conn.executemany("INSERT INTO pagerank VALUES (?, ?)", zip(doc_ids, scores.tolist()))
    conn.commit()
    return len(doc_ids), len(edges)
    • link_graph turns the crawl into a graph. Each node is a document, and each edge is "page A links to page B". The crawler stored every page's links in pages.outlinks as JSON, and json.loads turns that back into a list.
    • doc_of maps every URL we know to its document, including the URL a page redirected to (final_url), and maps duplicates to their original. So a link to /page/2 and a link to /page/2/ count as votes for the same page.
    • IFNULL(d.duplicate_of, d.id) means "the original's id if this is a duplicate, otherwise my own id".
    • edges is a set, so ten links from A to B count once. We also skip links from a page to itself.
    • position renumbers documents 0, 1, 2… so they can be used as positions in numpy arrays.
    • Vectorization, no Python loop over links: rank[source] / out_degree[source] computes the share each of the 165,530 links carries, all at once. np.bincount(target, weights=...) adds those shares up per target page. numpy does this in compiled C code, hundreds of times faster than a for loop.
    • np.bincount(source, minlength=n) counts how many links leave each page: its out-degree.
    • Dangling pages have no outgoing links, so their score would leak away. We collect it (dangling) and spread it evenly over all pages, as if the surfer jumped somewhere random. That keeps the total at exactly 1.
    • We stop when the scores change by less than tolerance in total, which usually takes 30–60 rounds.
    • compute_pagerank recomputes from scratch every time (DELETE, then insert). Any new page can change everyone's score, so there's no point doing it incrementally.
  2. Wire up the rank command (whole updated file)
    seekr/__main__.py
    """Command-line entry point: python -m seekr <command>."""
import argparse
import asyncio

from seekr import config, crawler, db, embed, extract, index, rank


def print_host_stats(conn):
    print(f"{'host':26} {'done':>5} {'queued':>7} {'failed':>7} {'error':>6} {'blocked':>8}")
    for r in db.host_stats(conn):
        print(f"{r['host']:26} {r['done']:5} {r['queued']:7} {r['failed']:7} {r['error']:6} {r['blocked']:8}")


def cmd_crawl(args):
    conn = db.connect()
    asyncio.run(crawler.crawl(config.read_seeds(), conn))
    print_host_stats(conn)


def cmd_index(args):
    conn = db.connect()
    print("Extracting text from crawled pages:")
    counts = extract.index_pages(conn)
    print(f"new: {counts['new']}  duplicates: {counts['duplicate']}  no main text: {counts['empty']}")
    print(f"Keyword index: added {index.build_index(conn)} chunks")
    print(f"Embedding chunks on {embed.best_device()}:")
    print(f"embedded: {embed.embed_chunks(conn)}")


def cmd_rank(args):
    conn = db.connect()
    pages, links = rank.compute_pagerank(conn)
    print(f"PageRank over {pages} pages and {links} links. Top 10:")
    top = conn.execute(
        "SELECT r.score, d.url FROM pagerank r JOIN documents d ON d.id = r.doc_id ORDER BY r.score DESC LIMIT 10"
    )
    for row in top:
        print(f"  {row['score']:.5f}  {row['url']}")


def cmd_stats(args):
    seeds = config.read_seeds()
    db_state = "exists" if config.DB_PATH.exists() else "not created yet"
    print(f"seeds:    {len(seeds)}")
    print(f"database: {config.DB_PATH} ({db_state})")
    if config.DB_PATH.exists():
        conn = db.connect()
        print_host_stats(conn)
        for table in ("pages", "documents", "chunks", "embeddings"):
            print(f"{table + ':':10} {conn.execute(f'SELECT COUNT(*) FROM {table}').fetchone()[0]}")


def not_built_yet(args):
    print(f"'{args.command}' is not built yet - see guide.html for the next chapter.")


COMMANDS = {
    "crawl": cmd_crawl,
    "index": cmd_index,
    "rank": cmd_rank,
    "serve": not_built_yet,
    "stats": cmd_stats,
}


def main(argv=None):
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog="seekr", description="Seekr search engine")
    sub = parser.add_subparsers(dest="command", required=True)
    for name in COMMANDS:
        sub.add_parser(name)
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
    COMMANDS[args.command](args)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
    cmd_rank computes PageRank and prints the 10 highest-scoring pages. It uses a JOIN to show URLs instead of document ids. :.5f formats a number with 5 decimals.
  3. Write the PageRank tests
    tests/test_rank.py
    import numpy as np

from seekr import db, rank


def test_cycle_gives_equal_scores():
    scores = rank.pagerank(3, [(0, 1), (1, 2), (2, 0)])
    assert np.allclose(scores, 1 / 3)


def test_most_linked_page_wins_and_scores_sum_to_one():
    scores = rank.pagerank(4, [(1, 0), (2, 0), (3, 0)])  # page 0 has no links of its own (dangling)
    assert scores.argmax() == 0
    assert np.isclose(scores.sum(), 1.0)
    assert np.allclose(scores[1:], scores[1])  # 1, 2 and 3 are in the same position


def test_known_values_for_a_small_graph():
    # Worked by hand: B and C link to A, A links to B. Solve r = 0.05 + 0.85 * (incoming shares).
    a, b, c = rank.pagerank(3, [(1, 0), (2, 0), (0, 1)])
    assert np.isclose(c, 0.05)
    assert np.isclose(a, 0.05 + 0.85 * (b + c))
    assert np.isclose(b, 0.05 + 0.85 * a)


def test_link_graph_follows_redirects_and_merges_duplicates(tmp_path):
    conn = db.connect(tmp_path / "t.db")
    db.save_page(conn, "https://x.com/a", "https://x.com/a", 200, "A", "", ["https://x.com/old-b", "https://x.com/a"])
    db.save_page(conn, "https://x.com/old-b", "https://x.com/b", 200, "B", "", ["https://x.com/a-copy"])
    db.save_page(conn, "https://x.com/a-copy", "https://x.com/a-copy", 200, "A", "", [])
    conn.execute("INSERT INTO documents (id, url) VALUES (1, 'https://x.com/a'), (2, 'https://x.com/old-b')")
    conn.execute("INSERT INTO documents (id, url, duplicate_of) VALUES (3, 'https://x.com/a-copy', 1)")
    doc_ids, edges = rank.link_graph(conn)
    assert doc_ids == [1, 2]
    assert edges == [(0, 1), (1, 0)]  # a -> b (via redirect) and b -> a (via the duplicate); no self-link


def test_compute_pagerank_stores_scores(tmp_path):
    conn = db.connect(tmp_path / "t.db")
    db.save_page(conn, "https://x.com/a", "https://x.com/a", 200, "A", "", ["https://x.com/b"])
    db.save_page(conn, "https://x.com/b", "https://x.com/b", 200, "B", "", [])
    conn.execute("INSERT INTO documents (id, url) VALUES (1, 'https://x.com/a'), (2, 'https://x.com/b')")
    assert rank.compute_pagerank(conn) == (2, 1)
    scores = dict(conn.execute("SELECT doc_id, score FROM pagerank").fetchall())
    assert scores[2] > scores[1]
    • A cycle (A→B→C→A) is perfectly symmetric, so every page must get exactly 1/3.
    • In the star, three pages point at page 0, which has no links of its own. Page 0 must win, the total must stay 1 (dangling handling), and the three identical pages must tie.
    • test_known_values_for_a_small_graph checks the formula itself. Nobody links to C, so C gets only the "random jump" part: (1 − 0.85) / 3 = 0.05.
    • np.isclose and np.allclose compare decimal numbers with a tiny tolerance, because 0.1 + 0.2 == 0.3 is False in floating point.

no such table: pagerank
Run python -m seekr rank first. It creates the table.
The sum isn't 1.0
Dangling pages are leaking score. Check the dangling / n term in pagerank().
PageRank over 0 pages
The documents table is empty. Run python -m seekr index (Chapter 3) before rank.
RuntimeWarning: divide by zero
You divided by out_degree for pages with no links. Only out_degree[source] is used, and every source has at least one link, so check that you index with [source].

Chapter 7 · Hybrid search & relevance score ready

The heart of Seekr: combine keyword and semantic candidates, then let a relevance model read each one and decide how well it answers the query. Then measure whether it actually helped.

Two stages, like every modern search engine:

  1. Retrieve (fast, rough): BM25 (Chapter 4) and vectors (Chapter 5) each return their top 100 chunks in milliseconds. They find good candidates, but their ordering is only approximate.
  2. Rerank (slow, sharp): a cross-encoder reads the query and each of the top 30 pages together and outputs a relevance score. It's too slow to run on 28,841 chunks, but on 30 it takes under 0.1 s on the GPU.

Bi-encoder vs cross-encoder. The embedding model in Chapter 5 is a bi-encoder: it turns the query and the passage into vectors separately, and similarity is just how close the vectors are. That's fast, because passages are encoded ahead of time, but the model never sees the query and the passage side by side. A cross-encoder feeds query + passage into one network, so every query word can pay attention to every passage word. It can tell "how do I free memory" from "memory is free". That's why its score is a real relevance score, and similarity isn't.

Reciprocal Rank Fusion (RRF). How do you merge a BM25 list (scores like 17.9) with a vector list (scores like 0.74)? The numbers aren't comparable, but positions are. RRF gives each chunk 1 / (60 + rank) from every list it appears in and adds them up. A chunk ranked 2nd by BM25 and 1st by vectors gets 1/62 + 1/61 and beats a chunk that's 1st in only one list. The 60 stops the very top position from dominating. RRF has no weights to tune, which is why it's the standard way to fuse rankings.

  1. Add the search settings to seekr/config.py (whole updated file)
    seekr/config.py
    """All settings in one place. Override any of them with an environment variable."""

# MiniLM beat BAAI/bge-reranker-base on our eval (MRR 0.733 vs 0.648) and is 3x faster; try either.
RERANK_MODEL = os.environ.get("SEEKR_RERANK_MODEL", "cross-encoder/ms-marco-MiniLM-L-6-v2")

def read_seeds(path=None):
    """Return the seed URLs, skipping blank lines and # comments."""
    lines = Path(path or SEEDS_FILE).read_text().splitlines()
    return [line.strip() for line in lines if line.strip() and not line.startswith("#")]
    The weights and the reranker model aren't guesses: they came out of the evaluation at the end of this chapter (see "What the measurements taught us"). a, b = 1, 2 assigns several names in one line.
  2. Create the search module
    seekr/search.py
    """Hybrid search: keyword + semantic candidates, then a cross-encoder decides how relevant each page is."""
import html
import math
import re
from datetime import date
from urllib.parse import urlsplit

import numpy as np
import torch
from sentence_transformers import CrossEncoder

from seekr import config, embed, index

CHUNK_SQL = """SELECT c.doc_id, c.text, d.url, d.title, d.published, d.language, d.word_count
               FROM chunks c JOIN documents d ON d.id = c.doc_id WHERE c.id = ?"""
STOPWORDS = {"the", "and", "for", "how", "can", "what", "with", "does", "are", "you", "your", "from", "that", "this",
             "when", "why", "which", "into", "about", "same"}
_reranker = None


def get_reranker():
    global _reranker
    if _reranker is None:
        device = embed.best_device()
        _reranker = CrossEncoder(config.RERANK_MODEL, device=device, max_length=config.RERANK_MAX_TOKENS)
        if device != "cpu":
            _reranker.model.half()
    return _reranker


def sigmoid(x):
    return 1 / (1 + np.exp(-x))


def rrf(rankings, k=60):
    """Reciprocal Rank Fusion. rankings: {name: [(id, score), ...] best first} -> (fused scores, ranks)."""
    fused, ranks = {}, {}
    for name, hits in rankings.items():
        for rank, (item, _) in enumerate(hits, start=1):
            fused[item] = fused.get(item, 0.0) + 1 / (k + rank)
            ranks.setdefault(item, {})[name] = rank
    return fused, ranks


def query_words(query):
    return re.findall(r"\w+", query.lower())


def highlight(text, query, width=300):
    """A window of text around the first query word, HTML-escaped, with query words wrapped in <mark>."""
    words = [re.escape(word) for word in query_words(query) if len(word) > 2 and word not in STOPWORDS]
    pattern = re.compile(r"\b(?:" + "|".join(words) + r")\w*", re.IGNORECASE) if words else None
    first = pattern.search(text) if pattern else None
    start = max(0, first.start() - 80) if first else 0
    start = text.find(" ", start) + 1 if start else 0  # begin on a whole word
    window = ("…" if start else "") + text[start : start + width] + ("…" if start + width < len(text) else "")
    parts, last = [], 0
    for match in pattern.finditer(window) if pattern else []:
        parts += [html.escape(window[last : match.start()]), "<mark>", html.escape(match.group()), "</mark>"]
        last = match.end()
    return "".join(parts) + html.escape(window[last:])


def freshness(published):
    """1.0 for a page from today, 0.5 after a year, 0.33 after two... and 0.5 when there is no date."""
    try:
        age_years = (date.today() - date.fromisoformat(published[:10])).days / 365
    except (TypeError, ValueError):
        return 0.5
    return 1 / (1 + max(age_years, 0))


class SearchEngine:
    def __init__(self, conn):
        self.conn = conn
        self.vectors = embed.VectorIndex(conn)
        logs = {doc: math.log(score) for doc, score in conn.execute("SELECT doc_id, score FROM pagerank")}
        low, high = min(logs.values(), default=0.0), max(logs.values(), default=1.0)
        self.pagerank = {doc: (value - low) / ((high - low) or 1) for doc, value in logs.items()}  # 0..1

    def candidates(self, query, mode="full", site=None):
        """Best chunk per page from BM25 and/or vectors, fused with RRF. English pages only."""
        rankings = {}
        if mode in ("bm25", "hybrid", "full"):
            rankings["bm25"] = index.keyword_search(self.conn, query, config.CANDIDATES)
        if mode in ("vector", "hybrid", "full"):
            rankings["vector"] = self.vectors.search(embed.embed_query(query), config.CANDIDATES)
        fused, ranks = rrf(rankings)
        pages, seen = [], set()
        for chunk_id in sorted(fused, key=fused.get, reverse=True):
            page = dict(self.conn.execute(CHUNK_SQL, (chunk_id,)).fetchone())
            host = urlsplit(page["url"]).netloc
            if page["doc_id"] in seen or not (page["language"] or "en").startswith("en"):
                continue
            if site and host != site and not host.endswith("." + site):
                continue
            seen.add(page["doc_id"])
            pages.append({**page, "ranks": ranks[chunk_id]})
        return pages

    def rerank(self, query, pages):
        """Score each page: mostly the cross-encoder's relevance, plus authority, freshness and exact matches."""
        if not pages:
            return []
        pairs = [(query, f"{page['title']}\n{page['text']}") for page in pages]
        logits = get_reranker().predict(pairs, batch_size=16, activation_fn=torch.nn.Identity())
        words = query_words(query)
        for page, logit in zip(pages, logits):
            title_and_url = f"{page['title']} {page['url']}".lower()
            page["relevance"] = float(sigmoid(logit))
            page["pagerank"] = self.pagerank.get(page["doc_id"], 0.0)
            page["freshness"] = freshness(page["published"])
            page["exact"] = sum(word in title_and_url for word in words) / max(len(words), 1)
            page["final_score"] = (
                config.W_RELEVANCE * page["relevance"] + config.W_PAGERANK * page["pagerank"]
                + config.W_FRESHNESS * page["freshness"] + config.W_EXACT * page["exact"]
            )
            if page["word_count"] < config.THIN_PAGE_WORDS:
                page["final_score"] *= config.THIN_PAGE_PENALTY
        return sorted(pages, key=lambda page: page["final_score"], reverse=True)

    def search(self, query, limit=10, offset=0, site=None, mode="full"):
        pages = self.candidates(query, mode, site)
        if mode == "full":
            pages = self.rerank(query, pages[: config.RERANK_TOP])
        return [self.to_result(page, query) for page in pages[offset : offset + limit]]

    def to_result(self, page, query):
        return {
            "url": page["url"],
            "title": page["title"] or page["url"],
            "snippet": highlight(page["text"], query),
            "relevance": page.get("relevance"),
            "final_score": page.get("final_score"),
            "signals": {
                "bm25_rank": page["ranks"].get("bm25"),
                "vector_rank": page["ranks"].get("vector"),
                "relevance": page.get("relevance"),
                "pagerank": page.get("pagerank"),
                "freshness": page.get("freshness"),
            },
        }
    • rrf implements the fusion described above. ranks remembers each chunk's position in each list, for the signals breakdown.
    • candidates walks the fused list best-first and keeps the best chunk per page, so one long page can't fill the results with ten of its own chunks. It also skips non-English pages (FastAPI's translations) and applies the optional site filter. dict(row) turns a SQLite row into a normal dictionary we can add keys to, and {**page, "ranks": ...} copies a dict and adds one key.
    • get_reranker loads the cross-encoder once, on the GPU, in half precision, just like the embedding model. max_length=384 tokens covers the title plus our ~300-word chunk.
    • Sigmoid calibration: the cross-encoder outputs a raw number (a logit) from about −11 to +11. We ask for it raw (activation_fn=torch.nn.Identity()), then squash it into 0–1 with the sigmoid 1 / (1 + e−x). 0 becomes 0.5, large positive numbers approach 1, and large negative numbers approach 0. The model was trained so this number behaves like "probability that this passage answers the query", so relevance = 0.97 means something on its own, not just "better than the others".
    • The final score is a weighted sum: 90% relevance, 5% PageRank (log-scaled to 0–1 in __init__, because raw PageRank spans 0.00005 to 0.02), and 5% for the fraction of query words that appear in the title or URL. Pages under 50 words lose 20%.
    • Why log-scale PageRank? The top page has 400× the score of a typical page. Linearly, everything except a few hubs would round to 0. math.log turns "400× more" into "a bit more".
    • highlight cuts a ~300-character window around the first query word and wraps matches in <mark>. It escapes every piece of page text with html.escape before adding our own tags. Otherwise a page containing <script> could run code in the user's browser (an XSS attack). \w* after the word also highlights "tasks" for "task". Words in STOPWORDS ("the", "how", …) and words of 1–2 letters are not highlighted, because marking every "the" makes a snippet harder to scan. The window also starts on a whole word, never mid-word.
    • freshness is computed and reported in signals, but its weight is 0. Documentation sites stamp "last updated" on every page, so the dates misled the ranking.
    • mode lets us switch stages off (bm25, vector, hybrid, full), which the evaluation needs.

    tests/test_search.py
    import numpy as np
import pytest

from seekr import db, embed, index, rank, search


def test_rrf_rewards_agreement():
    fused, ranks = search.rrf({"bm25": [("a", 9), ("b", 5)], "vector": [("b", 0.9), ("c", 0.8)]})
    assert max(fused, key=fused.get) == "b"  # 2nd + 1st beats a single 1st place
    assert ranks["b"] == {"bm25": 2, "vector": 1}
    assert np.isclose(fused["a"], 1 / 61)


def test_highlight_marks_words_and_escapes_html():

    assert "&lt;b&gt;<mark>asyncio</mark>&lt;/b&gt;" in snippet
    assert "<mark>Tasks</mark>" in snippet


def test_freshness_fades_with_age():
    assert search.freshness("") == 0.5
    assert search.freshness("2000-01-01") < search.freshness("2020-01-01") < 1


def test_sigmoid_maps_logits_to_probabilities():
    assert np.isclose(search.sigmoid(0), 0.5)
    assert search.sigmoid(-10) < 0.001 < 0.999 < search.sigmoid(10)

  5. Create the evaluation module
    seekr/evaluate.py
    """Measure search quality on hand-labelled queries: MRR@10 and nDCG@10 for each way of searching."""
import json
import math
from pathlib import Path

from seekr import config

QUERIES_FILE = config.ROOT / "evals" / "queries.jsonl"
MODES = ["bm25", "vector", "hybrid", "full"]  # full = hybrid + cross-encoder relevance + final score


def reciprocal_rank(urls, relevant):
    """1/position of the first relevant result in the top 10, or 0 if there is none."""
    for position, url in enumerate(urls[:10], start=1):
        if url in relevant:
            return 1 / position
    return 0.0


def ndcg(urls, relevant):
    """Discounted gain of every relevant result (worth less further down), divided by the best possible."""
    gain = sum(1 / math.log2(position + 1) for position, url in enumerate(urls[:10], start=1) if url in relevant)
    ideal = sum(1 / math.log2(position + 1) for position in range(1, min(len(relevant), 10) + 1))
    return gain / ideal


def load_queries(path=QUERIES_FILE):
    return [json.loads(line) for line in Path(path).read_text().splitlines() if line.strip()]


def evaluate(engine, queries):
    """Returns {mode: (MRR@10, nDCG@10)}, averaged over all queries."""
    report = {}
    for mode in MODES:
        mrr = total_ndcg = 0.0
        for item in queries:
            urls = [result["url"] for result in engine.search(item["query"], limit=10, mode=mode)]
            mrr += reciprocal_rank(urls, set(item["relevant"]))
            total_ndcg += ndcg(urls, set(item["relevant"]))
        report[mode] = (mrr / len(queries), total_ndcg / len(queries))
    return report
    • MRR@10 (mean reciprocal rank): for each query, 1 divided by the position of the first correct result (1st place = 1.0, 2nd = 0.5, 3rd = 0.33, not in the top 10 = 0), averaged over all queries. It answers: "how quickly does the user see a right answer?"
    • nDCG@10 (normalized discounted cumulative gain): every correct result counts, but less the lower it is: position p is worth 1/log₂(p+1). That's divided by the best possible score for that query, so 1.0 means "all the right answers, at the very top". It rewards finding all relevant pages, not just the first.
    • We run the same queries through all four modes, so the comparison is fair.
  6. Test the metrics
    tests/test_evaluate.py
    import numpy as np

from seekr import evaluate


def test_reciprocal_rank():
    assert evaluate.reciprocal_rank(["a", "b", "c"], {"c"}) == 1 / 3
    assert evaluate.reciprocal_rank(["a", "b"], {"z"}) == 0


def test_ndcg_is_one_for_a_perfect_ranking_and_lower_otherwise():
    assert np.isclose(evaluate.ndcg(["r1", "r2", "x"], {"r1", "r2"}), 1.0)
    assert evaluate.ndcg(["x", "r1", "r2"], {"r1", "r2"}) < 1.0
    assert evaluate.ndcg(["x"], {"r1"}) == 0


def test_eval_file_is_valid():
    queries = evaluate.load_queries()
    assert len(queries) >= 20
    assert all(item["query"] and item["relevant"] for item in queries)
  7. Add the search and eval commands (whole updated file)
    seekr/__main__.py
    """Command-line entry point: python -m seekr <command>."""
import argparse
import asyncio

from seekr import config, crawler, db, embed, evaluate, extract, index, rank, search


def print_host_stats(conn):
    print(f"{'host':26} {'done':>5} {'queued':>7} {'failed':>7} {'error':>6} {'blocked':>8}")
    for r in db.host_stats(conn):
        print(f"{r['host']:26} {r['done']:5} {r['queued']:7} {r['failed']:7} {r['error']:6} {r['blocked']:8}")


def cmd_crawl(args):
    conn = db.connect()
    asyncio.run(crawler.crawl(config.read_seeds(), conn))
    print_host_stats(conn)


def cmd_index(args):
    conn = db.connect()
    print("Extracting text from crawled pages:")
    counts = extract.index_pages(conn)
    print(f"new: {counts['new']}  duplicates: {counts['duplicate']}  no main text: {counts['empty']}")
    print(f"Keyword index: added {index.build_index(conn)} chunks")
    print(f"Embedding chunks on {embed.best_device()}:")
    print(f"embedded: {embed.embed_chunks(conn)}")


def cmd_rank(args):
    conn = db.connect()
    pages, links = rank.compute_pagerank(conn)
    print(f"PageRank over {pages} pages and {links} links. Top 10:")
    top = conn.execute(
        "SELECT r.score, d.url FROM pagerank r JOIN documents d ON d.id = r.doc_id ORDER BY r.score DESC LIMIT 10"
    )
    for row in top:
        print(f"  {row['score']:.5f}  {row['url']}")


def cmd_search(args):
    engine = search.SearchEngine(db.connect())
    for i, result in enumerate(engine.search(" ".join(args.query)), start=1):
        print(f"{i:2}. [{result['relevance']:.2f}] {result['title']}\n    {result['url']}")


def cmd_eval(args):
    engine = search.SearchEngine(db.connect())
    queries = evaluate.load_queries()
    print(f"{len(queries)} queries   {'MRR@10':>7} {'nDCG@10':>8}")
    for mode, (mrr, ndcg) in evaluate.evaluate(engine, queries).items():
        print(f"{mode:18} {mrr:7.3f} {ndcg:8.3f}")


def cmd_stats(args):
    seeds = config.read_seeds()
    db_state = "exists" if config.DB_PATH.exists() else "not created yet"
    print(f"seeds:    {len(seeds)}")
    print(f"database: {config.DB_PATH} ({db_state})")
    if config.DB_PATH.exists():
        conn = db.connect()
        print_host_stats(conn)
        for table in ("pages", "documents", "chunks", "embeddings"):
            print(f"{table + ':':10} {conn.execute(f'SELECT COUNT(*) FROM {table}').fetchone()[0]}")


def not_built_yet(args):
    print(f"'{args.command}' is not built yet - see guide.html for the next chapter.")


COMMANDS = {
    "crawl": cmd_crawl,
    "index": cmd_index,
    "rank": cmd_rank,
    "serve": not_built_yet,
    "stats": cmd_stats,
    "search": cmd_search,
    "eval": cmd_eval,
}


def main(argv=None):
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog="seekr", description="Seekr search engine")
    sub = parser.add_subparsers(dest="command", required=True)
    for name in COMMANDS:
        command = sub.add_parser(name)
        if name == "search":
            command.add_argument("query", nargs="+")
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
    COMMANDS[args.command](args)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
    search takes words after it, so the parser gets an extra argument: nargs="+" means "one or more words", and we join them back into a query. [{result['relevance']:.2f}] prints the relevance score next to each result.

  10. Measure search quality
    python -m seekr eval
    22 queries    MRR@10  nDCG@10
bm25                 0.624    0.630
vector               0.678    0.712
hybrid               0.652    0.694
full                 0.774    0.782

What the measurements taught us

The first version used the planned settings: the bigger BAAI/bge-reranker-base model and weights of 0.75 relevance / 0.15 PageRank / 0.05 freshness / 0.05 exact match. It scored worse than plain vector search (MRR 0.522). Without the evaluation we'd never have known. What we found:

ExperimentMRR@10nDCG@10Lesson
bge-reranker-base, planned weights0.5220.563Starting point: worse than no reranker at all.
bge-reranker-base, relevance only0.6480.648The extra signals were hurting.
MiniLM-L6, relevance only0.7330.716The smaller model, trained on web-search queries, is better and 3× faster (0.4 s vs 1.2 s for 50 passages).
MiniLM, PageRank 0.15 → 0.050.7480.743PageRank pushed hubs (and the Rust book's whole-book print.html) above the real answers. A little authority helps; a lot hurts.
Thin-page penalty: <100 words ×0.5 → <50 words ×0.8+0.04+0.06Short chapter introductions (Rust's ch04-00) are real answers.
Freshness 0.05 → 0 (final)0.7740.782Docs say "last updated today" on every page, so freshness favoured whatever was rebuilt recently.
Rerank top 50 instead of 300.7140.723More candidates gave the reranker more chances to promote a wrong page.
Near-duplicate penalty (embedding similarity > 0.95)±0±0No measurable effect, so we removed it to keep the code small. Exact duplicates are already dropped in Chapter 3.
An honest caveat: 22 queries is a small evaluation, and we tuned on the same queries we report. Treat these numbers as "clearly better" rather than precise. To trust a change, add new queries to evals/queries.jsonl that you didn't tune on, and run eval again.

Checkpoint

python -m seekr eval

The full row should be clearly the best, around MRR 0.77 and nDCG 0.78 on our crawl. Your numbers will differ a little if your crawl differs.

no such table: pagerank
Run python -m seekr rank (Chapter 6) before searching.
KeyError or empty results for every query
Run python -m seekr index, so the keyword index and embeddings exist.
full is worse than vector
That's what we saw first. Check the weights in config.py, and print result["signals"] for a bad query to see which signal pushed the wrong page up.
Every search takes several seconds
The reranker is running on the CPU, or you switched to BAAI/bge-reranker-base. Check embed.best_device() and SEEKR_RERANK_MODEL.

Chapter 8 · REST API ready

Put the search engine behind a web API, so any program can call it: with API keys, a rate limit per key, and interactive documentation.

What we're building: a small web server. A program sends GET /api/v1/search?q=rust+ownership with its key, and gets JSON back: the same results, relevance scores and signals you saw in Chapter 7. That's how other apps (a chatbot, an IDE plugin, a script) integrate with Seekr. The web UI in Chapter 9 sits on the same server.

Why FastAPI? You write ordinary Python functions with type hints, and FastAPI turns them into web endpoints. It checks the inputs for you (a limit of 500 is rejected before your code runs) and generates interactive docs at /docs automatically.

Why API keys? Every search costs GPU time. A key tells us who is calling, so we can rate-limit each caller and revoke a key that misbehaves. We store only a hash of each key, so even someone who copies the database can't use the keys.

  1. Add FastAPI and uvicorn to the requirements
    requirements.txt
    pytest==8.3.4

trafilatura==2.3.1
torch==2.15.0
sentence-transformers==6.3.0
numpy==2.5.4
fastapi==0.163.0
uvicorn==0.54.0
    FastAPI is the web framework. uvicorn is the web server that runs it: it listens on a port and hands each HTTP request to FastAPI.

  3. Add the API settings to seekr/config.py (whole updated file)
    seekr/config.py
    """All settings in one place. Override any of them with an environment variable."""
import os
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent

DB_PATH = Path(os.environ.get("SEEKR_DB", ROOT / "data" / "seekr.db"))
SEEDS_FILE = Path(os.environ.get("SEEKR_SEEDS", ROOT / "seeds.txt"))
USER_AGENT = os.environ.get("SEEKR_USER_AGENT", "SeekrBot/0.1")
HOST = os.environ.get("SEEKR_HOST", "127.0.0.1")
PORT = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_PORT", "8000"))

# Crawler
MAX_DEPTH = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_MAX_DEPTH", "3"))
MAX_PAGES_PER_DOMAIN = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_MAX_PAGES_PER_DOMAIN", "300"))
CRAWL_DELAY = float(os.environ.get("SEEKR_CRAWL_DELAY", "1.0"))  # seconds between requests to one host
REQUEST_TIMEOUT = float(os.environ.get("SEEKR_REQUEST_TIMEOUT", "15"))
MAX_PAGE_BYTES = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_MAX_PAGE_BYTES", "2000000"))
RETRIES = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_RETRIES", "2"))

# Extraction
CHUNK_WORDS = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_CHUNK_WORDS", "300"))
CHUNK_OVERLAP = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_CHUNK_OVERLAP", "50"))

# Embeddings (runs on the GPU when there is one)
EMBED_MODEL = os.environ.get("SEEKR_EMBED_MODEL", "BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5")
EMBED_BATCH = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_EMBED_BATCH", "64"))
DEVICE = os.environ.get("SEEKR_DEVICE", "")  # "", "cuda", "mps" or "cpu"; "" picks the best one

# Search and relevance scoring
CANDIDATES = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_CANDIDATES", "100"))  # from each of BM25 and vectors
RERANK_TOP = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_RERANK_TOP", "30"))  # pages the cross-encoder reads per query
# MiniLM beat BAAI/bge-reranker-base on our eval (MRR 0.733 vs 0.648) and is 3x faster; try either.
RERANK_MODEL = os.environ.get("SEEKR_RERANK_MODEL", "cross-encoder/ms-marco-MiniLM-L-6-v2")
RERANK_MAX_TOKENS = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_RERANK_MAX_TOKENS", "384"))
# Tuned on evals/queries.jsonl. Freshness is 0: docs show "last updated today", so dates mislead.
W_RELEVANCE, W_PAGERANK, W_FRESHNESS, W_EXACT = 0.90, 0.05, 0.0, 0.05
THIN_PAGE_WORDS, THIN_PAGE_PENALTY = 50, 0.8

# REST API
RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE", "60"))  # per API key
CORS_ORIGINS = os.environ.get("SEEKR_CORS_ORIGINS", "*").split(",")  # websites allowed to call the API


def read_seeds(path=None):
    """Return the seed URLs, skipping blank lines and # comments."""
    lines = Path(path or SEEDS_FILE).read_text().splitlines()
    return [line.strip() for line in lines if line.strip() and not line.startswith("#")]
    60 searches per minute per key by default. CORS_ORIGINS lists which websites may call the API from a browser (* means any). .split(",") lets you set several, e.g. SEEKR_CORS_ORIGINS=https://a.com,https://b.com.
  4. Create the API module
    seekr/api.py
    """REST API: search Seekr from any program, with API keys and a rate limit per key."""
import hashlib
import secrets
import time
from collections import defaultdict, deque
from contextlib import asynccontextmanager
from datetime import datetime, timezone

from fastapi import APIRouter, Depends, FastAPI, HTTPException, Query, Request
from fastapi.middleware.cors import CORSMiddleware
from fastapi.security import HTTPAuthorizationCredentials, HTTPBearer
from pydantic import BaseModel

from seekr import config, db, embed, search
from seekr.urls import normalize_url

KEYS_SCHEMA = """CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS api_keys (
    key_hash TEXT PRIMARY KEY, name TEXT NOT NULL, is_admin INTEGER NOT NULL, created_at TEXT NOT NULL);"""


def hash_key(key):
    return hashlib.sha256(key.encode()).hexdigest()


def create_key(conn, name, admin=False):
    """Make a new random key and store only its hash. The key itself is shown once, then forgotten."""
    conn.executescript(KEYS_SCHEMA)
    key = "sk_" + secrets.token_urlsafe(24)
    created_at = datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
    conn.execute("INSERT INTO api_keys VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?)", (hash_key(key), name, int(admin), created_at))
    conn.commit()
    return key


class RateLimiter:
    """Allows `limit` requests per key in any 60-second window (a "sliding window")."""

    def __init__(self, limit):
        self.limit, self.hits = limit, defaultdict(deque)

    def allow(self, key, now=None):
        now = time.monotonic() if now is None else now
        hits = self.hits[key]
        while hits and hits[0] <= now - 60:
            hits.popleft()
        if len(hits) >= self.limit:
            return False
        hits.append(now)
        return True


class Signals(BaseModel):
    bm25_rank: int | None
    vector_rank: int | None
    relevance: float | None
    pagerank: float | None
    freshness: float | None


class Result(BaseModel):
    url: str
    title: str
    snippet: str  # HTML: page text is escaped, query words are wrapped in <mark>
    relevance: float | None
    final_score: float | None
    signals: Signals


class SearchResponse(BaseModel):
    query: str
    took_ms: int
    results: list[Result]


class Page(BaseModel):
    url: str
    title: str | None
    description: str | None
    published: str | None
    language: str | None
    word_count: int
    pagerank: float | None
    chunks: int


class Seeds(BaseModel):
    urls: list[str]


bearer = HTTPBearer(auto_error=False)
router = APIRouter(prefix="/api/v1")


async def api_key(request: Request, credentials: HTTPAuthorizationCredentials | None = Depends(bearer)):
    """Every endpoint depends on this: a valid key that is under its rate limit.
    All handlers are `async` so they run on one thread: a SQLite connection may only be used by the thread
    that opened it."""
    if credentials is None:
        raise HTTPException(401, "Missing API key. Send the header: Authorization: Bearer <key>")
    key_hash = hash_key(credentials.credentials)
    row = request.app.state.conn.execute("SELECT name, is_admin FROM api_keys WHERE key_hash = ?", (key_hash,)).fetchone()
    if row is None:
        raise HTTPException(401, "Invalid API key")
    if not request.app.state.limiter.allow(key_hash):
        raise HTTPException(429, "Rate limit exceeded, try again in a minute", headers={"Retry-After": "60"})
    return row


async def admin_key(key=Depends(api_key)):
    if not key["is_admin"]:
        raise HTTPException(403, "This endpoint needs an admin key")
    return key


@router.get("/search", response_model=SearchResponse)
async def search_pages(
    request: Request,
    q: str = Query(min_length=1, max_length=200, description="What to search for"),
    limit: int = Query(10, ge=1, le=30),
    offset: int = Query(0, ge=0, le=29),
    site: str | None = Query(None, description="Only results from this host, e.g. docs.python.org"),
    key=Depends(api_key),
):
    started = time.perf_counter()
    results = request.app.state.engine.search(q, limit=limit, offset=offset, site=site)
    return {"query": q, "took_ms": round((time.perf_counter() - started) * 1000), "results": results}


@router.get("/page", response_model=Page)
async def page_info(request: Request, url: str, key=Depends(api_key)):
    row = request.app.state.conn.execute(
        """SELECT d.url, d.title, d.description, d.published, d.language, d.word_count, r.score AS pagerank,
                  (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM chunks c WHERE c.doc_id = d.id) AS chunks
           FROM documents d LEFT JOIN pagerank r ON r.doc_id = d.id WHERE d.url = ? AND d.duplicate_of IS NULL""",
        (url,),
    ).fetchone()
    if row is None:
        raise HTTPException(404, "That URL is not in the index")
    return dict(row)


@router.get("/stats")
async def stats(request: Request, key=Depends(api_key)):
    count = lambda sql: request.app.state.conn.execute(sql).fetchone()[0]  # noqa: E731
    return {
        "pages": count("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM pages"),
        "documents": count("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM documents WHERE duplicate_of IS NULL AND word_count > 0"),
        "chunks": count("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM chunks"),
        "embeddings": count("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM embeddings"),
        "device": embed.best_device(),
    }


@router.post("/crawl/seeds")
async def add_seeds(body: Seeds, key=Depends(admin_key)):
    """Add start URLs for the next crawl. Crawling itself runs from the command line."""
    urls = [normalize_url(url) for url in body.urls]
    if None in urls:
        raise HTTPException(422, "Every seed must be an http(s) URL")
    known = set(config.read_seeds())
    added = [url for url in dict.fromkeys(urls) if url not in known]
    with open(config.SEEDS_FILE, "a") as seeds_file:
        seeds_file.writelines(f"{url}\n" for url in added)
    return {"added": added, "next": "python -m seekr crawl && python -m seekr index && python -m seekr rank"}


def create_app(db_path=None):
    @asynccontextmanager
    async def lifespan(app):
        app.state.conn = db.connect(db_path)
        app.state.conn.executescript(KEYS_SCHEMA)
        app.state.engine = search.SearchEngine(app.state.conn)  # loads vectors + models once, at startup
        app.state.engine.search("warm up")  # the GPU compiles its kernels on first use; do it before users arrive
        app.state.limiter = RateLimiter(config.RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE)
        yield
        app.state.conn.close()

    app = FastAPI(title="Seekr API", version="0.1", lifespan=lifespan)
    app.add_middleware(CORSMiddleware, allow_origins=config.CORS_ORIGINS, allow_methods=["GET", "POST"],
                       allow_headers=["Authorization", "Content-Type"])
    app.include_router(router)
    return app
    • Routes: @router.get("/search") is a decorator: it registers the function below it as the handler for GET /api/v1/search (the router adds the /api/v1 prefix). Putting the version in the URL means we can change the API later as /api/v2 without breaking existing users.
    • Query parameters with validation: limit: int = Query(10, ge=1, le=30) means "an integer from the URL, default 10, at least 1, at most 30". FastAPI converts "5" to 5 and answers 422 Unprocessable Entity with a clear message for anything invalid, before our function runs.
    • Pydantic models (class Result(BaseModel)) describe the shape of the JSON. response_model=SearchResponse makes FastAPI check every response against it and document it in /docs. int | None means "a number, or null" (BM25 may not have found that page).
    • Dependencies: key=Depends(api_key) tells FastAPI "before running this handler, run api_key() and pass me its result". If api_key raises HTTPException(401), the handler never runs. One dependency protects every endpoint, and admin_key builds on it by depending on api_key itself.
    • HTTPBearer reads the standard header Authorization: Bearer sk_.... It also adds the "Authorize" button in /docs.
    • API-key hashing: secrets.token_urlsafe(24) makes 24 random bytes (192 bits) from the operating system's secure random generator, so keys can't be guessed. We store sha256(key), and on each request we hash the key we receive and look up the hash. Passwords need a slow hash like bcrypt, because people pick guessable ones. A long random key can't be guessed, so a fast hash is enough.
    • Status codes: 401 means "who are you?" (missing or wrong key), 403 means "I know you, but you may not do this" (not an admin), 404 means not found, 422 means bad input, and 429 means too many requests. Retry-After: 60 tells polite clients when to try again.
    • Sliding-window rate limit: for each key, a deque (a list that's fast to change at both ends) holds the times of its recent requests. On each request we drop times older than 60 seconds, then allow the request only if fewer than limit remain. time.monotonic() is a clock that never jumps backwards, even if the system time changes. It lives in memory, so restarting the server resets it, which is fine for one machine.
    • CORS: by default, browsers block a web page on myapp.com from reading responses from localhost:8000. CORSMiddleware adds the Access-Control-Allow-Origin header that says "this site may read me".
    • lifespan runs once when the server starts (everything before yield) and once when it stops (after). We open the database, load the vectors and models onto the GPU, and run one warm-up search. The GPU compiles its kernels the first time they run, and without the warm-up the first user waited 1.5 s instead of about 0.15 s.
    • Why async def everywhere? FastAPI runs plain def handlers in a pool of worker threads, but a SQLite connection may only be used by the thread that opened it. async def handlers all run on the server's single main thread, the same one that opened the connection in lifespan. One search at a time is fine for a laptop. A bigger deployment would open one connection per thread instead.
    • create_app(db_path) is a factory: it builds a fresh app, so tests can point it at a small test database.
    • dict.fromkeys(urls) removes duplicates while keeping the order. We only record new seeds here, because a crawl takes minutes and doesn't belong inside a web request.
  5. Add the serve and keys commands (whole updated file)
    seekr/__main__.py
    """Command-line entry point: python -m seekr <command>."""
import argparse
import asyncio

from seekr import api, config, crawler, db, embed, evaluate, extract, index, rank, search


def print_host_stats(conn):
    print(f"{'host':26} {'done':>5} {'queued':>7} {'failed':>7} {'error':>6} {'blocked':>8}")
    for r in db.host_stats(conn):
        print(f"{r['host']:26} {r['done']:5} {r['queued']:7} {r['failed']:7} {r['error']:6} {r['blocked']:8}")


def cmd_crawl(args):
    conn = db.connect()
    asyncio.run(crawler.crawl(config.read_seeds(), conn))
    print_host_stats(conn)


def cmd_index(args):
    conn = db.connect()
    print("Extracting text from crawled pages:")
    counts = extract.index_pages(conn)
    print(f"new: {counts['new']}  duplicates: {counts['duplicate']}  no main text: {counts['empty']}")
    print(f"Keyword index: added {index.build_index(conn)} chunks")
    print(f"Embedding chunks on {embed.best_device()}:")
    print(f"embedded: {embed.embed_chunks(conn)}")


def cmd_rank(args):
    conn = db.connect()
    pages, links = rank.compute_pagerank(conn)
    print(f"PageRank over {pages} pages and {links} links. Top 10:")
    top = conn.execute(
        "SELECT r.score, d.url FROM pagerank r JOIN documents d ON d.id = r.doc_id ORDER BY r.score DESC LIMIT 10"
    )
    for row in top:
        print(f"  {row['score']:.5f}  {row['url']}")


def cmd_search(args):
    engine = search.SearchEngine(db.connect())
    for i, result in enumerate(engine.search(" ".join(args.query)), start=1):
        print(f"{i:2}. [{result['relevance']:.2f}] {result['title']}\n    {result['url']}")


def cmd_eval(args):
    engine = search.SearchEngine(db.connect())
    queries = evaluate.load_queries()
    print(f"{len(queries)} queries   {'MRR@10':>7} {'nDCG@10':>8}")
    for mode, (mrr, ndcg) in evaluate.evaluate(engine, queries).items():
        print(f"{mode:18} {mrr:7.3f} {ndcg:8.3f}")


def cmd_serve(args):
    import uvicorn

    print(f"Seekr API on http://{config.HOST}:{config.PORT}  (interactive docs: /docs)")
    uvicorn.run(api.create_app(), host=config.HOST, port=config.PORT)


def cmd_keys(args):
    key = api.create_key(db.connect(), args.name, admin=args.admin)
    print(f"New {'admin ' if args.admin else ''}API key for {args.name!r} (shown once, store it safely):\n{key}")


def cmd_stats(args):
    seeds = config.read_seeds()
    db_state = "exists" if config.DB_PATH.exists() else "not created yet"
    print(f"seeds:    {len(seeds)}")
    print(f"database: {config.DB_PATH} ({db_state})")
    if config.DB_PATH.exists():
        conn = db.connect()
        print_host_stats(conn)
        for table in ("pages", "documents", "chunks", "embeddings"):
            print(f"{table + ':':10} {conn.execute(f'SELECT COUNT(*) FROM {table}').fetchone()[0]}")


COMMANDS = {
    "crawl": cmd_crawl,
    "index": cmd_index,
    "rank": cmd_rank,
    "serve": cmd_serve,
    "stats": cmd_stats,
    "search": cmd_search,
    "eval": cmd_eval,
    "keys": cmd_keys,
}


def main(argv=None):
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog="seekr", description="Seekr search engine")
    sub = parser.add_subparsers(dest="command", required=True)
    for name in COMMANDS:
        command = sub.add_parser(name)
        if name == "search":
            command.add_argument("query", nargs="+")
        if name == "keys":
            command.add_argument("action", choices=["create"])
            command.add_argument("name", help="who or what the key is for")
            command.add_argument("--admin", action="store_true", help="may add seeds")
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
    COMMANDS[args.command](args)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
    Every command is built now, so the not_built_yet placeholder from Chapter 1 is gone. import uvicorn sits inside cmd_serve, so other commands don't pay for loading it. action="store_true" makes --admin a simple on/off flag. {args.name!r} prints the name with quotes around it.
  6. Write the API tests
    tests/test_api.py
    import pytest
from fastapi.testclient import TestClient
from test_search import PAGES

from seekr import api, config, db, embed, index, rank


@pytest.fixture(scope="module")
def setup(tmp_path_factory):
    """A tiny index (the 3 pages from test_search), a normal key and an admin key, and a running app."""
    path = tmp_path_factory.mktemp("api") / "t.db"
    conn = db.connect(path)
    for doc_id, (url, (title, text)) in enumerate(PAGES.items(), start=1):
        conn.execute("INSERT INTO documents (id, url, title, language, word_count, published) VALUES (?, ?, ?, 'en', ?, '')",
                     (doc_id, url, title, len(text.split())))
        conn.execute("INSERT INTO chunks (id, doc_id, position, text) VALUES (?, ?, 0, ?)", (doc_id, doc_id, text))
        db.save_page(conn, url, url, 200, title, "", list(PAGES))
    index.build_index(conn)
    embed.embed_chunks(conn)
    rank.compute_pagerank(conn)
    user, admin = api.create_key(conn, "tests"), api.create_key(conn, "admin", admin=True)
    with TestClient(api.create_app(path)) as client:
        yield client, {"Authorization": f"Bearer {user}"}, {"Authorization": f"Bearer {admin}"}, conn


def test_keys_are_required(setup):
    client, *_ = setup
    assert client.get("/api/v1/search?q=rust").status_code == 401
    assert client.get("/api/v1/search?q=rust", headers={"Authorization": "Bearer sk_wrong"}).status_code == 401


def test_only_the_hash_is_stored(setup):
    client, user, _, conn = setup
    raw_key = user["Authorization"].split()[1]
    stored = [row[0] for row in conn.execute("SELECT key_hash FROM api_keys")]
    assert raw_key not in stored and api.hash_key(raw_key) in stored


def test_search(setup):
    client, user, *_ = setup
    response = client.get("/api/v1/search", params={"q": "free memory without garbage collector"}, headers=user)
    assert response.status_code == 200
    body = response.json()
    assert body["results"][0]["url"] == "https://x.com/rust-ownership"
    assert 0 <= body["results"][0]["relevance"] <= 1
    assert "pagerank" in body["results"][0]["signals"]


def test_bad_parameters_are_rejected(setup):
    client, user, *_ = setup
    assert client.get("/api/v1/search?q=rust&limit=500", headers=user).status_code == 422
    assert client.get("/api/v1/search?q=", headers=user).status_code == 422


def test_page_and_stats(setup):
    client, user, *_ = setup
    page = client.get("/api/v1/page", params={"url": "https://x.com/cooking"}, headers=user).json()
    assert page["title"] == "Pasta recipes" and page["chunks"] == 1
    assert client.get("/api/v1/page", params={"url": "https://nope.com/"}, headers=user).status_code == 404
    assert client.get("/api/v1/stats", headers=user).json()["documents"] == 3


def test_seeds_need_an_admin_key(setup, tmp_path, monkeypatch):
    client, user, admin, _ = setup
    seeds = tmp_path / "seeds.txt"
    seeds.write_text("https://a.com/\n")
    monkeypatch.setattr(config, "SEEDS_FILE", seeds)
    body = {"urls": ["https://a.com/", "https://B.com/docs/", "https://B.com/docs/"]}
    assert client.post("/api/v1/crawl/seeds", json=body, headers=user).status_code == 403
    assert client.post("/api/v1/crawl/seeds", json=body, headers=admin).json()["added"] == ["https://b.com/docs/"]
    assert seeds.read_text() == "https://a.com/\nhttps://b.com/docs/\n"
    assert client.post("/api/v1/crawl/seeds", json={"urls": ["ftp://x"]}, headers=admin).status_code == 422


def test_cors_header(setup):
    client, user, *_ = setup
    response = client.get("/api/v1/stats", headers={**user, "Origin": "https://myapp.example"})
    assert response.headers["access-control-allow-origin"] == "*"


def test_rate_limiter_uses_a_sliding_window():
    limiter = api.RateLimiter(limit=2)
    assert limiter.allow("k", now=0) and limiter.allow("k", now=10)
    assert not limiter.allow("k", now=20)
    assert limiter.allow("other", now=20)  # every key has its own budget
    assert limiter.allow("k", now=61)  # the request at t=0 has left the window


def test_rate_limit_returns_429(setup):
    client, user, *_ = setup
    client.app.state.limiter = api.RateLimiter(limit=1)
    assert client.get("/api/v1/stats", headers=user).status_code == 200
    response = client.get("/api/v1/stats", headers=user)
    assert response.status_code == 429 and response.headers["retry-after"] == "60"
    • TestClient calls the app directly, with no real server and no network. Using it in a with block runs lifespan, just like a real start-up.
    • from test_search import PAGES reuses the three test pages from Chapter 7. pytest puts the tests/ folder on Python's import path, so one test file can import another.
    • yield inside a fixture hands the client to the tests, then continues (closing the app) when the tests in the file are done.
    • The rate-limiter test passes now= explicitly, so it can check a 60-second window without waiting 60 seconds.

    python3 -m json.tool just pretty-prints the JSON. Look at signals: BM25 didn't even have this page in its top 100 (null), because the index pages crowded it out. Checkpoint

curl -s -H "Authorization: Bearer $KEY" http://127.0.0.1:8000/api/v1/stats
{"pages":4086,"documents":3884,"chunks":28841,"embeddings":28841,"device":"mps"}

And without a key: curl -s http://127.0.0.1:8000/api/v1/stats should print {"detail":"Missing API key. Send the header: Authorization: Bearer <key>"}.

sqlite3.ProgrammingError: SQLite objects created in a thread can only be used in that same thread
A handler or dependency is a plain def. Make it async def (see the explanation above). We hit exactly this while building this chapter.
{"detail":"Invalid API key"}
Check that $KEY is set in this terminal (echo $KEY), and that the server uses the same database you created the key in.
[Errno 48] Address already in use
Something else is on port 8000, maybe an old server. Stop it, or pick another port: SEEKR_PORT=8001 python -m seekr serve.
no such table: pagerank / embeddings
The server needs a finished index: run python -m seekr index and python -m seekr rank first.
A browser app gets "blocked by CORS policy"
Add your app's address to SEEKR_CORS_ORIGINS and restart the server.

Chapter 9 · Web UI ready

A clean search page for humans: one box, fast results, a relevance badge on every result, dark mode, and a layout that works on a phone. Three plain files, no build step.

What we're building: the page you see at http://127.0.0.1:8000/. It's static HTML, CSS and JavaScript served by the same FastAPI server. The browser downloads the page once, then the JavaScript asks the server for results with fetch() and draws them, without reloading the page.

Why a second search endpoint? Anything inside a web page is public: anyone can press "View source". If we put an API key in app.js, the whole internet would have it. So the page calls /ui/search, which needs no key. Instead of a per-key limit it has a per-visitor limit (30 searches a minute per IP address). The /api/v1 endpoints still require keys, for programs that need more, or the extra endpoints.

Why no framework (React, Vue…)? The page has one form, one list and two buttons. Plain JavaScript does that in about 120 lines, with nothing to install or compile. You can read every line.

  1. Add the website setting to seekr/config.py (whole updated file)
    seekr/config.py
    """All settings in one place. Override any of them with an environment variable."""
import os
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent

DB_PATH = Path(os.environ.get("SEEKR_DB", ROOT / "data" / "seekr.db"))
SEEDS_FILE = Path(os.environ.get("SEEKR_SEEDS", ROOT / "seeds.txt"))
USER_AGENT = os.environ.get("SEEKR_USER_AGENT", "SeekrBot/0.1")
HOST = os.environ.get("SEEKR_HOST", "127.0.0.1")
PORT = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_PORT", "8000"))

# Crawler
MAX_DEPTH = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_MAX_DEPTH", "3"))
MAX_PAGES_PER_DOMAIN = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_MAX_PAGES_PER_DOMAIN", "300"))
CRAWL_DELAY = float(os.environ.get("SEEKR_CRAWL_DELAY", "1.0"))  # seconds between requests to one host
REQUEST_TIMEOUT = float(os.environ.get("SEEKR_REQUEST_TIMEOUT", "15"))
MAX_PAGE_BYTES = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_MAX_PAGE_BYTES", "2000000"))
RETRIES = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_RETRIES", "2"))

# Extraction
CHUNK_WORDS = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_CHUNK_WORDS", "300"))
CHUNK_OVERLAP = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_CHUNK_OVERLAP", "50"))

# Embeddings (runs on the GPU when there is one)
EMBED_MODEL = os.environ.get("SEEKR_EMBED_MODEL", "BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5")
EMBED_BATCH = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_EMBED_BATCH", "64"))
DEVICE = os.environ.get("SEEKR_DEVICE", "")  # "", "cuda", "mps" or "cpu"; "" picks the best one

# Search and relevance scoring
CANDIDATES = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_CANDIDATES", "100"))  # from each of BM25 and vectors
RERANK_TOP = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_RERANK_TOP", "30"))  # pages the cross-encoder reads per query
# MiniLM beat BAAI/bge-reranker-base on our eval (MRR 0.733 vs 0.648) and is 3x faster; try either.
RERANK_MODEL = os.environ.get("SEEKR_RERANK_MODEL", "cross-encoder/ms-marco-MiniLM-L-6-v2")
RERANK_MAX_TOKENS = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_RERANK_MAX_TOKENS", "384"))
# Tuned on evals/queries.jsonl. Freshness is 0: docs show "last updated today", so dates mislead.
W_RELEVANCE, W_PAGERANK, W_FRESHNESS, W_EXACT = 0.90, 0.05, 0.0, 0.05
THIN_PAGE_WORDS, THIN_PAGE_PENALTY = 50, 0.8

# REST API
RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE", "60"))  # per API key
CORS_ORIGINS = os.environ.get("SEEKR_CORS_ORIGINS", "*").split(",")  # websites allowed to call the API

# Website
UI_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_UI_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE", "30"))  # per visitor IP


def read_seeds(path=None):
    """Return the seed URLs, skipping blank lines and # comments."""
    lines = Path(path or SEEDS_FILE).read_text().splitlines()
    return [line.strip() for line in lines if line.strip() and not line.startswith("#")]
    One new setting at the end: how many searches per minute one visitor may run from the web page.
  2. Create the website module
    seekr/web.py
    """The search website: the files in web/ plus a key-less search endpoint for the browser."""
from fastapi import HTTPException, Query, Request
from fastapi.staticfiles import StaticFiles

from seekr import api, config


def create_site(db_path=None):
    """The API app from Chapter 8, plus the website. A browser can't keep a secret key, so the page calls
    /ui/search, which needs no key but is rate-limited per visitor (IP address) instead."""
    app = api.create_app(db_path)
    app.state.ui_limiter = api.RateLimiter(config.UI_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE)

    @app.get("/ui/search", response_model=api.SearchResponse, include_in_schema=False)
    async def ui_search(request: Request, q: str = Query(min_length=1, max_length=200), offset: int = Query(0, ge=0, le=29)):
        if not request.app.state.ui_limiter.allow(request.client.host):
            raise HTTPException(429, "Too many searches. Wait a minute and try again.", headers={"Retry-After": "60"})
        return await api.search_pages(request, q=q, limit=10, offset=offset, site=None, key=None)

    app.mount("/", StaticFiles(directory=config.ROOT / "web", html=True), name="web")  # last: API routes win
    return app
    • create_site starts from the API app of Chapter 8 and adds two things, so api.py doesn't change at all.
    • /ui/search reuses the API's search_pages function directly (it's just a Python function), with a fixed page size of 10 and no key. request.client.host is the visitor's IP address, which is what we rate-limit. include_in_schema=False keeps it out of the public /docs, because it's meant for our page, not for apps.
    • StaticFiles(directory=..., html=True) serves the files in web/ as they are. html=True means a request for / returns index.html.
    • Order matters: app.mount("/", ...) catches every path, so it's added last. FastAPI checks routes in the order they were added, so /api/v1/..., /ui/search and /docs still win.
  3. Create the page
    web/index.html
    <!doctype html>
<html lang="en">
<head>
  <meta charset="utf-8">
  <meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width, initial-scale=1">
  <title>Seekr</title>
  <link rel="icon" href="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg' viewBox='0 0 32 32'%3E%3Ccircle cx='13' cy='13' r='8' fill='none' stroke='%232f6bff' stroke-width='4'/%3E%3Cpath d='M19 19l8 8' stroke='%232f6bff' stroke-width='4' stroke-linecap='round'/%3E%3C/svg%3E">
  <link rel="stylesheet" href="style.css">
</head>
<body>
  <button id="theme" class="theme" type="button" aria-label="Switch light or dark mode">◐</button>
  <main>
    <a class="wordmark" href="/" aria-label="Seekr home">Seek<span>r</span></a>
    <p class="tagline">Search developer docs and engineering blogs. No SEO spam.</p>
    <form id="search-form" role="search">
      <input id="q" type="search" placeholder="Search docs and blogs…" autocomplete="off" autofocus aria-label="Search">
      <button type="submit">Search</button>
    </form>
    <p class="hint">Press <kbd>/</kbd> to search from anywhere</p>
    <p id="status" class="status" aria-live="polite"></p>
    <ol id="results" class="results"></ol>
    <nav id="pager" class="pager" hidden>
      <button id="prev" type="button">← Previous</button>
      <span id="page-label"></span>
      <button id="next" type="button">Next →</button>
    </nav>
  </main>
  <footer>Open source · runs on your own machine · <a href="/docs">API</a></footer>
  <script src="app.js"></script>
</body>
</html>
    • <meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width"> tells phones to use their real width instead of pretending to be a 980-pixel desktop. Without it, mobile layouts don't work.
    • The favicon (the little tab icon) is an SVG magnifying glass written straight into the href as a data URL, so we don't need another file.
    • role="search", aria-label and aria-live="polite" help screen readers. aria-live makes them announce "Results 1–10" when it changes.
    • hidden on the pager hides it until there's more than one page.
    • <script src="app.js"> sits at the end of <body>, so every element exists by the time the script looks for it.

  button[type="submit"] { height: 44px; }
}
    • CSS variables (--bg, --text, …) hold every color, and the rest of the file only uses var(--bg). Dark mode just redefines the variables.
    • Dark mode, two ways: @media (prefers-color-scheme: dark) follows the computer's setting, unless the visitor chose light with the button (:not([data-theme="light"])). [data-theme="dark"] applies when they chose dark.
    • [hidden] { display: none !important; } fixes a classic trap we actually hit: .pager { display: flex } was stronger than the hidden attribute, so Previous/Next showed on the empty home page.
    • body.searching is a class the script switches on once there's a query. The big centered logo shrinks and moves to the top, like a classic search engine.
    • .url { min-width: 0 }: flex items refuse to shrink below their content by default, so one long URL made the whole page scroll sideways on phones. overflow-wrap: anywhere lets long URLs break.
    • @media (max-width: 520px) stacks the search box above the button on narrow screens.
    • color-mix(in srgb, var(--accent) 25%, transparent) makes a soft, see-through version of the accent color for the focus ring.
  5. Create the script
    web/app.js
    // Seekr's search page: reads the query from the address bar, asks the server, shows the results.
const PAGE_SIZE = 10;
const MAX_RESULTS = 30; // the reranker scores the best 30 pages per query

const form = document.getElementById("search-form");
const input = document.getElementById("q");
const statusLine = document.getElementById("status");
const resultList = document.getElementById("results");
const pager = document.getElementById("pager");
const prevButton = document.getElementById("prev");
const nextButton = document.getElementById("next");
const pageLabel = document.getElementById("page-label");

function readUrl() {
  const params = new URLSearchParams(location.search);
  const page = Math.max(1, parseInt(params.get("page"), 10) || 1);
  return { query: params.get("q") || "", page };
}

async function search(query, page) {
  input.value = query;
  document.body.classList.toggle("searching", query !== "");
  document.title = query ? `${query} - Seekr` : "Seekr";
  resultList.replaceChildren();
  pager.hidden = true;
  statusLine.textContent = query ? "Searching…" : "";
  if (!query) return;
  try {
    const offset = (page - 1) * PAGE_SIZE;
    const response = await fetch(`/ui/search?q=${encodeURIComponent(query)}&offset=${offset}`);
    const data = await response.json();
    if (!response.ok) throw new Error(typeof data.detail === "string" ? data.detail : "That search didn't work.");
    showResults(data, page);
  } catch (error) {
    statusLine.textContent = error.message;
  }
}

function showResults(data, page) {
  const first = (page - 1) * PAGE_SIZE + 1;
  const last = first + data.results.length - 1;
  statusLine.textContent = data.results.length
    ? `Results ${first}–${last} · ${data.took_ms} ms`
    : `No results for “${data.query}”.`;
  resultList.append(...data.results.map(resultItem));
  pageLabel.textContent = `Page ${page}`;
  prevButton.disabled = page === 1;
  nextButton.disabled = data.results.length < PAGE_SIZE || page * PAGE_SIZE >= MAX_RESULTS;
  pager.hidden = prevButton.disabled && nextButton.disabled;
}

function resultItem(result) {
  const item = document.createElement("li");
  item.className = "result";
  const title = document.createElement("a");
  title.className = "title";
  title.href = result.url;
  title.textContent = result.title; // textContent never interprets HTML, so a page title can't inject code
  const meta = document.createElement("div");
  meta.className = "meta";
  const badge = document.createElement("span");
  badge.className = `badge ${relevanceLevel(result.relevance)}`;
  badge.textContent = `${Math.round(result.relevance * 100)}% relevant`;
  badge.title = "How well this page answers your query, according to the relevance model";
  const url = document.createElement("span");
  url.className = "url";
  url.textContent = result.url.replace(/^https?:\/\//, "").replace(/\/$/, "");
  meta.append(badge, url);
  const snippet = document.createElement("p");
  snippet.className = "snippet";
  snippet.innerHTML = result.snippet; // safe: the server escaped the page text and only added <mark> tags
  item.append(title, meta, snippet);
  return item;
}

function relevanceLevel(relevance) {
  if (relevance >= 0.8) return "high";
  return relevance >= 0.4 ? "mid" : "low";
}

function go(query, page) {
  const pagePart = page > 1 ? `&page=${page}` : "";
  history.pushState(null, "", query ? `/?q=${encodeURIComponent(query)}${pagePart}` : "/");
  window.scrollTo(0, 0);
  search(query, page);
}

form.addEventListener("submit", (event) => {
  event.preventDefault(); // stop the browser's own page reload; we fetch the results ourselves
  go(input.value.trim(), 1);
});
prevButton.addEventListener("click", () => go(readUrl().query, readUrl().page - 1));
nextButton.addEventListener("click", () => go(readUrl().query, readUrl().page + 1));
window.addEventListener("popstate", () => search(readUrl().query, readUrl().page)); // Back/Forward buttons
document.addEventListener("keydown", (event) => {
  if (event.key === "/" && document.activeElement !== input) {
    event.preventDefault();
    input.focus();
    input.select();
  }
});

// Dark mode: follow the system setting until the visitor picks one with the button.
const root = document.documentElement;
try {
  const saved = localStorage.getItem("seekr-theme");
  if (saved) root.dataset.theme = saved;
} catch (error) {} // storage can be blocked (private mode); the page still works
document.getElementById("theme").addEventListener("click", () => {
  const isDark = root.dataset.theme ? root.dataset.theme === "dark" : matchMedia("(prefers-color-scheme: dark)").matches;
  root.dataset.theme = isDark ? "light" : "dark";
  try {
    localStorage.setItem("seekr-theme", root.dataset.theme);
  } catch (error) {}
});

search(readUrl().query, readUrl().page);
    • fetch() sends an HTTP request from the browser and returns a promise: a value that arrives later. await waits for it without freezing the page, just like Python's await in Chapter 2. response.json() parses the JSON body, and response.ok is true for 2xx status codes.
    • encodeURIComponent(query) makes text safe to put in a URL: c++ & rust becomes c%2B%2B%20%26%20rust. Without it, the & would start a new parameter.
    • The address bar is the state. history.pushState changes the URL to /?q=...&page=2 without reloading, so results can be bookmarked and shared. The popstate event fires when you press Back or Forward, and we search again for whatever the URL now says. URLSearchParams reads ?q=....
    • textContent vs innerHTML: titles and URLs come from other people's websites, so we set them with textContent, which shows text literally. A title like <img onerror=...> stays harmless text. Only the snippet uses innerHTML, because the server (Chapter 7) already escaped the page text and added nothing but <mark> tags.
    • event.preventDefault() stops the form's normal behaviour (reloading the page). We fetch the results ourselves instead.
    • The relevance badge shows the calibrated score from Chapter 7 as a percentage, colored green (≥ 80%), amber (≥ 40%) or grey.
    • Pagination stops at 30 results, because that's how many pages the reranker scores per query.
    • Pressing / anywhere focuses the search box, as on GitHub or YouTube, unless you're already typing in it.
    • localStorage remembers the visitor's light/dark choice. It's wrapped in try/catch because some browsers block storage, and the page must work anyway.
    • [...].map(resultItem) turns each result object into a <li> element, and append(...list) adds them all at once.
  6. Serve the website with serve (whole updated file)
    seekr/__main__.py
    """Command-line entry point: python -m seekr <command>."""
import argparse
import asyncio

from seekr import api, config, crawler, db, embed, evaluate, extract, index, rank, search, web


def print_host_stats(conn):
    print(f"{'host':26} {'done':>5} {'queued':>7} {'failed':>7} {'error':>6} {'blocked':>8}")
    for r in db.host_stats(conn):
        print(f"{r['host']:26} {r['done']:5} {r['queued']:7} {r['failed']:7} {r['error']:6} {r['blocked']:8}")


def cmd_crawl(args):
    conn = db.connect()
    asyncio.run(crawler.crawl(config.read_seeds(), conn))
    print_host_stats(conn)


def cmd_index(args):
    conn = db.connect()
    print("Extracting text from crawled pages:")
    counts = extract.index_pages(conn)
    print(f"new: {counts['new']}  duplicates: {counts['duplicate']}  no main text: {counts['empty']}")
    print(f"Keyword index: added {index.build_index(conn)} chunks")
    print(f"Embedding chunks on {embed.best_device()}:")
    print(f"embedded: {embed.embed_chunks(conn)}")


def cmd_rank(args):
    conn = db.connect()
    pages, links = rank.compute_pagerank(conn)
    print(f"PageRank over {pages} pages and {links} links. Top 10:")
    top = conn.execute(
        "SELECT r.score, d.url FROM pagerank r JOIN documents d ON d.id = r.doc_id ORDER BY r.score DESC LIMIT 10"
    )
    for row in top:
        print(f"  {row['score']:.5f}  {row['url']}")


def cmd_search(args):
    engine = search.SearchEngine(db.connect())
    for i, result in enumerate(engine.search(" ".join(args.query)), start=1):
        print(f"{i:2}. [{result['relevance']:.2f}] {result['title']}\n    {result['url']}")


def cmd_eval(args):
    engine = search.SearchEngine(db.connect())
    queries = evaluate.load_queries()
    print(f"{len(queries)} queries   {'MRR@10':>7} {'nDCG@10':>8}")
    for mode, (mrr, ndcg) in evaluate.evaluate(engine, queries).items():
        print(f"{mode:18} {mrr:7.3f} {ndcg:8.3f}")


def cmd_serve(args):
    import uvicorn

    print(f"Seekr on http://{config.HOST}:{config.PORT}  (search page: /, API docs: /docs)")
    uvicorn.run(web.create_site(), host=config.HOST, port=config.PORT)


def cmd_keys(args):
    key = api.create_key(db.connect(), args.name, admin=args.admin)
    print(f"New {'admin ' if args.admin else ''}API key for {args.name!r} (shown once, store it safely):\n{key}")


def cmd_stats(args):
    seeds = config.read_seeds()
    db_state = "exists" if config.DB_PATH.exists() else "not created yet"
    print(f"seeds:    {len(seeds)}")
    print(f"database: {config.DB_PATH} ({db_state})")
    if config.DB_PATH.exists():
        conn = db.connect()
        print_host_stats(conn)
        for table in ("pages", "documents", "chunks", "embeddings"):
            print(f"{table + ':':10} {conn.execute(f'SELECT COUNT(*) FROM {table}').fetchone()[0]}")


COMMANDS = {
    "crawl": cmd_crawl,
    "index": cmd_index,
    "rank": cmd_rank,
    "serve": cmd_serve,
    "stats": cmd_stats,
    "search": cmd_search,
    "eval": cmd_eval,
    "keys": cmd_keys,
}


def main(argv=None):
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog="seekr", description="Seekr search engine")
    sub = parser.add_subparsers(dest="command", required=True)
    for name in COMMANDS:
        command = sub.add_parser(name)
        if name == "search":
            command.add_argument("query", nargs="+")
        if name == "keys":
            command.add_argument("action", choices=["create"])
            command.add_argument("name", help="who or what the key is for")
            command.add_argument("--admin", action="store_true", help="may add seeds")
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
    COMMANDS[args.command](args)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
    One change: cmd_serve runs web.create_site() (the API plus the website) instead of api.create_app().

from seekr import api, db, embed, index, rank, web

def client(tmp_path_factory):

    with TestClient(web.create_site(path)) as client:
        yield client


def test_home_page_and_assets_are_served(client):

    assert home.status_code == 200 and 'class="wordmark"' in home.text
    assert client.get("/app.js").headers["content-type"].startswith("text/javascript")

    These check that the page and its files are served with the right content types, that the browser endpoint needs no key while /api/v1 still does, and that one visitor gets rate-limited.
