Checkpoint

Search for rust ownership. You should see:

  • the logo moving to the top, and "Results 1–10 · about 150 ms";
  • "What is Ownership? - The Rust Programming Language" first, with a green 100% relevant badge, and the query words highlighted in the snippet;
  • a Next → button that goes to results 11–20 and changes the address to ?q=rust+ownership&page=2. The browser's Back button returns to page 1.

Also try: the ◐ button (dark/light mode, remembered after a reload), pressing / after clicking somewhere else on the page, and a phone-sized window. In Chrome, open DevTools (⌥⌘I) and click the phone icon.

The page loads but every search says "That search didn't work."
Look at the server's terminal for the real error. Usually the index is missing: run index and rank first.
{"detail":"Not Found"} at http://127.0.0.1:8000/
The web/ folder isn't next to seekr/, or html=True is missing from StaticFiles.
/api/v1/search returns the HTML page instead of JSON
The app.mount("/", ...) line runs before the routes are added. It must be the last thing in create_site.
Previous/Next show up on the empty home page
The [hidden] { display: none !important; } rule is missing from style.css.
The page scrolls sideways on a phone
Check .url { min-width: 0 } and the viewport <meta> tag.
Your changes to app.js don't appear
The browser cached the old file. Hard-reload with ⌘⇧R.

Chapter 10 · Hardening ready

Make Seekr pleasant to run and easy to debug: logs that say what happened, a health check that says what's wrong and how to fix it, Docker, and a README.

Why this matters: until now, when something went wrong you found out from a stack trace, or not at all. A crawl that silently skipped a site looked exactly like one that worked. "Hardening" is the unglamorous work that turns a project that works on my machine into one other people can run and fix.

  1. Add the log-level setting to seekr/config.py (whole updated file)
    seekr/config.py
    """All settings in one place. Override any of them with an environment variable."""
import os
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent

DB_PATH = Path(os.environ.get("SEEKR_DB", ROOT / "data" / "seekr.db"))
SEEDS_FILE = Path(os.environ.get("SEEKR_SEEDS", ROOT / "seeds.txt"))
USER_AGENT = os.environ.get("SEEKR_USER_AGENT", "SeekrBot/0.1")
HOST = os.environ.get("SEEKR_HOST", "127.0.0.1")
PORT = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_PORT", "8000"))

# Crawler
MAX_DEPTH = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_MAX_DEPTH", "3"))
MAX_PAGES_PER_DOMAIN = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_MAX_PAGES_PER_DOMAIN", "300"))
CRAWL_DELAY = float(os.environ.get("SEEKR_CRAWL_DELAY", "1.0"))  # seconds between requests to one host
REQUEST_TIMEOUT = float(os.environ.get("SEEKR_REQUEST_TIMEOUT", "15"))
MAX_PAGE_BYTES = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_MAX_PAGE_BYTES", "2000000"))
RETRIES = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_RETRIES", "2"))

# Extraction
CHUNK_WORDS = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_CHUNK_WORDS", "300"))
CHUNK_OVERLAP = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_CHUNK_OVERLAP", "50"))

# Embeddings (runs on the GPU when there is one)
EMBED_MODEL = os.environ.get("SEEKR_EMBED_MODEL", "BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5")
EMBED_BATCH = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_EMBED_BATCH", "64"))
DEVICE = os.environ.get("SEEKR_DEVICE", "")  # "", "cuda", "mps" or "cpu"; "" picks the best one

# Search and relevance scoring
CANDIDATES = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_CANDIDATES", "100"))  # from each of BM25 and vectors
RERANK_TOP = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_RERANK_TOP", "30"))  # pages the cross-encoder reads per query
# MiniLM beat BAAI/bge-reranker-base on our eval (MRR 0.733 vs 0.648) and is 3x faster; try either.
RERANK_MODEL = os.environ.get("SEEKR_RERANK_MODEL", "cross-encoder/ms-marco-MiniLM-L-6-v2")
RERANK_MAX_TOKENS = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_RERANK_MAX_TOKENS", "384"))
# Tuned on evals/queries.jsonl. Freshness is 0: docs show "last updated today", so dates mislead.
W_RELEVANCE, W_PAGERANK, W_FRESHNESS, W_EXACT = 0.90, 0.05, 0.0, 0.05
THIN_PAGE_WORDS, THIN_PAGE_PENALTY = 50, 0.8

# REST API
RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE", "60"))  # per API key
CORS_ORIGINS = os.environ.get("SEEKR_CORS_ORIGINS", "*").split(",")  # websites allowed to call the API

# Website
UI_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_UI_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE", "30"))  # per visitor IP

# Operations
LOG_LEVEL = os.environ.get("SEEKR_LOG_LEVEL", "INFO")  # DEBUG shows every fetched URL


def read_seeds(path=None):
    """Return the seed URLs, skipping blank lines and # comments."""
    lines = Path(path or SEEDS_FILE).read_text().splitlines()
    return [line.strip() for line in lines if line.strip() and not line.startswith("#")]
    INFO logs one line per search and per finished site. SEEKR_LOG_LEVEL=DEBUG python -m seekr crawl also shows every URL the crawler fetches.
  2. Log what the crawler does (whole updated file)
    seekr/crawler.py
    """A small, polite, resumable web crawler."""
import asyncio
import logging
from html.parser import HTMLParser
from urllib.robotparser import RobotFileParser

import httpx

from seekr import config, db
from seekr.urls import build_scopes, in_scope, normalize_url

RETRY_STATUSES = {None, 429, 500, 502, 503, 504}
log = logging.getLogger(__name__)


class LinkParser(HTMLParser):
    """Collects the <title> text, every <a href> and every <iframe src> on a page."""

    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__()
        self.title, self.links, self.in_title = "", [], False

    def handle_starttag(self, tag, attrs):
        attrs = dict(attrs)
        if tag == "a" and attrs.get("href"):
            self.links.append(attrs["href"])
        elif tag == "iframe" and attrs.get("src"):  # some docs load their table of contents this way
            self.links.append(attrs["src"])
        elif tag == "title":
            self.in_title = True

    def handle_endtag(self, tag):
        if tag == "title":
            self.in_title = False

    def handle_data(self, data):
        if self.in_title:
            self.title += data


def parse_page(html, base_url):
    parser = LinkParser()
    parser.feed(html)
    links = {normalize_url(href, base_url) for href in parser.links}
    return parser.title.strip(), sorted(link for link in links if link)


async def load_robots(client, host):
    """Missing robots.txt (4xx) means allow all. Unreachable (5xx, network error) returns None."""
    try:
        resp = await client.get(f"https://{host}/robots.txt")
    except httpx.HTTPError:
        return None
    if resp.status_code >= 500:
        return None
    robots = RobotFileParser()
    if resp.status_code >= 400:
        robots.allow_all = True
    else:
        robots.parse(resp.text.splitlines())
    return robots


async def fetch_once(client, url):
    """Return (status, final_url, html). html is None for non-HTML or oversized pages."""
    async with client.stream("GET", url) as resp:
        final_url = str(resp.url)
        if resp.status_code != 200 or "text/html" not in resp.headers.get("content-type", ""):
            return resp.status_code, final_url, None
        body = b""
        async for chunk in resp.aiter_bytes():
            body += chunk
            if len(body) > config.MAX_PAGE_BYTES:
                return resp.status_code, final_url, None
        return resp.status_code, final_url, body.decode(resp.charset_encoding or "utf-8", "replace")


async def fetch(client, url):
    """fetch_once, retried with exponential backoff (1s, 2s, ...) on network errors, 429 and 5xx."""
    for attempt in range(config.RETRIES + 1):
        try:
            status, final_url, html = await fetch_once(client, url)
        except httpx.HTTPError:
            status, final_url, html = None, url, None
        if status not in RETRY_STATUSES or attempt == config.RETRIES:
            return status, final_url, html
        await asyncio.sleep(2**attempt)


async def crawl_host(client, conn, host, scopes):
    """Crawl one host, one request at a time, waiting between requests."""
    robots = await load_robots(client, host)
    if robots is None:  # can't read the rules, so stay away from this host until the next run
        log.warning("%s: robots.txt unreachable, skipping this host until the next run", host)
        return
    delay = max(config.CRAWL_DELAY, robots.crawl_delay(config.USER_AGENT) or 0)
    while db.count_done(conn, host) < config.MAX_PAGES_PER_DOMAIN:
        row = db.next_url(conn, host)
        if row is None:
            break
        url, depth = row["url"], row["depth"]
        if not robots.can_fetch(config.USER_AGENT, url):
            db.mark(conn, url, "blocked")
            continue
        status, final_url, html = await fetch(client, url)
        log.debug("%s %s", status, url)
        if depth == 0 and final_url != url:  # a seed redirected, e.g. /en/stable/ -> /en/6.1/
            scopes.extend(build_scopes([final_url]))
        if html:
            title, links = parse_page(html, final_url)
            db.save_page(conn, url, final_url, status, title, html, links)
            if depth < config.MAX_DEPTH:
                db.enqueue(conn, [link for link in links if in_scope(link, scopes)], depth + 1)
        if html:
            db.mark(conn, url, "done")
        else:  # no status means a network error: worth retrying on the next run
            db.mark(conn, url, "failed" if status else "error")
            log.info("%s %s", status or "network error", url)
        conn.commit()
        await asyncio.sleep(delay)
    log.info("%s: done (%d pages)", host, db.count_done(conn, host))


async def crawl(seeds, conn, transport=None):
    """Crawl all seed hosts at the same time (each host politely, one request at a time)."""
    seed_urls = [normalize_url(seed) for seed in seeds]
    scopes = build_scopes(seed_urls + db.redirect_targets(conn, seed_urls))
    db.enqueue(conn, seed_urls, depth=0)
    db.requeue_errors(conn)
    conn.commit()
    hosts = sorted({host for host, _ in scopes})
    headers = {"User-Agent": config.USER_AGENT}
    async with httpx.AsyncClient(
        headers=headers, timeout=config.REQUEST_TIMEOUT, follow_redirects=True, transport=transport
    ) as client:
        await asyncio.gather(*(crawl_host(client, conn, host, scopes) for host in hosts))
    • logging.getLogger(__name__) gives each module its own named logger: here seekr.crawler. Every log line shows where it came from, and you can turn modules up or down separately.
    • Levels: debug (every fetched URL; hidden by default) < info (normal events: a site finished, a page failed) < warning (we skipped something on purpose: robots.txt unreachable) < error. Only messages at or above the configured level are printed.
    • log.info("%s: done (%d pages)", host, count) passes the values separately instead of using an f-string. The text is only built if the line is actually printed, which matters for debug lines inside a loop over thousands of pages.
    • Six lines were added, and nothing else changed: the crawler's logic is identical to Chapter 2.
  3. Log every search in the API (whole updated file)
    seekr/api.py
    """REST API: search Seekr from any program, with API keys and a rate limit per key."""
import hashlib
import logging
import secrets
import time
from collections import defaultdict, deque
from contextlib import asynccontextmanager
from datetime import datetime, timezone

from fastapi import APIRouter, Depends, FastAPI, HTTPException, Query, Request
from fastapi.middleware.cors import CORSMiddleware
from fastapi.security import HTTPAuthorizationCredentials, HTTPBearer
from pydantic import BaseModel

from seekr import config, db, embed, search
from seekr.urls import normalize_url

log = logging.getLogger(__name__)
KEYS_SCHEMA = """CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS api_keys (
    key_hash TEXT PRIMARY KEY, name TEXT NOT NULL, is_admin INTEGER NOT NULL, created_at TEXT NOT NULL);"""


def hash_key(key):
    return hashlib.sha256(key.encode()).hexdigest()


def create_key(conn, name, admin=False):
    """Make a new random key and store only its hash. The key itself is shown once, then forgotten."""
    conn.executescript(KEYS_SCHEMA)
    key = "sk_" + secrets.token_urlsafe(24)
    created_at = datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
    conn.execute("INSERT INTO api_keys VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?)", (hash_key(key), name, int(admin), created_at))
    conn.commit()
    return key


class RateLimiter:
    """Allows `limit` requests per key in any 60-second window (a "sliding window")."""

    def __init__(self, limit):
        self.limit, self.hits = limit, defaultdict(deque)

    def allow(self, key, now=None):
        now = time.monotonic() if now is None else now
        hits = self.hits[key]
        while hits and hits[0] <= now - 60:
            hits.popleft()
        if len(hits) >= self.limit:
            return False
        hits.append(now)
        return True


class Signals(BaseModel):
    bm25_rank: int | None
    vector_rank: int | None
    relevance: float | None
    pagerank: float | None
    freshness: float | None


class Result(BaseModel):
    url: str
    title: str
    snippet: str  # HTML: page text is escaped, query words are wrapped in <mark>
    relevance: float | None
    final_score: float | None
    signals: Signals


class SearchResponse(BaseModel):
    query: str
    took_ms: int
    results: list[Result]


class Page(BaseModel):
    url: str
    title: str | None
    description: str | None
    published: str | None
    language: str | None
    word_count: int
    pagerank: float | None
    chunks: int


class Seeds(BaseModel):
    urls: list[str]


bearer = HTTPBearer(auto_error=False)
router = APIRouter(prefix="/api/v1")


async def api_key(request: Request, credentials: HTTPAuthorizationCredentials | None = Depends(bearer)):
    """Every endpoint depends on this: a valid key that is under its rate limit.
    All handlers are `async` so they run on one thread: a SQLite connection may only be used by the thread
    that opened it."""
    if credentials is None:
        raise HTTPException(401, "Missing API key. Send the header: Authorization: Bearer <key>")
    key_hash = hash_key(credentials.credentials)
    row = request.app.state.conn.execute("SELECT name, is_admin FROM api_keys WHERE key_hash = ?", (key_hash,)).fetchone()
    if row is None:
        raise HTTPException(401, "Invalid API key")
    if not request.app.state.limiter.allow(key_hash):
        raise HTTPException(429, "Rate limit exceeded, try again in a minute", headers={"Retry-After": "60"})
    return row


async def admin_key(key=Depends(api_key)):
    if not key["is_admin"]:
        raise HTTPException(403, "This endpoint needs an admin key")
    return key


@router.get("/search", response_model=SearchResponse)
async def search_pages(
    request: Request,
    q: str = Query(min_length=1, max_length=200, description="What to search for"),
    limit: int = Query(10, ge=1, le=30),
    offset: int = Query(0, ge=0, le=29),
    site: str | None = Query(None, description="Only results from this host, e.g. docs.python.org"),
    key=Depends(api_key),
):
    started = time.perf_counter()
    results = request.app.state.engine.search(q, limit=limit, offset=offset, site=site)
    took_ms = round((time.perf_counter() - started) * 1000)
    log.info("search %r offset=%d site=%s -> %d results in %d ms", q, offset, site, len(results), took_ms)
    return {"query": q, "took_ms": took_ms, "results": results}


@router.get("/page", response_model=Page)
async def page_info(request: Request, url: str, key=Depends(api_key)):
    row = request.app.state.conn.execute(
        """SELECT d.url, d.title, d.description, d.published, d.language, d.word_count, r.score AS pagerank,
                  (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM chunks c WHERE c.doc_id = d.id) AS chunks
           FROM documents d LEFT JOIN pagerank r ON r.doc_id = d.id WHERE d.url = ? AND d.duplicate_of IS NULL""",
        (url,),
    ).fetchone()
    if row is None:
        raise HTTPException(404, "That URL is not in the index")
    return dict(row)


@router.get("/stats")
async def stats(request: Request, key=Depends(api_key)):
    count = lambda sql: request.app.state.conn.execute(sql).fetchone()[0]  # noqa: E731
    return {
        "pages": count("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM pages"),
        "documents": count("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM documents WHERE duplicate_of IS NULL AND word_count > 0"),
        "chunks": count("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM chunks"),
        "embeddings": count("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM embeddings"),
        "device": embed.best_device(),
    }


@router.post("/crawl/seeds")
async def add_seeds(body: Seeds, key=Depends(admin_key)):
    """Add start URLs for the next crawl. Crawling itself runs from the command line."""
    urls = [normalize_url(url) for url in body.urls]
    if None in urls:
        raise HTTPException(422, "Every seed must be an http(s) URL")
    known = set(config.read_seeds())
    added = [url for url in dict.fromkeys(urls) if url not in known]
    with open(config.SEEDS_FILE, "a") as seeds_file:
        seeds_file.writelines(f"{url}\n" for url in added)
    return {"added": added, "next": "python -m seekr crawl && python -m seekr index && python -m seekr rank"}


def create_app(db_path=None):
    @asynccontextmanager
    async def lifespan(app):
        app.state.conn = db.connect(db_path)
        app.state.conn.executescript(KEYS_SCHEMA)
        app.state.engine = search.SearchEngine(app.state.conn)  # loads vectors + models once, at startup
        app.state.engine.search("warm up")  # the GPU compiles its kernels on first use; do it before users arrive
        app.state.limiter = RateLimiter(config.RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE)
        yield
        app.state.conn.close()

    app = FastAPI(title="Seekr API", version="0.1", lifespan=lifespan)
    app.add_middleware(CORSMiddleware, allow_origins=config.CORS_ORIGINS, allow_methods=["GET", "POST"],
                       allow_headers=["Authorization", "Content-Type"])
    app.include_router(router)
    return app
    One log.info per search, with the query, page, site filter, result count and time. That's enough to spot slow queries and to see what people search for. %r prints the query with quotes, so leading or trailing spaces are visible.
  4. Create the health check
    seekr/doctor.py
    """`python -m seekr doctor`: check that everything Seekr needs is in place, and say how to fix what isn't."""
import importlib.metadata
import shutil
import sqlite3
import sys

from seekr import config

PACKAGES = ["httpx", "trafilatura", "torch", "sentence-transformers", "numpy", "fastapi", "uvicorn"]
# table -> the command that fills it
TABLES = {"pages": "crawl", "documents": "index", "chunks": "index", "chunks_fts": "index",
          "embeddings": "index", "pagerank": "rank"}


def check_python():
    ok = sys.version_info >= (3, 11)
    return ok, f"Python {sys.version.split()[0]}" + ("" if ok else " (need 3.11 or newer)")


def check_packages():
    missing = []
    for name in PACKAGES:
        try:
            importlib.metadata.version(name)
        except importlib.metadata.PackageNotFoundError:
            missing.append(name)
    if missing:
        return False, f"missing packages: {', '.join(missing)} (run: pip install -r requirements.txt)"
    return True, f"{len(PACKAGES)} packages installed"


def check_device():
    from seekr import embed  # imported here because it loads torch, which takes a few seconds

    device = embed.best_device()
    return True, f"compute device: {device}" + (" (no GPU found: search works, just slower)" if device == "cpu" else "")


def check_seeds():
    seeds = config.read_seeds() if config.SEEDS_FILE.exists() else []
    return bool(seeds), f"{len(seeds)} seed URLs in {config.SEEDS_FILE.name}"


def check_database():
    if not config.DB_PATH.exists():
        return False, f"no database at {config.DB_PATH} (run: python -m seekr crawl)"
    conn = sqlite3.connect(config.DB_PATH)
    counts = {}
    for table, command in TABLES.items():
        try:
            counts[table] = conn.execute(f"SELECT COUNT(*) FROM {table}").fetchone()[0]
        except sqlite3.OperationalError:
            return False, f"table {table} is missing (run: python -m seekr {command})"
        if counts[table] == 0:
            return False, f"table {table} is empty (run: python -m seekr {command})"
    if not counts["chunks"] == counts["chunks_fts"] == counts["embeddings"]:
        return False, (f"index is out of date: {counts['chunks']} chunks, {counts['chunks_fts']} keyword-indexed, "
                       f"{counts['embeddings']} embedded (run: python -m seekr index)")
    return True, ", ".join(f"{table} {count:,}" for table, count in counts.items())


def check_models():
    from huggingface_hub import CacheNotFound, scan_cache_dir

    try:
        cached = {repo.repo_id for repo in scan_cache_dir().repos}
    except CacheNotFound:  # nothing downloaded on this machine yet, e.g. a fresh Docker container
        cached = set()
    missing = [model for model in (config.EMBED_MODEL, config.RERANK_MODEL) if model not in cached]
    if missing:
        return False, f"models not downloaded yet: {', '.join(missing)} (run: python -m seekr index, needs internet once)"
    return True, "embedding and reranker models are cached (works offline)"


def check_disk():
    free_gb = shutil.disk_usage(config.DB_PATH.parent if config.DB_PATH.parent.exists() else ".").free / 1e9
    return free_gb > 5, f"{free_gb:.0f} GB free disk" + ("" if free_gb > 5 else " (less than 5 GB: crawls may fail)")


CHECKS = [check_python, check_packages, check_device, check_seeds, check_database, check_models, check_disk]


def run_checks():
    """Run every check, print one line each, and return True if all passed."""
    all_ok = True
    for check in CHECKS:
        try:
            ok, message = check()
        except Exception as error:  # a broken check must not hide the others
            ok, message = False, f"{check.__name__} crashed: {error}"
        print(f"{'✓' if ok else '✗'} {message}")
        all_ok &= ok
    return all_ok
    • Each check is a small function that returns (ok, message), and CHECKS is just a list of them. Adding a check means writing one function and adding it to the list.
    • Every failure says how to fix it ("run: python -m seekr index"). That's the difference between a health check and an error message.
    • importlib.metadata.version("torch") asks Python whether a package is installed, without importing it, which would take seconds for torch.
    • check_database also compares the chunk, keyword-index and embedding counts. If a crawl added pages but you forgot index, they differ, and doctor tells you.
    • scan_cache_dir() lists the models already downloaded by Hugging Face. On a brand-new machine the cache folder doesn't exist at all, and the library raises CacheNotFound. We found that out by running doctor inside a fresh Docker container, where it originally crashed instead of saying "not downloaded yet".
    • run_checks catches any exception from a check, so one broken check can't hide the others.
  5. Turn on logging and add the doctor command (whole updated file)
    seekr/__main__.py
    """Command-line entry point: python -m seekr <command>."""
import argparse
import asyncio
import logging
import sys

from seekr import api, config, crawler, db, doctor, embed, evaluate, extract, index, rank, search, web


def print_host_stats(conn):
    print(f"{'host':26} {'done':>5} {'queued':>7} {'failed':>7} {'error':>6} {'blocked':>8}")
    for r in db.host_stats(conn):
        print(f"{r['host']:26} {r['done']:5} {r['queued']:7} {r['failed']:7} {r['error']:6} {r['blocked']:8}")


def cmd_crawl(args):
    conn = db.connect()
    asyncio.run(crawler.crawl(config.read_seeds(), conn))
    print_host_stats(conn)


def cmd_index(args):
    conn = db.connect()
    print("Extracting text from crawled pages:")
    counts = extract.index_pages(conn)
    print(f"new: {counts['new']}  duplicates: {counts['duplicate']}  no main text: {counts['empty']}")
    print(f"Keyword index: added {index.build_index(conn)} chunks")
    print(f"Embedding chunks on {embed.best_device()}:")
    print(f"embedded: {embed.embed_chunks(conn)}")


def cmd_rank(args):
    conn = db.connect()
    pages, links = rank.compute_pagerank(conn)
    print(f"PageRank over {pages} pages and {links} links. Top 10:")
    top = conn.execute(
        "SELECT r.score, d.url FROM pagerank r JOIN documents d ON d.id = r.doc_id ORDER BY r.score DESC LIMIT 10"
    )
    for row in top:
        print(f"  {row['score']:.5f}  {row['url']}")


def cmd_search(args):
    engine = search.SearchEngine(db.connect())
    for i, result in enumerate(engine.search(" ".join(args.query)), start=1):
        print(f"{i:2}. [{result['relevance']:.2f}] {result['title']}\n    {result['url']}")


def cmd_eval(args):
    engine = search.SearchEngine(db.connect())
    queries = evaluate.load_queries()
    print(f"{len(queries)} queries   {'MRR@10':>7} {'nDCG@10':>8}")
    for mode, (mrr, ndcg) in evaluate.evaluate(engine, queries).items():
        print(f"{mode:18} {mrr:7.3f} {ndcg:8.3f}")


def cmd_serve(args):
    import uvicorn

    print(f"Seekr on http://{config.HOST}:{config.PORT}  (search page: /, API docs: /docs)")
    uvicorn.run(web.create_site(), host=config.HOST, port=config.PORT)


def cmd_keys(args):
    key = api.create_key(db.connect(), args.name, admin=args.admin)
    print(f"New {'admin ' if args.admin else ''}API key for {args.name!r} (shown once, store it safely):\n{key}")


def cmd_doctor(args):
    sys.exit(0 if doctor.run_checks() else 1)


def cmd_stats(args):
    seeds = config.read_seeds()
    db_state = "exists" if config.DB_PATH.exists() else "not created yet"
    print(f"seeds:    {len(seeds)}")
    print(f"database: {config.DB_PATH} ({db_state})")
    if config.DB_PATH.exists():
        conn = db.connect()
        print_host_stats(conn)
        for table in ("pages", "documents", "chunks", "embeddings"):
            print(f"{table + ':':10} {conn.execute(f'SELECT COUNT(*) FROM {table}').fetchone()[0]}")


COMMANDS = {
    "crawl": cmd_crawl,
    "index": cmd_index,
    "rank": cmd_rank,
    "serve": cmd_serve,
    "stats": cmd_stats,
    "search": cmd_search,
    "eval": cmd_eval,
    "keys": cmd_keys,
    "doctor": cmd_doctor,
}


def main(argv=None):
    logging.basicConfig(level=config.LOG_LEVEL, format="%(asctime)s %(levelname)-7s %(name)s: %(message)s",
                        datefmt="%H:%M:%S")
    for noisy in ("sentence_transformers", "httpx", "httpx2"):  # progress bars and one line per HTTP request
        logging.getLogger(noisy).setLevel(logging.WARNING)
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog="seekr", description="Seekr search engine")
    sub = parser.add_subparsers(dest="command", required=True)
    for name in COMMANDS:
        command = sub.add_parser(name)
        if name == "search":
            command.add_argument("query", nargs="+")
        if name == "keys":
            command.add_argument("action", choices=["create"])
            command.add_argument("name", help="who or what the key is for")
            command.add_argument("--admin", action="store_true", help="may add seeds")
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
    COMMANDS[args.command](args)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
    • logging.basicConfig(...) runs once at start-up and decides where log lines go (the terminal), what they look like (time, level, logger name, message) and the minimum level.
    • Some libraries log a lot at INFO: sentence-transformers draws a "Batches:" progress bar for every search, and httpx logs every HTTP request. We raise just their loggers to WARNING.
    • sys.exit(0 if ok else 1): the exit code tells scripts whether the command succeeded (0) or failed (anything else). So python -m seekr doctor && python -m seekr serve only starts the server when everything is healthy, and CI systems and Docker health checks understand it too.
  6. Write the hardening tests
    tests/test_hardening.py
    import logging

import pytest

from seekr import config, db, doctor


@pytest.fixture
def empty_project(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setattr(config, "DB_PATH", tmp_path / "data" / "seekr.db")
    monkeypatch.setattr(config, "SEEDS_FILE", tmp_path / "seeds.txt")
    return tmp_path


def test_doctor_explains_how_to_fix_a_missing_database(empty_project):
    ok, message = doctor.check_database()
    assert not ok and "python -m seekr crawl" in message


def test_doctor_spots_an_unfinished_index(empty_project):
    conn = db.connect()
    db.save_page(conn, "https://x.com/", "https://x.com/", 200, "X", "", [])
    conn.commit()
    ok, message = doctor.check_database()
    assert not ok and "documents is empty" in message and "seekr index" in message


def test_doctor_checks_seeds_and_python(empty_project):
    assert doctor.check_seeds()[0] is False
    (empty_project / "seeds.txt").write_text("https://a.com/\n")
    assert doctor.check_seeds() == (True, "1 seed URLs in seeds.txt")
    assert doctor.check_python()[0]


def test_doctor_reports_models_missing_on_a_fresh_machine(monkeypatch):
    import huggingface_hub

    def no_cache_yet():
        raise huggingface_hub.CacheNotFound("no cache", cache_dir="/nowhere")

    monkeypatch.setattr(huggingface_hub, "scan_cache_dir", no_cache_yet)
    ok, message = doctor.check_models()
    assert not ok and "not downloaded yet" in message


def test_run_checks_keeps_going_after_a_crash(monkeypatch, capsys):
    def broken():
        raise RuntimeError("boom")

    monkeypatch.setattr(doctor, "CHECKS", [broken, doctor.check_python])
    assert doctor.run_checks() is False
    output = capsys.readouterr().out
    assert "✗ broken crashed: boom" in output and "✓ Python" in output


def test_crawler_logs_skipped_hosts(tmp_path, caplog):
    import asyncio

    import httpx

    from seekr import crawler

    conn = db.connect(tmp_path / "t.db")
    down = httpx.MockTransport(lambda request: httpx.Response(503))
    with caplog.at_level(logging.WARNING, logger="seekr.crawler"):
        asyncio.run(crawler.crawl(["https://example.com/"], conn, transport=down))
    assert "example.com: robots.txt unreachable" in caplog.text
    • caplog is a pytest fixture that captures log messages, so we can check the crawler really warns about an unreachable robots.txt.
    • The "fresh machine" test replaces scan_cache_dir with a function that raises CacheNotFound. Our first version set the HF_HUB_CACHE environment variable instead. It passed on its own, then failed in the full run, because huggingface_hub reads that variable only once, when another test first imports it. A useful lesson: a test that only passes alone isn't a passing test.

  9. Create the Dockerfile
    Dockerfile
    # Seekr in a container. Docker on a Mac can't reach the Apple GPU, so inside Docker everything runs on the CPU.
FROM python:3.12-slim

WORKDIR /app
ENV PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1 \
    SEEKR_HOST=0.0.0.0 \
    HF_HOME=/app/data/huggingface

COPY requirements.txt .
# CPU-only PyTorch first: ~200 MB instead of ~2.5 GB of NVIDIA libraries the container couldn't use anyway
RUN pip install --no-cache-dir torch==2.15.0 --index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cpu \
 && pip install --no-cache-dir -r requirements.txt

COPY seeds.txt ./
COPY seekr ./seekr
COPY web ./web
COPY evals ./evals

EXPOSE 8000
CMD ["python", "-m", "seekr", "serve"]
    • An image is a frozen file system with everything Seekr needs (Linux, Python, packages, our code). A container is a running copy of it. Anyone with Docker can run Seekr with no Python setup at all.
    • Docker builds in layers and reuses a layer if nothing it depends on changed. We copy requirements.txt and install packages before copying our code, so editing a .py file rebuilds in seconds instead of re-downloading 1 GB of packages.
    • CPU-only PyTorch: Docker on a Mac runs Linux in a virtual machine, which can't reach the Apple GPU. The normal Linux PyTorch download includes about 2.5 GB of NVIDIA libraries we couldn't use, so we install the CPU build from PyTorch's own package index first. pip install -r requirements.txt then sees torch==2.15.0 is already satisfied.
    • SEEKR_HOST=0.0.0.0: inside a container, 127.0.0.1 means "only this container", so the server must listen on all interfaces for your browser to reach it.
    • HF_HOME=/app/data/huggingface puts the downloaded models in the data folder, which we keep outside the container (see compose), so they survive rebuilds.
  10. Create the compose file
    docker-compose.yml
    # Start:       docker compose up --build        then open http://localhost:8000
# Other steps: docker compose run --rm seekr python -m seekr crawl    (or index, rank, doctor, keys create NAME)
services:
  seekr:
    build: .
    ports:
      - "8000:8000"
    volumes:
      - ./data:/app/data  # the database and downloaded models stay on your disk, outside the container
    environment:
      SEEKR_LOG_LEVEL: INFO
    restart: unless-stopped
    • ports: "8000:8000" connects port 8000 on your Mac to port 8000 in the container.
    • Volumes: ./data:/app/data makes the container's /app/data be your project's data/ folder. The 500 MB database and the models stay on your disk. A container can be deleted and recreated at any time without losing them, and the container can use the index you already built.
    • restart: unless-stopped brings Seekr back after a crash or a reboot.
  11. Tell Docker what to leave out
    .dockerignore
    .venv
data
__pycache__
.pytest_cache
.git
    Everything in the project folder is sent to Docker when it builds (the "build context"). Without this file, it would copy your 1 GB .venv (macOS packages, useless in Linux) and the database (mounted at run time instead) on every build.
  12. Build and start Seekr in Docker
    docker compose up --build
    The first build downloads about 1 GB and took 1.5 minutes here. The image is about 2 GB. Rebuilds after a code change take seconds. On first start the container downloads the two models (about 200 MB) into data/huggingface. Then open http://localhost:8000. Stop the native serve first, or both will try to use port 8000.
  13. Run the other commands inside the container
    docker compose run --rm seekr python -m seekr doctor
    ✓ Python 3.12.16
✓ 7 packages installed
✓ compute device: cpu (no GPU found: search works, just slower)
✓ 15 seed URLs in seeds.txt
✓ pages 4,086, documents 4,086, chunks 28,841, chunks_fts 28,841, embeddings 28,841, pagerank 3,885
✓ embedding and reranker models are cached (works offline)
✓ 232 GB free disk
    run --rm starts a one-off container for a single command and deletes it afterwards. crawl, index, rank and keys create NAME work the same way. In Docker on a Mac, a search takes about 1.3–1.5 s on the CPU instead of about 0.1 s on the GPU: same results, just slower. For daily use on a Mac, run Seekr natively.
  14. Rewrite the README
    README.md
    # Seekr

A small, fully open-source search engine for **developer documentation and engineering blogs**, without SEO spam.
It runs on your own laptop: a polite crawler, SQLite storage, keyword (BM25) plus semantic (embedding) retrieval,
and a **relevance score** from a cross-encoder model that reads each result together with your query.
Search through a clean web page or a REST API with API keys.

**Build it yourself, line by line:** open [`guide.html`](guide.html) in a browser.

| Search quality (22 labelled queries) | MRR@10 | nDCG@10 |
|---|---|---|
| Keyword only (BM25) | 0.624 | 0.630 |
| Semantic only (embeddings) | 0.678 | 0.712 |
| Hybrid (RRF) | 0.652 | 0.694 |
| **Hybrid + relevance reranker (Seekr)** | **0.774** | **0.782** |

## Quick start (macOS, Python 3.11+)

```bash
python3 -m venv .venv && source .venv/bin/activate
pip install -r requirements.txt

python -m seekr crawl     # politely download pages from seeds.txt (about 10 minutes, resumable)
python -m seekr index     # clean text, chunks, keyword index, embeddings (uses the GPU if there is one)
python -m seekr rank      # PageRank over the link graph
python -m seekr serve     # http://127.0.0.1:8000  (search page)  and  /docs  (API docs)
```

Check that everything is in place at any time:

```bash
python -m seekr doctor
```

Other commands: `stats`, `search <words>` (search from the terminal), `eval` (measure quality),
`keys create NAME [--admin]` (API keys).

## The REST API

Create a key (it is shown once, and only its hash is stored):

```bash
python -m seekr keys create my-app
export KEY=sk_...   # the key it printed
```

Search:

```bash
curl -s -H "Authorization: Bearer $KEY" "http://127.0.0.1:8000/api/v1/search?q=rust+ownership&limit=3"
```

```json
{"query": "rust ownership", "took_ms": 87, "results": [
  {"url": "https://doc.rust-lang.org/book/ch04-01-what-is-ownership.html",
   "title": "What is Ownership? - The Rust Programming Language",
   "snippet": "What Is <mark>Ownership</mark>? <mark>Ownership</mark> is a set of rules that govern...",
   "relevance": 0.9994, "final_score": 0.96,
   "signals": {"bm25_rank": 3, "vector_rank": 2, "relevance": 0.9994, "pagerank": 0.28, "freshness": 0.26}}]}
```

More endpoints:

```bash
curl -s -H "Authorization: Bearer $KEY" "http://127.0.0.1:8000/api/v1/search?q=concurrency&site=eli.thegreenplace.net"
curl -s -H "Authorization: Bearer $KEY" "http://127.0.0.1:8000/api/v1/page?url=https://www.sqlite.org/fts5.html"
curl -s -H "Authorization: Bearer $KEY" "http://127.0.0.1:8000/api/v1/stats"
# admin keys only (python -m seekr keys create me --admin):
curl -s -X POST -H "Authorization: Bearer $ADMIN_KEY" -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
     -d '{"urls": ["https://go.dev/blog/"]}' "http://127.0.0.1:8000/api/v1/crawl/seeds"
```

Each key may make 60 requests per minute (`429 Too Many Requests` after that). Interactive docs: http://127.0.0.1:8000/docs.

## Docker

```bash
docker compose up --build                                   # http://localhost:8000
docker compose run --rm seekr python -m seekr crawl         # and index, rank, doctor, keys create NAME
```

The database and the downloaded models live in `./data` on your disk. Docker on a Mac cannot use the Apple GPU,
so inside the container everything runs on the CPU: same results, but a search takes about 1.3–1.5 s instead of
~0.1 s. Run it natively (Quick start) to use the GPU.

## Settings

Everything is in [`seekr/config.py`](seekr/config.py) and can be overridden with environment variables, for example
`SEEKR_MAX_PAGES_PER_DOMAIN=1000`, `SEEKR_DEVICE=cpu`, `SEEKR_RERANK_MODEL=BAAI/bge-reranker-base`,
`SEEKR_PORT=9000`, `SEEKR_LOG_LEVEL=DEBUG`.

## How it works

```
seeds.txt → crawler → SQLite (frontier, pages) → extractor (text, chunks)
          → FTS5 BM25 index + embeddings (GPU) + PageRank
query     → BM25 top-100 + vector top-100 → Reciprocal Rank Fusion → best chunk per page
          → cross-encoder relevance (GPU, 0–1) → final score → REST API + web page
```

Progress and design decisions: [`PROGRESS.md`](PROGRESS.md). Tests: `python -m pytest -q`.
    The README is the front door: what Seekr is, how good it is (the evaluation table), the four commands in order, real curl examples, Docker, and settings. Every number in it was measured, not guessed.

Checkpoint

python -m seekr doctor && python -m seekr serve

All seven checks show ✓, and the server starts. Every search you make prints one line like:

13:03:14 INFO    seekr.api: search 'django models' offset=0 site=None -> 10 results in 99 ms
doctor shows ✗ and exits with code 1
Read the message: it names the command to run. Then run doctor again.
Cannot connect to the Docker daemon
Docker Desktop isn't running. Start it (open -a Docker) and wait for the whale icon to settle.
Bind for 0.0.0.0:8000 failed: port is already allocated
The native serve is still running. Stop it, or change the left number in ports to "8001:8000".
The Docker build downloads gigabytes of nvidia-* packages
The CPU-only torch line must come before pip install -r requirements.txt.
Logs full of "Batches: 100%" lines
The for noisy in (...) loop is missing from main().

Chapter 11 · Growing the crawl & live mode ready

More sites, a crawl that keeps going, and an index that keeps up by itself while you search. Plus two real crashes, and why the fan stayed quiet.

Why more seeds make crawling faster: the crawler runs one task per site, all at the same time, and each site gets at most one request per second. So the total speed is roughly one page per second times the number of sites. With 15 sites the crawler can never go faster than about 15 pages a second, however fast your computer is. With 51 sites, it can reach about 51. Each individual site sees exactly the same gentle traffic as before.

Why not "GPU-accelerate" the crawler? A GPU speeds up heavy math, such as the matrix multiplications in embeddings and the reranker, which already run on it (Chapters 5 and 7). A crawler does almost no math: it spends nearly all its time waiting, for the network and for the polite delay between requests. More parallel sites is the speed-up that actually works.

From a niche to the general web. Seekr only knows what it crawled. With only developer docs, a search for "Adele" finds nothing. So the second half of the list adds general sites: news, music, film, sport, science, health and books. Many big sites don't want bots. Reuters, CNN, the New York Times, AllMusic and IMDb disallow them in robots.txt, so we leave them out. Others, including Wikipedia, AP, The Guardian, Britannica and the CDC, answered our check with 403 Forbidden. Wikipedia's rules require a bot's User-Agent to include contact information (a URL or email address), and ours has none.

How we picked the new sites: for each candidate, a small script fetched robots.txt with our User-Agent and checked can_fetch for the seed URL, exactly as the crawler does. We left out nodejs.org (its robots.txt disallows us), the Oracle Java tutorial and Microsoft Learn (both answer 403 Forbidden), and rachelbythebay.com (unreachable). Two sites ask for a longer Crawl-delay (gnu.org 4 s, brendangregg.com 10 s). The crawler from Chapter 2 already obeys that, so they're simply slower.

Keeping everything running at once. Until now we ran crawl, then index, then rank, then serve, one after another. In this chapter they run together: a new live command crawls non-stop in a background thread, and re-indexes and re-ranks the new pages every 10 minutes. Meanwhile, serve notices new vectors and reloads them. Three changes make that possible:

  • WAL mode for SQLite. Normally a program writing to SQLite blocks everyone else. In write-ahead logging (WAL) mode, writes go to a separate -wal file first, so readers keep reading the last committed data while one writer works. Writers still take turns, so we also tell SQLite to wait up to 60 seconds for its turn instead of failing at once with "database is locked".
  • A background thread for the crawl. A thread runs code alongside the main program in the same process. The crawl thread mostly waits on the network, while the main thread does the heavy indexing.
  • Auto-refresh in the search engine. Before each search, one quick query asks "has the largest chunk id with an embedding, or the number of PageRank rows, changed?" If so, the engine reloads its vector matrix and PageRank. That makes one search slower by a second or two every 10 minutes, and all the others are unaffected.

Why the fan stayed quiet: we watched top while indexing and saw 14 extraction workers at only ~11% each. The old loop handed out 200 pages, then waited for all 200 before starting the next 200. One huge page, such as a 2 MB reference page, kept one core busy for seconds while the other 13 sat idle. Bigger rounds (1,000 pages), with one page per task, roughly doubled extraction speed (about 13 → 33 pages a second on an M4 Pro). The crawler itself barely uses the CPU (~2%), and nothing in it would benefit from the GPU.

    tests/test_cli.py
    import pytest

from seekr import config
from seekr.__main__ import main


def test_read_seeds_skips_blanks_and_comments(tmp_path):
    seeds = tmp_path / "seeds.txt"
    seeds.write_text("# my seeds\nhttps://a.com/\n\n  https://b.com/  \n")
    assert config.read_seeds(seeds) == ["https://a.com/", "https://b.com/"]


def test_project_seeds_are_web_urls_without_repeats():
    seeds = config.read_seeds()
    assert all(seed.startswith(("http://", "https://")) and " " not in seed for seed in seeds)
    assert len(seeds) == len(set(seeds))


def test_stats_prints_seed_count(capsys):
    main(["stats"])
    assert f"seeds:    {len(config.read_seeds())}" in capsys.readouterr().out


def test_unknown_command_fails():
    with pytest.raises(SystemExit):
        main(["fly"])
    The seed test no longer checks an exact number, which broke every time we added a site. It checks what actually matters: every line is a web URL with no spaces (two URLs pasted onto one line would fail that), and no seed appears twice. The stats test compares with however many seeds there are.
  3. Skip malformed links instead of crashing (whole updated seekr/urls.py)
    seekr/urls.py
    """URL helpers: clean up links and decide which ones we are allowed to crawl."""
from urllib.parse import parse_qsl, urlencode, urljoin, urlsplit, urlunsplit

TRACKING_PARAMS = {"fbclid", "gclid", "ref", "ref_src"}
SKIP_EXTENSIONS = (
    ".pdf", ".zip", ".gz", ".tar", ".png", ".jpg", ".jpeg", ".gif", ".svg", ".webp",
    ".ico", ".css", ".js", ".json", ".xml", ".txt", ".mp3", ".mp4", ".epub",
)


def normalize_url(href, base=""):
    """Make a link absolute and canonical, or return None if it isn't a web link."""
    try:
        parts = urlsplit(urljoin(base, href.strip()))
    except ValueError:  # malformed links like "http://[oops/" exist on real pages
        return None
    if parts.scheme not in ("http", "https") or not parts.netloc:
        return None
    query = [
        (key, value)
        for key, value in parse_qsl(parts.query)
        if not key.startswith("utm_") and key not in TRACKING_PARAMS
    ]
    return urlunsplit((parts.scheme.lower(), parts.netloc.lower(), parts.path or "/", urlencode(query), ""))


def build_scopes(seeds):
    """Each seed allows its host plus the folder it lives in, e.g. docs.python.org + /3/."""
    scopes = []
    for seed in seeds:
        parts = urlsplit(normalize_url(seed))
        folder = parts.path[: parts.path.rfind("/") + 1]
        scopes.append((parts.netloc, folder))
    return scopes


def in_scope(url, scopes):
    parts = urlsplit(url)
    if parts.path.lower().endswith(SKIP_EXTENSIONS):
        return False
    return any(parts.netloc == host and parts.path.startswith(folder) for host, folder in scopes)
    A bug we really hit: with 51 sites, the crawler eventually found a page with a broken link like http://[oops/. Square brackets in a host mean an IPv6 address, so urlsplit raised ValueError: Invalid IPv6 URL. Nothing caught it, so asyncio.gather stopped the whole crawl. The fix is a try/except that treats an unparseable link like any other link we can't use, returning None. Nothing is lost: the frontier is in SQLite, and the next crawl resumes.
  4. Test it (whole updated tests/test_urls.py)
    tests/test_urls.py
    from seekr.urls import build_scopes, in_scope, normalize_url


def test_normalize_makes_links_absolute_and_clean():
    base = "https://Docs.Python.org/3/library/"
    assert normalize_url("asyncio.html#top", base) == "https://docs.python.org/3/library/asyncio.html"
    assert normalize_url("https://a.com?utm_source=x&page=2") == "https://a.com/?page=2"


def test_normalize_rejects_non_web_links():
    assert normalize_url("mailto:me@example.com") is None
    assert normalize_url("javascript:void(0)") is None
    assert normalize_url("http://[oops/") is None  # Python raises "Invalid IPv6 URL" here


def test_scopes_keep_the_seed_folder():
    scopes = build_scopes(["https://docs.python.org/3/", "https://paulgraham.com/articles.html"])
    assert scopes == [("docs.python.org", "/3/"), ("paulgraham.com", "/")]
    assert in_scope("https://docs.python.org/3/library/os.html", scopes)
    assert not in_scope("https://docs.python.org/2/library/os.html", scopes)
    assert not in_scope("https://evil.com/3/", scopes)
    assert not in_scope("https://paulgraham.com/essay.pdf", scopes)
    One new line in test_normalize_rejects_non_web_links. Run it against the old urls.py first, and you'll see the same ValueError the crawler hit.
  5. Faster extraction, and skip pages that break the extractor (whole updated seekr/extract.py)
    seekr/extract.py
    """Turn raw HTML into clean text, split it into overlapping chunks, and store them."""
import hashlib
import json
import re
from concurrent.futures import ProcessPoolExecutor

import trafilatura

from seekr import config

HTML_LANG = re.compile(r"<html[^>]*\blang=[\"']?([a-zA-Z-]+)", re.IGNORECASE)


def extract(html, url, page_title=""):
    """Main text plus metadata, or None if the page has no real content (e.g. a pure link list)."""
    result = trafilatura.extract(html, url=url, output_format="json", with_metadata=True)
    if not result:
        return None
    data = json.loads(result)
    lang = HTML_LANG.search(html[:2000])
    return {
        "title": page_title or data.get("title") or url,
        "description": data.get("excerpt") or "",
        "published": data.get("date") or "",
        "language": lang.group(1).lower() if lang else "",
        "text": data["text"].replace("¶", ""),  # Sphinx docs add ¶ after every heading
    }


def chunk_words(text, size=config.CHUNK_WORDS, overlap=config.CHUNK_OVERLAP):
    """Split text into chunks of `size` words; neighbouring chunks share `overlap` words."""
    words = text.split()
    step = size - overlap
    return [" ".join(words[start : start + size]) for start in range(0, max(len(words) - overlap, 1), step)]


def content_hash(text):
    normalized = " ".join(text.lower().split())
    return hashlib.sha256(normalized.encode()).hexdigest()


def save_document(conn, url, doc):
    """Insert one document and its chunks. Returns 'new', 'duplicate' or 'empty'."""
    if doc is None:
        conn.execute("INSERT INTO documents (url, word_count) VALUES (?, 0)", (url,))
        return "empty"
    digest = content_hash(doc["text"])
    original = conn.execute(
        "SELECT id FROM documents WHERE content_hash = ? AND duplicate_of IS NULL", (digest,)
    ).fetchone()
    cur = conn.execute(
        "INSERT INTO documents (url, title, description, published, language, word_count, content_hash,"
        " duplicate_of) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)",
        (url, doc["title"], doc["description"], doc["published"], doc["language"],
         len(doc["text"].split()), digest, original["id"] if original else None),
    )
    if original:
        return "duplicate"
    chunks = [(cur.lastrowid, i, text) for i, text in enumerate(chunk_words(doc["text"]))]
    conn.executemany("INSERT INTO chunks (doc_id, position, text) VALUES (?, ?, ?)", chunks)
    return "new"


def extract_row(row):
    try:
        return extract(row[1], row[2], row[3])
    except Exception:  # e.g. trafilatura writing broken JSON for one odd page: skip that page, keep going
        return None


def index_pages(conn, batch_size=1000):
    """Extract every page that has no document yet, using all CPU cores. Returns counts."""
    sql = """SELECT p.url, p.html, p.final_url, p.title FROM pages p
             LEFT JOIN documents d ON d.url = p.url WHERE d.url IS NULL LIMIT ?"""
    counts = {"new": 0, "duplicate": 0, "empty": 0}
    with ProcessPoolExecutor() as pool:
        while rows := [tuple(row) for row in conn.execute(sql, (batch_size,))]:
            # One page per task, so a single huge page never leaves the other cores waiting.
            for row, doc in zip(rows, pool.map(extract_row, rows)):
                counts[save_document(conn, row[0], doc)] += 1
            conn.commit()
            print(f"  extracted {sum(counts.values())} pages...", flush=True)
    return counts
    • batch_size=1000 and plain pool.map(extract_row, rows) (one page per task) keep all cores busy, as explained above.
    • The try/except in extract_row is the second real crash we hit: for one odd page, trafilatura produced JSON with an invalid \escape, and json.loads raised. That killed the whole run. Now the page is saved as "no main text" and indexing goes on. except Exception is normally too broad, but here one page's text is worth far less than the other 20,000.
  6. Let several programs share the database (whole updated seekr/db.py)
    seekr/db.py
    """SQLite storage: one file on disk, a few small tables."""
import json
import sqlite3
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from pathlib import Path

from seekr import config

SCHEMA = """
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS frontier (
    url    TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    host   TEXT NOT NULL,
    depth  INTEGER NOT NULL,
    status TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'queued'  -- queued, done, failed, error or blocked
);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS frontier_next ON frontier (host, status, depth);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS pages (
    url        TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    final_url  TEXT,
    status     INTEGER,
    title      TEXT,
    html       TEXT,
    outlinks   TEXT,  -- JSON list of absolute URLs
    fetched_at TEXT
);

-- One row per crawled page. Duplicates and pages without main text get no chunks.
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS documents (
    id           INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
    url          TEXT UNIQUE NOT NULL,
    title        TEXT,
    description  TEXT,
    published    TEXT,
    language     TEXT,
    word_count   INTEGER,
    content_hash TEXT,
    duplicate_of INTEGER
);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS documents_hash ON documents (content_hash);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS chunks (
    id       INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
    doc_id   INTEGER NOT NULL,
    position INTEGER NOT NULL,
    text     TEXT NOT NULL
);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS embeddings (
    chunk_id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
    vector   BLOB NOT NULL  -- float32 bytes, length 1.0
);
"""


def connect(path=None):
    path = Path(path or config.DB_PATH)
    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    conn = sqlite3.connect(path, timeout=60)  # wait (up to 60 s) for another program's write instead of failing
    conn.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL")  # lets crawl, index and serve use the database at the same time
    conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
    conn.executescript(SCHEMA)
    return conn


def enqueue(conn, urls, depth):
    rows = [(url, url.split("/")[2], depth) for url in urls]
    conn.executemany("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO frontier (url, host, depth) VALUES (?, ?, ?)", rows)


def next_url(conn, host):
    return conn.execute(
        "SELECT url, depth FROM frontier WHERE host = ? AND status = 'queued' ORDER BY depth LIMIT 1",
        (host,),
    ).fetchone()


def mark(conn, url, status):
    conn.execute("UPDATE frontier SET status = ? WHERE url = ?", (status, url))


def redirect_targets(conn, urls):
    """Where these URLs ended up after redirects, for the ones that were redirected."""
    marks = ",".join("?" * len(urls))
    sql = f"SELECT final_url FROM pages WHERE url IN ({marks}) AND final_url != url"
    return [row["final_url"] for row in conn.execute(sql, urls)]


def requeue_errors(conn):
    conn.execute("UPDATE frontier SET status = 'queued' WHERE status = 'error'")


def count_done(conn, host):
    sql = "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM frontier WHERE host = ? AND status = 'done'"
    return conn.execute(sql, (host,)).fetchone()[0]


def save_page(conn, url, final_url, status, title, html, outlinks):
    fetched_at = datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
    conn.execute(
        "INSERT OR REPLACE INTO pages VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)",
        (url, final_url, status, title, html, json.dumps(outlinks), fetched_at),
    )


def host_stats(conn):
    return conn.execute(
        """SELECT host,
                  SUM(status = 'done')    AS done,
                  SUM(status = 'queued')  AS queued,
                  SUM(status = 'failed')  AS failed,
                  SUM(status = 'error')   AS error,
                  SUM(status = 'blocked') AS blocked
           FROM frontier GROUP BY host ORDER BY host"""
    ).fetchall()
    Two new lines in connect: timeout=60 (wait for a writer instead of failing) and PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL. WAL mode is stored in the database file itself, so setting it on every connection is harmless.
  7. Reload new vectors automatically (whole updated seekr/search.py)
    seekr/search.py
    """Hybrid search: keyword + semantic candidates, then a cross-encoder decides how relevant each page is."""
import html
import math
import re
from datetime import date
from urllib.parse import urlsplit

import numpy as np
import torch
from sentence_transformers import CrossEncoder

from seekr import config, embed, index

CHUNK_SQL = """SELECT c.doc_id, c.text, d.url, d.title, d.published, d.language, d.word_count
               FROM chunks c JOIN documents d ON d.id = c.doc_id WHERE c.id = ?"""
STOPWORDS = {"the", "and", "for", "how", "can", "what", "with", "does", "are", "you", "your", "from", "that", "this",
             "when", "why", "which", "into", "about", "same"}
_reranker = None


def get_reranker():
    global _reranker
    if _reranker is None:
        device = embed.best_device()
        _reranker = CrossEncoder(config.RERANK_MODEL, device=device, max_length=config.RERANK_MAX_TOKENS)
        if device != "cpu":
            _reranker.model.half()
    return _reranker


def sigmoid(x):
    return 1 / (1 + np.exp(-x))


def rrf(rankings, k=60):
    """Reciprocal Rank Fusion. rankings: {name: [(id, score), ...] best first} -> (fused scores, ranks)."""
    fused, ranks = {}, {}
    for name, hits in rankings.items():
        for rank, (item, _) in enumerate(hits, start=1):
            fused[item] = fused.get(item, 0.0) + 1 / (k + rank)
            ranks.setdefault(item, {})[name] = rank
    return fused, ranks


def query_words(query):
    return re.findall(r"\w+", query.lower())


def highlight(text, query, width=300):
    """A window of text around the first query word, HTML-escaped, with query words wrapped in <mark>."""
    words = [re.escape(word) for word in query_words(query) if len(word) > 2 and word not in STOPWORDS]
    pattern = re.compile(r"\b(?:" + "|".join(words) + r")\w*", re.IGNORECASE) if words else None
    first = pattern.search(text) if pattern else None
    start = max(0, first.start() - 80) if first else 0
    start = text.find(" ", start) + 1 if start else 0  # begin on a whole word
    window = ("…" if start else "") + text[start : start + width] + ("…" if start + width < len(text) else "")
    parts, last = [], 0
    for match in pattern.finditer(window) if pattern else []:
        parts += [html.escape(window[last : match.start()]), "<mark>", html.escape(match.group()), "</mark>"]
        last = match.end()
    return "".join(parts) + html.escape(window[last:])


def freshness(published):
    """1.0 for a page from today, 0.5 after a year, 0.33 after two... and 0.5 when there is no date."""
    try:
        age_years = (date.today() - date.fromisoformat(published[:10])).days / 365
    except (TypeError, ValueError):
        return 0.5
    return 1 / (1 + max(age_years, 0))


class SearchEngine:
    def __init__(self, conn):
        self.conn = conn
        self.version = None
        self.refresh()

    def refresh(self):
        """Reload the vectors and PageRank if `index` or `rank` changed them since the last load."""
        version = tuple(self.conn.execute(
            "SELECT (SELECT MAX(chunk_id) FROM embeddings), (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM pagerank)").fetchone())
        if version == self.version:
            return
        self.version = version
        self.vectors = embed.VectorIndex(self.conn)
        logs = {doc: math.log(score) for doc, score in self.conn.execute("SELECT doc_id, score FROM pagerank")}
        low, high = min(logs.values(), default=0.0), max(logs.values(), default=1.0)
        self.pagerank = {doc: (value - low) / ((high - low) or 1) for doc, value in logs.items()}  # 0..1

    def candidates(self, query, mode="full", site=None):
        """Best chunk per page from BM25 and/or vectors, fused with RRF. English pages only."""
        rankings = {}
        if mode in ("bm25", "hybrid", "full"):
            rankings["bm25"] = index.keyword_search(self.conn, query, config.CANDIDATES)
        if mode in ("vector", "hybrid", "full"):
            rankings["vector"] = self.vectors.search(embed.embed_query(query), config.CANDIDATES)
        fused, ranks = rrf(rankings)
        pages, seen = [], set()
        for chunk_id in sorted(fused, key=fused.get, reverse=True):
            page = dict(self.conn.execute(CHUNK_SQL, (chunk_id,)).fetchone())
            host = urlsplit(page["url"]).netloc
            if page["doc_id"] in seen or not (page["language"] or "en").startswith("en"):
                continue
            if site and host != site and not host.endswith("." + site):
                continue
            seen.add(page["doc_id"])
            pages.append({**page, "ranks": ranks[chunk_id]})
        return pages

    def rerank(self, query, pages):
        """Score each page: mostly the cross-encoder's relevance, plus authority, freshness and exact matches."""
        if not pages:
            return []
        pairs = [(query, f"{page['title']}\n{page['text']}") for page in pages]
        logits = get_reranker().predict(pairs, batch_size=16, activation_fn=torch.nn.Identity())
        words = query_words(query)
        for page, logit in zip(pages, logits):
            title_and_url = f"{page['title']} {page['url']}".lower()
            page["relevance"] = float(sigmoid(logit))
            page["pagerank"] = self.pagerank.get(page["doc_id"], 0.0)
            page["freshness"] = freshness(page["published"])
            page["exact"] = sum(word in title_and_url for word in words) / max(len(words), 1)
            page["final_score"] = (
                config.W_RELEVANCE * page["relevance"] + config.W_PAGERANK * page["pagerank"]
                + config.W_FRESHNESS * page["freshness"] + config.W_EXACT * page["exact"]
            )
            if page["word_count"] < config.THIN_PAGE_WORDS:
                page["final_score"] *= config.THIN_PAGE_PENALTY
        return sorted(pages, key=lambda page: page["final_score"], reverse=True)

    def search(self, query, limit=10, offset=0, site=None, mode="full"):
        self.refresh()
        pages = self.candidates(query, mode, site)
        if mode == "full":
            pages = self.rerank(query, pages[: config.RERANK_TOP])
        return [self.to_result(page, query) for page in pages[offset : offset + limit]]

    def to_result(self, page, query):
        return {
            "url": page["url"],
            "title": page["title"] or page["url"],
            "snippet": highlight(page["text"], query),
            "relevance": page.get("relevance"),
            "final_score": page.get("final_score"),
            "signals": {
                "bm25_rank": page["ranks"].get("bm25"),
                "vector_rank": page["ranks"].get("vector"),
                "relevance": page.get("relevance"),
                "pagerank": page.get("pagerank"),
                "freshness": page.get("freshness"),
            },
        }
    __init__ now calls the new refresh, and so does search. refresh compares a cheap version (the largest embedded chunk id plus the number of PageRank rows) with the one from the last load, and reloads only when it changed. MAX(chunk_id) is instant because chunk_id is the primary key, so SQLite reads it straight from the index.

    seekr/config.py
    """All settings in one place. Override any of them with an environment variable."""
import os
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent

DB_PATH = Path(os.environ.get("SEEKR_DB", ROOT / "data" / "seekr.db"))
SEEDS_FILE = Path(os.environ.get("SEEKR_SEEDS", ROOT / "seeds.txt"))
USER_AGENT = os.environ.get("SEEKR_USER_AGENT", "SeekrBot/0.1")
HOST = os.environ.get("SEEKR_HOST", "127.0.0.1")
PORT = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_PORT", "8000"))

# Crawler
MAX_DEPTH = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_MAX_DEPTH", "3"))
MAX_PAGES_PER_DOMAIN = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_MAX_PAGES_PER_DOMAIN", "300"))
LIVE_INTERVAL = float(os.environ.get("SEEKR_LIVE_INTERVAL", "600"))  # seconds between index runs in `live`
CRAWL_DELAY = float(os.environ.get("SEEKR_CRAWL_DELAY", "1.0"))  # seconds between requests to one host
REQUEST_TIMEOUT = float(os.environ.get("SEEKR_REQUEST_TIMEOUT", "15"))
MAX_PAGE_BYTES = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_MAX_PAGE_BYTES", "2000000"))
RETRIES = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_RETRIES", "2"))

# Extraction
CHUNK_WORDS = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_CHUNK_WORDS", "300"))
CHUNK_OVERLAP = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_CHUNK_OVERLAP", "50"))

# Embeddings (runs on the GPU when there is one)
EMBED_MODEL = os.environ.get("SEEKR_EMBED_MODEL", "BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5")
EMBED_BATCH = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_EMBED_BATCH", "64"))
DEVICE = os.environ.get("SEEKR_DEVICE", "")  # "", "cuda", "mps" or "cpu"; "" picks the best one

# Search and relevance scoring
CANDIDATES = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_CANDIDATES", "100"))  # from each of BM25 and vectors
RERANK_TOP = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_RERANK_TOP", "30"))  # pages the cross-encoder reads per query
# MiniLM beat BAAI/bge-reranker-base on our eval (MRR 0.733 vs 0.648) and is 3x faster; try either.
RERANK_MODEL = os.environ.get("SEEKR_RERANK_MODEL", "cross-encoder/ms-marco-MiniLM-L-6-v2")
RERANK_MAX_TOKENS = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_RERANK_MAX_TOKENS", "384"))
# Tuned on evals/queries.jsonl. Freshness is 0: docs show "last updated today", so dates mislead.
W_RELEVANCE, W_PAGERANK, W_FRESHNESS, W_EXACT = 0.90, 0.05, 0.0, 0.05
THIN_PAGE_WORDS, THIN_PAGE_PENALTY = 50, 0.8

# REST API
RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE", "60"))  # per API key
CORS_ORIGINS = os.environ.get("SEEKR_CORS_ORIGINS", "*").split(",")  # websites allowed to call the API

# Website
UI_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_UI_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE", "30"))  # per visitor IP

# Operations
LOG_LEVEL = os.environ.get("SEEKR_LOG_LEVEL", "INFO")  # DEBUG shows every fetched URL


def read_seeds(path=None):
    """Return the seed URLs, skipping blank lines and # comments."""
    lines = Path(path or SEEDS_FILE).read_text().splitlines()
    return [line.strip() for line in lines if line.strip() and not line.startswith("#")]
    LIVE_INTERVAL: seconds between index runs in live mode, 10 minutes by default.
  9. Add the live command (whole updated seekr/__main__.py)
    seekr/__main__.py
    """Command-line entry point: python -m seekr <command>."""
import argparse
import asyncio
import logging
import sys
import threading
import time

from seekr import api, config, crawler, db, doctor, embed, evaluate, extract, index, rank, search, web


def print_host_stats(conn):
    print(f"{'host':26} {'done':>5} {'queued':>7} {'failed':>7} {'error':>6} {'blocked':>8}")
    for r in db.host_stats(conn):
        print(f"{r['host']:26} {r['done']:5} {r['queued']:7} {r['failed']:7} {r['error']:6} {r['blocked']:8}")


def cmd_crawl(args):
    conn = db.connect()
    asyncio.run(crawler.crawl(config.read_seeds(), conn))
    print_host_stats(conn)


def cmd_index(args):
    conn = db.connect()
    print("Extracting text from crawled pages:")
    counts = extract.index_pages(conn)
    print(f"new: {counts['new']}  duplicates: {counts['duplicate']}  no main text: {counts['empty']}")
    print(f"Keyword index: added {index.build_index(conn)} chunks")
    print(f"Embedding chunks on {embed.best_device()}:")
    print(f"embedded: {embed.embed_chunks(conn)}")


def cmd_rank(args):
    conn = db.connect()
    pages, links = rank.compute_pagerank(conn)
    print(f"PageRank over {pages} pages and {links} links. Top 10:")
    top = conn.execute(
        "SELECT r.score, d.url FROM pagerank r JOIN documents d ON d.id = r.doc_id ORDER BY r.score DESC LIMIT 10"
    )
    for row in top:
        print(f"  {row['score']:.5f}  {row['url']}")


def cmd_live(args):
    """Crawl non-stop in a background thread, and index and rank the new pages every few minutes."""
    crawling = threading.Thread(target=cmd_crawl, args=(args,), daemon=True)
    crawling.start()
    while True:
        cmd_index(args)
        cmd_rank(args)
        if not crawling.is_alive():
            break
        crawling.join(timeout=config.LIVE_INTERVAL)  # wait, but wake up early if the crawl ends
    print("The crawl has run out of links. Add seeds or raise SEEKR_MAX_PAGES_PER_DOMAIN to keep growing.")


def cmd_search(args):
    engine = search.SearchEngine(db.connect())
    for i, result in enumerate(engine.search(" ".join(args.query)), start=1):
        print(f"{i:2}. [{result['relevance']:.2f}] {result['title']}\n    {result['url']}")


def cmd_eval(args):
    engine = search.SearchEngine(db.connect())
    queries = evaluate.load_queries()
    print(f"{len(queries)} queries   {'MRR@10':>7} {'nDCG@10':>8}")
    for mode, (mrr, ndcg) in evaluate.evaluate(engine, queries).items():
        print(f"{mode:18} {mrr:7.3f} {ndcg:8.3f}")


def cmd_serve(args):
    import uvicorn

    print(f"Seekr on http://{config.HOST}:{config.PORT}  (search page: /, API docs: /docs)")
    uvicorn.run(web.create_site(), host=config.HOST, port=config.PORT)


def cmd_keys(args):
    key = api.create_key(db.connect(), args.name, admin=args.admin)
    print(f"New {'admin ' if args.admin else ''}API key for {args.name!r} (shown once, store it safely):\n{key}")


def cmd_doctor(args):
    sys.exit(0 if doctor.run_checks() else 1)


def cmd_stats(args):
    seeds = config.read_seeds()
    db_state = "exists" if config.DB_PATH.exists() else "not created yet"
    print(f"seeds:    {len(seeds)}")
    print(f"database: {config.DB_PATH} ({db_state})")
    if config.DB_PATH.exists():
        conn = db.connect()
        print_host_stats(conn)
        for table in ("pages", "documents", "chunks", "embeddings"):
            print(f"{table + ':':10} {conn.execute(f'SELECT COUNT(*) FROM {table}').fetchone()[0]}")


COMMANDS = {
    "crawl": cmd_crawl,
    "index": cmd_index,
    "rank": cmd_rank,
    "live": cmd_live,
    "serve": cmd_serve,
    "stats": cmd_stats,
    "search": cmd_search,
    "eval": cmd_eval,
    "keys": cmd_keys,
    "doctor": cmd_doctor,
}


def main(argv=None):
    logging.basicConfig(level=config.LOG_LEVEL, format="%(asctime)s %(levelname)-7s %(name)s: %(message)s",
                        datefmt="%H:%M:%S")
    for noisy in ("sentence_transformers", "httpx", "httpx2"):  # progress bars and one line per HTTP request
        logging.getLogger(noisy).setLevel(logging.WARNING)
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog="seekr", description="Seekr search engine")
    sub = parser.add_subparsers(dest="command", required=True)
    for name in COMMANDS:
        command = sub.add_parser(name)
        if name == "search":
            command.add_argument("query", nargs="+")
        if name == "keys":
            command.add_argument("action", choices=["create"])
            command.add_argument("name", help="who or what the key is for")
            command.add_argument("--admin", action="store_true", help="may add seeds")
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
    COMMANDS[args.command](args)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
    • threading.Thread(target=cmd_crawl, daemon=True) runs the normal crawl command in a background thread. daemon=True means it won't keep the program alive after Ctrl+C. That's safe, because every page is its own SQLite transaction and the crawl resumes next time.
    • The loop indexes and ranks first, because there may already be pages waiting, then waits with crawling.join(timeout=...). Unlike time.sleep, join returns early if the crawl thread ends, so the last pages are indexed straight away.
    • Each command opens its own connection, because a SQLite connection belongs to the thread that created it.

    import time

from seekr import __main__ as cli

def add_page(conn, doc_id, text):
    conn.execute("INSERT INTO documents (id, url, title, language, word_count, published) VALUES (?, ?, ?, 'en', ?, '')",
                 (doc_id, f"https://x.com/{doc_id}", text, len(text.split())))
    conn.execute("INSERT INTO chunks (id, doc_id, position, text) VALUES (?, ?, 0, ?)", (doc_id, doc_id, text))


def test_database_can_be_read_while_another_program_writes(tmp_path):
    writer, reader = db.connect(tmp_path / "t.db"), db.connect(tmp_path / "t.db")
    assert writer.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode").fetchone()[0] == "wal"
    db.enqueue(writer, ["https://a.com/"], depth=0)  # an open, uncommitted write
    assert reader.execute("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM frontier").fetchone()[0] == 0  # readers aren't blocked


def test_search_engine_picks_up_new_embeddings(tmp_path):
    conn = db.connect(tmp_path / "t.db")
    conn.execute(rank.SCHEMA)
    add_page(conn, 1, "Rust ownership rules")
    embed.embed_chunks(conn)
    engine = search.SearchEngine(conn)
    assert engine.vectors.chunk_ids == [1]
    add_page(conn, 2, "Python asyncio event loop")
    embed.embed_chunks(conn)
    engine.refresh()
    assert engine.vectors.chunk_ids == [1, 2]

    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "cmd_index", lambda args: calls.append("index"))

    assert len(calls) >= 6

    monkeypatch.setattr(extract, "extract", broken)
