  15. Leave live mode running
    SEEKR_CONTACT="Your Name; you@example.com" SEEKR_MAX_PAGES_PER_DOMAIN=5000 SEEKR_MAX_DEPTH=5 python -m seekr live
    From now on Seekr grows by itself: it crawls in 30-minute rounds, and every 10 minutes it indexes, ranks and discovers new sites. Stop it with Ctrl+C whenever you like. Everything resumes from the database.

Checkpoint

python -m seekr eval

Site authority should help a little, or at worst change nothing. In our run, with a 20× bigger index, the reranked search scored MRR 0.706 without it (SEEKR_W_SITE=0) and 0.716 with it (nDCG 0.727 → 0.735). All the scores are lower than in Chapter 7 because many more pages now compete for every query.

pytest never finishes
The live test is running a real crawl: check that it monkeypatches crawl_forever, not cmd_crawl.
sqlite3.OperationalError: no such table: sites
The new tables are missing from SCHEMA in db.py. connect() creates them.
Discovery adds Facebook, Twitter or link shorteners
Add them to SKIP_SITES.
Wikipedia pages fail with 403
SEEKR_CONTACT isn't set in the terminal that runs live. Pages refused before you set it stay "failed". Requeue them: UPDATE frontier SET status='queued' WHERE host LIKE 'en.wik%' AND status='failed'.
Every candidate is "skipped"
Check your network, and that fetch follows redirects. Many homepages redirect, for example to /en-us.

Chapter 13 · Fast searches & a bigger machine ready

After a day of live mode our index had 1.4 million chunks and searches had become slow. This chapter fixes that, and shows how to move Seekr onto a bigger computer when your laptop's disk fills up.

Why searches got slow: Chapter 11 made search() call refresh() every time. While live mode runs, new embeddings land every few seconds, so almost every search saw a new version and reloaded all the vectors: 700,000 of them, about 1 GB, read back from SQLite. The fix is to check for new data at most every 5 minutes. New pages still appear in results, just up to 5 minutes later.

Why move: the database keeps every page's HTML, so it grows fast. Ours reached 100 GB in a day. Everything lives in one SQLite file, so moving Seekr means copying the code, then copying that one file.

  1. Add REFRESH_SECONDS (whole updated seekr/config.py)
    seekr/config.py
    """All settings in one place. Override any of them with an environment variable."""
import os
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent

DB_PATH = Path(os.environ.get("SEEKR_DB", ROOT / "data" / "seekr.db"))
SEEDS_FILE = Path(os.environ.get("SEEKR_SEEDS", ROOT / "seeds.txt"))
CONTACT = os.environ.get("SEEKR_CONTACT", "")  # e.g. "Your Name; you@example.com". Some sites (Wikipedia) require it
USER_AGENT = os.environ.get("SEEKR_USER_AGENT", f"SeekrBot/0.1 ({CONTACT})" if CONTACT else "SeekrBot/0.1")
HOST = os.environ.get("SEEKR_HOST", "127.0.0.1")
PORT = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_PORT", "8000"))

# Crawler
MAX_DEPTH = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_MAX_DEPTH", "3"))
MAX_PAGES_PER_DOMAIN = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_MAX_PAGES_PER_DOMAIN", "300"))
LIVE_INTERVAL = float(os.environ.get("SEEKR_LIVE_INTERVAL", "600"))  # seconds between index runs in `live`
CRAWL_ROUND = float(os.environ.get("SEEKR_CRAWL_ROUND", "1800"))  # `live` restarts the crawl this often, with new sites
DISCOVER_MIN_LINKS = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_DISCOVER_MIN_LINKS", "3"))  # other sites that must link to a new one
DISCOVER_PER_ROUND = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_DISCOVER_PER_ROUND", "20"))  # sites checked per `discover`
CRAWL_DELAY = float(os.environ.get("SEEKR_CRAWL_DELAY", "1.0"))  # seconds between requests to one host
REQUEST_TIMEOUT = float(os.environ.get("SEEKR_REQUEST_TIMEOUT", "15"))
MAX_PAGE_BYTES = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_MAX_PAGE_BYTES", "5000000"))  # long Wikipedia articles are over 2 MB
RETRIES = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_RETRIES", "2"))

# Extraction
CHUNK_WORDS = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_CHUNK_WORDS", "300"))
CHUNK_OVERLAP = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_CHUNK_OVERLAP", "50"))

# Embeddings (runs on the GPU when there is one)
EMBED_MODEL = os.environ.get("SEEKR_EMBED_MODEL", "BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5")
EMBED_BATCH = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_EMBED_BATCH", "64"))
DEVICE = os.environ.get("SEEKR_DEVICE", "")  # "", "cuda", "mps" or "cpu"; "" picks the best one

# Search and relevance scoring
CANDIDATES = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_CANDIDATES", "100"))  # from each of BM25 and vectors
RERANK_TOP = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_RERANK_TOP", "30"))  # pages the cross-encoder reads per query
# MiniLM beat BAAI/bge-reranker-base on our eval (MRR 0.733 vs 0.648) and is 3x faster; try either.
RERANK_MODEL = os.environ.get("SEEKR_RERANK_MODEL", "cross-encoder/ms-marco-MiniLM-L-6-v2")
RERANK_MAX_TOKENS = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_RERANK_MAX_TOKENS", "384"))
# Tuned on evals/queries.jsonl. Freshness is 0: docs show "last updated today", so dates mislead.
W_RELEVANCE, W_PAGERANK, W_FRESHNESS, W_EXACT = 0.90, 0.05, 0.0, 0.05
W_SITE = float(os.environ.get("SEEKR_W_SITE", "0.05"))  # how many other sites link to the result's site
THIN_PAGE_WORDS, THIN_PAGE_PENALTY = 50, 0.8
REFRESH_SECONDS = float(os.environ.get("SEEKR_REFRESH_SECONDS", "300"))  # how often search picks up new pages

# REST API
RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE", "60"))  # per API key
CORS_ORIGINS = os.environ.get("SEEKR_CORS_ORIGINS", "*").split(",")  # websites allowed to call the API

# Website
UI_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE = int(os.environ.get("SEEKR_UI_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE", "30"))  # per visitor IP

# Operations
LOG_LEVEL = os.environ.get("SEEKR_LOG_LEVEL", "INFO")  # DEBUG shows every fetched URL


def read_seeds(path=None):
    """Return the seed URLs, skipping blank lines and # comments."""
    lines = Path(path or SEEDS_FILE).read_text().splitlines()
    return [line.strip() for line in lines if line.strip() and not line.startswith("#")]
    The only new line is REFRESH_SECONDS, under the scoring weights. 300 seconds is 5 minutes. Tests set it to 0 to force a reload.
  2. Reload at most every REFRESH_SECONDS (whole updated seekr/search.py)
    seekr/search.py
    """Hybrid search: keyword + semantic candidates, then a cross-encoder decides how relevant each page is."""
import html
import math
import re
import time
from datetime import date
from urllib.parse import urlsplit

import numpy as np
import torch
from sentence_transformers import CrossEncoder

from seekr import config, discover, embed, index

CHUNK_SQL = """SELECT c.doc_id, c.text, d.url, d.title, d.published, d.language, d.word_count
               FROM chunks c JOIN documents d ON d.id = c.doc_id WHERE c.id = ?"""
STOPWORDS = {"the", "and", "for", "how", "can", "what", "with", "does", "are", "you", "your", "from", "that", "this",
             "when", "why", "which", "into", "about", "same"}
_reranker = None


def get_reranker():
    global _reranker
    if _reranker is None:
        device = embed.best_device()
        _reranker = CrossEncoder(config.RERANK_MODEL, device=device, max_length=config.RERANK_MAX_TOKENS)
        if device != "cpu":
            _reranker.model.half()
    return _reranker


def sigmoid(x):
    return 1 / (1 + np.exp(-x))


def rrf(rankings, k=60):
    """Reciprocal Rank Fusion. rankings: {name: [(id, score), ...] best first} -> (fused scores, ranks)."""
    fused, ranks = {}, {}
    for name, hits in rankings.items():
        for rank, (item, _) in enumerate(hits, start=1):
            fused[item] = fused.get(item, 0.0) + 1 / (k + rank)
            ranks.setdefault(item, {})[name] = rank
    return fused, ranks


def query_words(query):
    return re.findall(r"\w+", query.lower())


def highlight(text, query, width=300):
    """A window of text around the first query word, HTML-escaped, with query words wrapped in <mark>."""
    words = [re.escape(word) for word in query_words(query) if len(word) > 2 and word not in STOPWORDS]
    pattern = re.compile(r"\b(?:" + "|".join(words) + r")\w*", re.IGNORECASE) if words else None
    first = pattern.search(text) if pattern else None
    start = max(0, first.start() - 80) if first else 0
    start = text.find(" ", start) + 1 if start else 0  # begin on a whole word
    window = ("…" if start else "") + text[start : start + width] + ("…" if start + width < len(text) else "")
    parts, last = [], 0
    for match in pattern.finditer(window) if pattern else []:
        parts += [html.escape(window[last : match.start()]), "<mark>", html.escape(match.group()), "</mark>"]
        last = match.end()
    return "".join(parts) + html.escape(window[last:])


def freshness(published):
    """1.0 for a page from today, 0.5 after a year, 0.33 after two... and 0.5 when there is no date."""
    try:
        age_years = (date.today() - date.fromisoformat(published[:10])).days / 365
    except (TypeError, ValueError):
        return 0.5
    return 1 / (1 + max(age_years, 0))


class SearchEngine:
    def __init__(self, conn):
        self.conn = conn
        self.version = None
        self.refresh()

    def refresh(self):
        """Reload the vectors and PageRank if `index` or `rank` changed them since the last load."""
        self.refreshed_at = time.monotonic()
        version = tuple(self.conn.execute(
            "SELECT (SELECT MAX(chunk_id) FROM embeddings), (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM pagerank), (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM sites)").fetchone())
        if version == self.version:
            return
        self.version = version
        self.vectors = embed.VectorIndex(self.conn)
        logs = {doc: math.log(score) for doc, score in self.conn.execute("SELECT doc_id, score FROM pagerank")}
        low, high = min(logs.values(), default=0.0), max(logs.values(), default=1.0)
        self.pagerank = {doc: (value - low) / ((high - low) or 1) for doc, value in logs.items()}  # 0..1
        self.sites = dict(self.conn.execute("SELECT site, score FROM sites").fetchall())

    def candidates(self, query, mode="full", site=None):
        """Best chunk per page from BM25 and/or vectors, fused with RRF. English pages only."""
        rankings = {}
        if mode in ("bm25", "hybrid", "full"):
            rankings["bm25"] = index.keyword_search(self.conn, query, config.CANDIDATES)
        if mode in ("vector", "hybrid", "full"):
            rankings["vector"] = self.vectors.search(embed.embed_query(query), config.CANDIDATES)
        fused, ranks = rrf(rankings)
        pages, seen = [], set()
        for chunk_id in sorted(fused, key=fused.get, reverse=True):
            page = dict(self.conn.execute(CHUNK_SQL, (chunk_id,)).fetchone())
            host = urlsplit(page["url"]).netloc
            if page["doc_id"] in seen or not (page["language"] or "en").startswith("en"):
                continue
            if site and host != site and not host.endswith("." + site):
                continue
            seen.add(page["doc_id"])
            pages.append({**page, "ranks": ranks[chunk_id]})
        return pages

    def rerank(self, query, pages):
        """Score each page: mostly the cross-encoder's relevance, plus authority, freshness and exact matches."""
        if not pages:
            return []
        pairs = [(query, f"{page['title']}\n{page['text']}") for page in pages]
        logits = get_reranker().predict(pairs, batch_size=16, activation_fn=torch.nn.Identity())
        words = query_words(query)
        for page, logit in zip(pages, logits):
            title_and_url = f"{page['title']} {page['url']}".lower()
            page["relevance"] = float(sigmoid(logit))
            page["pagerank"] = self.pagerank.get(page["doc_id"], 0.0)
            page["freshness"] = freshness(page["published"])
            page["exact"] = sum(word in title_and_url for word in words) / max(len(words), 1)
            page["site_authority"] = self.sites.get(discover.site_of(page["url"]), 0.0)
            page["final_score"] = (
                config.W_RELEVANCE * page["relevance"] + config.W_PAGERANK * page["pagerank"]
                + config.W_FRESHNESS * page["freshness"] + config.W_EXACT * page["exact"]
                + config.W_SITE * page["site_authority"]
            )
            if page["word_count"] < config.THIN_PAGE_WORDS:
                page["final_score"] *= config.THIN_PAGE_PENALTY
        return sorted(pages, key=lambda page: page["final_score"], reverse=True)

    def search(self, query, limit=10, offset=0, site=None, mode="full"):
        if time.monotonic() - self.refreshed_at > config.REFRESH_SECONDS:  # reloading takes seconds on a big index
            self.refresh()
        pages = self.candidates(query, mode, site)
        if mode == "full":
            pages = self.rerank(query, pages[: config.RERANK_TOP])
        return [self.to_result(page, query) for page in pages[offset : offset + limit]]

    def to_result(self, page, query):
        return {
            "url": page["url"],
            "title": page["title"] or page["url"],
            "snippet": highlight(page["text"], query),
            "relevance": page.get("relevance"),
            "final_score": page.get("final_score"),
            "signals": {
                "bm25_rank": page["ranks"].get("bm25"),
                "vector_rank": page["ranks"].get("vector"),
                "relevance": page.get("relevance"),
                "pagerank": page.get("pagerank"),
                "freshness": page.get("freshness"),
                "site_authority": page.get("site_authority"),
            },
        }
    refresh() now records when it last ran, in self.refreshed_at. search() only calls it once REFRESH_SECONDS have passed. time.monotonic() is a clock that never jumps backwards, even if you change the computer's time. When live mode is stopped nothing changes, so refresh stays cheap.
  3. Keep live mode running when the database is busy (whole updated seekr/__main__.py)
    seekr/__main__.py
    """Command-line entry point: python -m seekr <command>."""
import argparse
import asyncio
import logging
import sqlite3
import sys
import threading
import time

from seekr import api, config, crawler, db, discover, doctor, embed, evaluate, extract, index, rank, search, web

log = logging.getLogger("seekr.live")


def print_host_stats(conn):
    print(f"{'host':26} {'done':>5} {'queued':>7} {'failed':>7} {'error':>6} {'blocked':>8}")
    for r in db.host_stats(conn):
        print(f"{r['host']:26} {r['done']:5} {r['queued']:7} {r['failed']:7} {r['error']:6} {r['blocked']:8}")


def cmd_crawl(args):
    conn = db.connect()
    asyncio.run(crawler.crawl(config.read_seeds() + discover.found_seeds(conn), conn))
    print_host_stats(conn)


def cmd_index(args):
    conn = db.connect()
    print("Extracting text from crawled pages:")
    counts = extract.index_pages(conn)
    print(f"new: {counts['new']}  duplicates: {counts['duplicate']}  no main text: {counts['empty']}")
    print(f"Keyword index: added {index.build_index(conn)} chunks")
    print(f"Embedding chunks on {embed.best_device()}:")
    print(f"embedded: {embed.embed_chunks(conn)}")


def cmd_rank(args):
    conn = db.connect()
    pages, links = rank.compute_pagerank(conn)
    print(f"PageRank over {pages} pages and {links} links. Top 10:")
    top = conn.execute(
        "SELECT r.score, d.url FROM pagerank r JOIN documents d ON d.id = r.doc_id ORDER BY r.score DESC LIMIT 10"
    )
    for row in top:
        print(f"  {row['score']:.5f}  {row['url']}")


def cmd_discover(args):
    conn = db.connect()
    print(f"Site importance: {discover.compute_sites(conn)} sites are linked from our pages")
    for seed in discover.discover(conn, config.read_seeds()):
        print(f"  new site to crawl: {seed}")


def crawl_forever():
    """Crawl in rounds of CRAWL_ROUND seconds, so each round also crawls the sites discovered meanwhile."""
    while True:
        try:
            conn = db.connect()
            before = conn.execute("SELECT MAX(rowid) FROM pages").fetchone()[0]  # COUNT(*) would read every page
            seeds = config.read_seeds() + discover.found_seeds(conn)
            asyncio.run(crawler.crawl(seeds, conn, until=time.monotonic() + config.CRAWL_ROUND))
            if conn.execute("SELECT MAX(rowid) FROM pages").fetchone()[0] == before:
                time.sleep(config.LIVE_INTERVAL)  # nothing new to fetch: wait for discover to find more sites
        except sqlite3.OperationalError as error:  # "database is locked": the indexer wrote for too long
            log.warning("crawl round stopped (%s); starting a new one", error)


def cmd_live(args):
    """Crawl forever in a background thread. Every LIVE_INTERVAL: index, rank and discover new sites."""
    crawling = threading.Thread(target=crawl_forever, daemon=True)
    crawling.start()
    while True:
        try:
            cmd_index(args)
            cmd_rank(args)
            cmd_discover(args)
        except sqlite3.OperationalError as error:  # "database is locked": the crawler wrote for too long
            log.warning("index round stopped (%s); trying again later", error)
        if not crawling.is_alive():
            break
        crawling.join(timeout=config.LIVE_INTERVAL)  # wait, but wake up early if the crawl thread stops


def cmd_search(args):
    engine = search.SearchEngine(db.connect())
    for i, result in enumerate(engine.search(" ".join(args.query)), start=1):
        print(f"{i:2}. [{result['relevance']:.2f}] {result['title']}\n    {result['url']}")


def cmd_eval(args):
    engine = search.SearchEngine(db.connect())
    queries = evaluate.load_queries()
    print(f"{len(queries)} queries   {'MRR@10':>7} {'nDCG@10':>8}")
    for mode, (mrr, ndcg) in evaluate.evaluate(engine, queries).items():
        print(f"{mode:18} {mrr:7.3f} {ndcg:8.3f}")


def cmd_serve(args):
    import uvicorn

    print(f"Seekr on http://{config.HOST}:{config.PORT}  (search page: /, API docs: /docs)")
    uvicorn.run(web.create_site(), host=config.HOST, port=config.PORT)


def cmd_keys(args):
    key = api.create_key(db.connect(), args.name, admin=args.admin)
    print(f"New {'admin ' if args.admin else ''}API key for {args.name!r} (shown once, store it safely):\n{key}")


def cmd_doctor(args):
    sys.exit(0 if doctor.run_checks() else 1)


def cmd_stats(args):
    seeds = config.read_seeds()
    db_state = "exists" if config.DB_PATH.exists() else "not created yet"
    print(f"seeds:    {len(seeds)}")
    print(f"database: {config.DB_PATH} ({db_state})")
    if config.DB_PATH.exists():
        conn = db.connect()
        print_host_stats(conn)
        for table in ("pages", "documents", "chunks", "embeddings"):
            print(f"{table + ':':10} {conn.execute(f'SELECT COUNT(*) FROM {table}').fetchone()[0]}")


COMMANDS = {
    "crawl": cmd_crawl,
    "index": cmd_index,
    "rank": cmd_rank,
    "discover": cmd_discover,
    "live": cmd_live,
    "serve": cmd_serve,
    "stats": cmd_stats,
    "search": cmd_search,
    "eval": cmd_eval,
    "keys": cmd_keys,
    "doctor": cmd_doctor,
}


def main(argv=None):
    logging.basicConfig(level=config.LOG_LEVEL, format="%(asctime)s %(levelname)-7s %(name)s: %(message)s",
                        datefmt="%H:%M:%S")
    for noisy in ("sentence_transformers", "httpx", "httpx2"):  # progress bars and one line per HTTP request
        logging.getLogger(noisy).setLevel(logging.WARNING)
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog="seekr", description="Seekr search engine")
    sub = parser.add_subparsers(dest="command", required=True)
    for name in COMMANDS:
        command = sub.add_parser(name)
        if name == "search":
            command.add_argument("query", nargs="+")
        if name == "keys":
            command.add_argument("action", choices=["create"])
            command.add_argument("name", help="who or what the key is for")
            command.add_argument("--admin", action="store_true", help="may add seeds")
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
    COMMANDS[args.command](args)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
    On a slow disk, one writer can hold the database for longer than the 60-second timeout from Chapter 11. SQLite then raises sqlite3.OperationalError: database is locked, and before this change that one error ended live mode for good. Ours stopped overnight like that. Now crawl_forever and cmd_live catch that error, log a warning, and try again next round. They catch only OperationalError, so real bugs still stop the program, where you'll notice them. crawl_forever also checks MAX(rowid) now, not COUNT(*), to see whether a round found new pages. Counting means reading the whole pages table, all its HTML: about an hour for 100 GB on a USB hard drive. The highest row number comes straight from the table's index, and it still goes up whenever a page is saved.
  4. Test that searches don't reload too often (whole updated tests/test_live.py)
    tests/test_live.py
    import sqlite3
import time

from seekr import __main__ as cli
from seekr import config, db, embed, extract, rank, search


def add_page(conn, doc_id, text):
    conn.execute("INSERT INTO documents (id, url, title, language, word_count, published) VALUES (?, ?, ?, 'en', ?, '')",
                 (doc_id, f"https://x.com/{doc_id}", text, len(text.split())))
    conn.execute("INSERT INTO chunks (id, doc_id, position, text) VALUES (?, ?, 0, ?)", (doc_id, doc_id, text))


def test_database_can_be_read_while_another_program_writes(tmp_path):
    writer, reader = db.connect(tmp_path / "t.db"), db.connect(tmp_path / "t.db")
    assert writer.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode").fetchone()[0] == "wal"
    db.enqueue(writer, ["https://a.com/"], depth=0)  # an open, uncommitted write
    assert reader.execute("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM frontier").fetchone()[0] == 0  # readers aren't blocked


def test_search_engine_picks_up_new_embeddings(tmp_path):
    conn = db.connect(tmp_path / "t.db")
    conn.execute(rank.SCHEMA)
    add_page(conn, 1, "Rust ownership rules")
    embed.embed_chunks(conn)
    engine = search.SearchEngine(conn)
    assert engine.vectors.chunk_ids == [1]
    add_page(conn, 2, "Python asyncio event loop")
    embed.embed_chunks(conn)
    engine.refresh()
    assert engine.vectors.chunk_ids == [1, 2]


def test_live_indexes_while_the_crawl_runs(monkeypatch):
    calls = []
    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "crawl_forever", lambda: time.sleep(0.5))  # a fake crawl that ends quickly
    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "cmd_index", lambda args: calls.append("index"))
    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "cmd_rank", lambda args: calls.append("rank"))
    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "cmd_discover", lambda args: calls.append("discover"))
    monkeypatch.setattr(config, "LIVE_INTERVAL", 0.1)
    cli.main(["live"])
    assert calls[:3] == ["index", "rank", "discover"]  # right away, then again every LIVE_INTERVAL
    assert len(calls) >= 9


def test_one_broken_page_does_not_stop_indexing(monkeypatch):
    def broken(html, url, page_title=""):
        raise ValueError("Invalid \\escape")

    monkeypatch.setattr(extract, "extract", broken)
    assert extract.extract_row(("https://x.com/", "<html>", "https://x.com/", "")) is None


def test_search_reloads_at_most_every_refresh_seconds(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
    conn = db.connect(tmp_path / "t.db")
    conn.execute(rank.SCHEMA)
    add_page(conn, 1, "Rust ownership rules")
    embed.embed_chunks(conn)
    engine = search.SearchEngine(conn)
    add_page(conn, 2, "Python asyncio event loop")
    embed.embed_chunks(conn)
    engine.search("python", mode="vector")
    assert engine.vectors.chunk_ids == [1]  # too soon: keep the loaded vectors, so the search stays fast
    monkeypatch.setattr(config, "REFRESH_SECONDS", 0)
    engine.search("python", mode="vector")
    assert engine.vectors.chunk_ids == [1, 2]


def test_live_keeps_going_when_the_database_is_locked(monkeypatch):
    calls = []

    def locked(args):
        calls.append("index")
        raise sqlite3.OperationalError("database is locked")

    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "crawl_forever", lambda: time.sleep(0.5))
    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "cmd_index", locked)
    monkeypatch.setattr(config, "LIVE_INTERVAL", 0.1)
    cli.main(["live"])
    assert len(calls) >= 3  # it tried again every round instead of crashing
    There are two new tests at the end. test_search_reloads_at_most_every_refresh_seconds adds a page after the engine has loaded. A search straight away still uses the old vectors. After REFRESH_SECONDS is set to 0, the next search picks up the new page. mode="vector" keeps the test small: it needs no keyword index. test_live_keeps_going_when_the_database_is_locked makes every index round fail the way a slow disk does, and checks that live mode keeps trying.
  5. Run all the tests
    python -m pytest -q
    78 passed
  6. Optional: move Seekr to a bigger machine. First stop live mode, then fold the write-ahead log into the database file
    sqlite3 data/seekr.db "PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE);"
    In WAL mode (Chapter 11), recent writes sit in seekr.db-wal until they are checkpointed. TRUNCATE copies them into seekr.db and empties the log, so that one file holds everything.
  7. Copy the code and the database to the other machine
    rsync -rlt --exclude .venv --exclude data ./ you@bigbox:~/Search_engine/
rsync -rt --partial data/seekr.db you@bigbox:/mnt/bigdisk/seekr/
    rsync copies over SSH, and --partial lets an interrupted copy resume. The code goes to the machine's own disk. The virtualenv is rebuilt there, because it can't be copied between Mac and Linux. If the big disk is exFAT, keep only the data on it: exFAT can't store the symlinks a virtualenv needs. Compare shasum data/seekr.db with sha1sum on the other side before deleting your copy.
  8. Install and test on the new machine
    cd ~/Search_engine && python3 -m venv .venv && .venv/bin/pip install -r requirements.txt
.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q
  9. Run live mode and the website there, pointing at the big disk
    export SEEKR_DB=/mnt/bigdisk/seekr/seekr.db HF_HOME=/mnt/bigdisk/seekr/huggingface
SEEKR_CONTACT="Your Name; you@example.com" SEEKR_MAX_PAGES_PER_DOMAIN=100000 SEEKR_MAX_DEPTH=5 nohup .venv/bin/python -m seekr live > /mnt/bigdisk/seekr/live.log 2>&1 &
SEEKR_HOST=100.x.y.z nohup .venv/bin/python -m seekr serve > /mnt/bigdisk/seekr/serve.log 2>&1 &
    SEEKR_DB moves the database, and HF_HOME moves the downloaded models. nohup ... & keeps both running after you log out. A bigger SEEKR_MAX_PAGES_PER_DOMAIN lets big sites like Wikipedia go past 5,000 pages. Set SEEKR_HOST to the machine's private address, for example its Tailscale IP, so only your own devices can reach the website. 0.0.0.0 would open it to the whole local network.

Checkpoint

Open http://100.x.y.z:8000 from your laptop. A search takes well under a second again, except one search in every 5 minutes that reloads the vectors.

The live log ends with "database is locked"
You're running the code from before this chapter. Make sure __main__.py catches sqlite3.OperationalError. A database on a fast internal disk avoids the long waits.
New pages never show up
Wait REFRESH_SECONDS, then search again. New pages also need a finished index pass in live mode.
rsync: unrecognized option --info
macOS ships a different rsync. Use --stats instead.
The site won't load from another device
SEEKR_HOST is still 127.0.0.1, which only accepts connections from the same machine.

Summary · What you built complete

A real search engine, from crawler to website, running on your own laptop, and you know how every line works.

What works

  • Crawling: a polite, resumable crawler. It obeys robots.txt, sends at most one request per second per site, retries with backoff, follows redirects and iframes, and stays inside 15 hand-picked documentation sites and blogs. Result: 4,086 pages.
  • Indexing: clean main text (no menus or footers), 28,841 overlapping ~300-word chunks, exact-duplicate removal, a SQLite FTS5 keyword index, GPU embeddings (4 min 14 s on an M4 Pro), and PageRank over 165,530 links.
  • Ranking: BM25 and vector candidates fused with RRF, then a cross-encoder relevance score (0–1, calibrated with a sigmoid) as the main signal, plus a little PageRank and exact-match bonus. About 0.1 s per search on the GPU.
  • Access: a REST API with hashed API keys, per-key rate limits, CORS and interactive docs, and a clean, mobile-friendly search page with dark mode.
  • Operations: logging, doctor, Docker, a README, 67 tests, and two guide checkers. One compares every final listing with the real files. The other rebuilds the project from this guide alone, chapter by chapter, and runs each stage's tests.

How good is it?

System (22 labelled queries)MRR@10nDCG@10
Keyword only (BM25)0.6240.630
Semantic only (embeddings)0.6780.712
Hybrid (RRF)0.6520.694
Hybrid + relevance reranker0.7740.782

The relevance model lifts MRR by 24% over keyword search and 14% over semantic search. Remember the caveat from Chapter 7: 22 queries, tuned on themselves. A held-out set of new queries is the first thing to add.

What's next

  • AI answers with citations (RAG): send the top results' chunks to a language model, which writes a short answer and cites each claim as [1], [2] linking to the result. Seekr already has everything retrieval needs: chunks, relevance scores, snippets and an API. The new part is generation, and checking that every sentence is backed by a cited source.
  • More pages: import whole sites, or a filtered slice of Common Crawl, through its URL index instead of crawling everything yourself. The rest of the pipeline stays the same.
  • Better measurement: a held-out evaluation set, and more queries with several judged answers each.

Appendix · The guide checker

This guide promises that its code matches the real project exactly. This small script keeps that promise: it reads every full-file listing in guide.html and compares it with the file on disk. You don't need it to build Seekr, but it's a nice example of html.parser from the standard library.

  1. Create the checker
    tools/check_guide.py
    """Check that every full-file code block in guide.html matches the real file.

A block written as <pre data-file="seekr/config.py"> is a full-file listing.
If a file is shown more than once, the LAST listing must match the file on disk.
"""
import sys
from html.parser import HTMLParser
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent


class FileBlocks(HTMLParser):
    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__(convert_charrefs=True)
        self.blocks = {}
        self.path = None
        self.parts = []

    def handle_starttag(self, tag, attrs):
        attrs = dict(attrs)
        if tag == "pre" and "data-file" in attrs:
            self.path, self.parts = attrs["data-file"], []

    def handle_endtag(self, tag):
        if tag == "pre" and self.path:
            self.blocks[self.path] = "".join(self.parts)
            self.path = None

    def handle_data(self, data):
        if self.path:
            self.parts.append(data)


def main():
    parser = FileBlocks()
    parser.feed((ROOT / "guide.html").read_text())
    problems = []
    for path, shown in parser.blocks.items():
        real = ROOT / path
        if not real.exists():
            problems.append(f"{path}: shown in guide but missing on disk")
        elif real.read_text().strip("\n") != shown.strip("\n"):
            problems.append(f"{path}: guide listing differs from the file on disk")
    for problem in problems:
        print("MISMATCH", problem)
    print(f"checked {len(parser.blocks)} files, {len(problems)} problems")
    sys.exit(1 if problems else 0)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
    HTMLParser calls handle_starttag, handle_data and handle_endtag as it reads the page. We collect the text inside every <pre data-file="..."> block, and convert_charrefs=True turns &lt; back into <.
  2. Run it
    python tools/check_guide.py
    checked 40 files, 0 problems
  3. Create the chapter-by-chapter replay
    tools/replay_guide.py
    """Rebuild the project from guide.html alone, one chapter at a time, and run each stage's tests.

check_guide.py only compares the LAST listing of each file. This catches the other mistake: an earlier
chapter showing a version of a file that doesn't work at that point in the guide.
Usage: python tools/replay_guide.py   (builds into a temporary folder; your project is not touched)
"""
import re
import subprocess
import sys
import tempfile
from html.parser import HTMLParser
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent


class ChapterListings(HTMLParser):
    """Collects (chapter number, path, code) for every <pre data-file="..."> block, in page order."""

    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__(convert_charrefs=True)
        self.listings, self.chapter, self.path, self.parts = [], None, None, []

    def handle_starttag(self, tag, attrs):
        attrs = dict(attrs)
        if tag == "h2":  # chapter headings are <h2 id="ch3">; the map and the appendix don't count
            self.chapter = int(attrs["id"][2:]) if re.fullmatch(r"ch\d+", attrs.get("id", "")) else None
        if tag == "pre" and "data-file" in attrs:
            self.path, self.parts = attrs["data-file"], []

    def handle_endtag(self, tag):
        if tag == "pre" and self.path:
            self.listings.append((self.chapter, self.path, "".join(self.parts)))
            self.path = None

    def handle_data(self, data):
        if self.path:
            self.parts.append(data)


def main():
    parser = ChapterListings()
    parser.feed((ROOT / "guide.html").read_text())
    chapters = sorted({chapter for chapter, _, _ in parser.listings if chapter})
    failed = []
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as work:
        for chapter in chapters:
            folder = Path(work) / f"ch{chapter}"
            for listed_in, path, code in parser.listings:
                if listed_in and listed_in <= chapter:  # later listings of a file replace earlier ones
                    (folder / path).parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
                    (folder / path).write_text(code.strip("\n") + "\n")
            result = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-m", "pytest", "-q", "-p", "no:cacheprovider"],
                                    cwd=folder, capture_output=True, text=True)
            summary = (result.stdout.strip().splitlines() or ["no output"])[-1]
            print(f"chapter {chapter}: {'ok    ' if result.returncode == 0 else 'FAILED'} {summary}")
            if result.returncode != 0:
                failed.append(chapter)
    sys.exit(1 if failed else 0)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
    check_guide.py only compares the last listing of each file. While building Chapter 9 we found that earlier chapters were showing today's version of files like __main__.py, which imports modules that don't exist yet at Chapter 2. This script does what a reader does: for each chapter it writes every file exactly as the guide shows it up to that point, into a throw-away folder (tempfile.TemporaryDirectory deletes it afterwards), and runs that chapter's tests with subprocess.run. sys.executable is the Python running this script, so the tests use the same virtual environment.
  4. Run it (about a minute)
    python tools/replay_guide.py
    chapter 1: ok     4 passed in 0.01s
chapter 2: ok     16 passed in 1.15s
chapter 3: ok     21 passed in 1.51s
chapter 4: ok     26 passed in 1.50s
chapter 5: ok     31 passed in 6.89s
chapter 6: ok     36 passed in 5.65s
chapter 7: ok     48 passed in 6.12s
chapter 8: ok     57 passed in 6.42s
chapter 9: ok     61 passed in 6.91s
chapter 10: ok     67 passed in 6.78s
